#2266. When I overtook Jim Leighton, that was when I started to wind Kenny up about beating his cap tally... ANDY ROBERTSON on going level with Dalglish

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:28 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 18:04 EST, 12 November 2026

It's a story which extols the virtues of loyalty and leadership in the proper sense.

More than anything, though, Andy Robertson’s achievements in football are – and always will be – reminders of what’s possible when a talented individual takes rejection as their motivation to redefine themselves in the eyes of the world.

Even though it’s been told many times before, the tale of the full-back’s determination to bounce back from being shown the door as a teenager by Celtic will never grow old.

Without having the character to overcome such adversity, he would not have been caught in the safety net at Queen’s Park. There would have been no moves to Dundee United and Hull. No Champions League and title-winning medals from his time with Liverpool. No first cap for his country.

In terms of a life lesson for those aspiring footballers who may find themselves in a cul-de-sac in their own careers, it has few equals.

All being well, Robertson will make his 102nd appearance for Scotland against North Macedonia, equalling the record tally of his idol and great friend, Sir Kenny Dalglish.

Andy Robertson will go level with Kenny Dalglish as Scotland's record cap holder on Friday

Both men are proud Glaswegians and Scots who bonded throughout their time together at Anfield. Dalglish would be the first to admit, though, that their respective paths have been distinct.

With a God-given talent so bright it could blind you, Dalglish was a superstar in the making before he wore a Scotland jersey for the first time against Belgium in 1971.

Coming in a friendly in Poland in 2014, Robertson’s first appearance only arrived after those early struggles.

It took huge character for him to even get that far. To add a century more across a dozen years under four different managers is a gargantuan achievement.

Football has a funny habit of throwing up strange co-incidences. Yesterday happened to be 40 years to the day since Dalglish won the last of his caps in a 3-0 win over Luxembourg.

Characteristically, before he took his own walk down memory lane, the man who’ll join him on that mark this evening was at pains to give the Anfield icon his rightful place.

‘It’s not really about me,’ Robertson insisted. ‘For how long the record stood, for starters, for what he (Dalglish) achieved in his career, for the person he is... tomorrow is another reason for our country to honour one of the greatest players that has played a game. He’s definitely the greatest in Scotland.

Dalglish scored 30 goals in his 102 caps and is regarded as Scotland's greatest ever player

‘Obviously, in terms of the number, I will stand side by side with him. But I don’t want people to forget how important this man has been to our country.

‘Because, for me, he was arguably the best player in the world at one time and will always be the greatest in this country, no matter what happens in the future.

‘He’ll always be the first one to do X, Y and Z. I just think that shouldn’t be forgotten.

‘Tomorrow is definitely a reason to do that for Kenny.’

There is, though, still no understating what Robertson has achieved across 12 years. Playing for Scotland wasn’t always the joy that it’s been in more recent times.

‘We get looked after so well off the pitch,’ he offered. ‘That probably wasn’t always the case when I first came in.

‘Under Gordon (Strachan) in my first qualifying campaign, I think I was still young enough to not think too much about it. But then I also remember under Alex McLeish and then the start of Steve Clarke, there was a real disconnect between the country and the players.

‘There wasn’t too much love for the players. We were getting 10,000, 15,000 inside Hampden. That was the most difficult time, especially as captain and being at Liverpool as well.’

He stuck at it and never shirked responsibility. In time he was able to chalk up the numbers because the side steadily improved and broke through the glass ceiling.

Robertson shares a laugh with Nathan Patterson ahead of the visit of North Macedonia

‘Of course, I would be sitting here with a few less caps if we hadn’t been successful,’ he reflected. ‘The fact that we got to two Euros, a World Cup and all the warm-up games and things like that resulted in me having an extra maybe 15 caps.

‘What makes it more special to me is the fact that we’ve managed to achieve things.’

All of this was beyond his wildest dreams at the beginning. A late substitute for Barry Bannan in a friendly in Warsaw on March 5, 2014, he can still hear Gordon Strachan whispering in his ear.

‘He basically said, “Don’t be scared, don’t go into your shell”,’ he recalled. ‘He said, “The reason I’ve picked you is because you’re brave. I see you bombing up and down the pitch for Dundee United”. He said, “Go and be that player”.

‘I always remember the first ball came to me and the words were ringing in my ears. I think I carried it 30 yards forward. It gave me the confidence to just go and try and play exactly the way I was playing under Jackie McNamara.’

Four years later, by then a Liverpool player, Robertson was handed the armband by McLeish following Scott Brown’s retirement.

‘That made me grow up and made me realise what it took to become a leader,’ he reflected. ‘I kind of grew into it. I believe that I became the captain that I wanted to be.’

It was only when he equalled McLeish’s 77 appearances that the prospect of one day equalling or surpassing Dalglish entered his head.

‘I think everyone in the room and everyone who personally knows me well enough knows that personal achievements aren’t really what I focus on,’ he said. ‘But I would lie if I said this wasn’t one that I’ve chased.

Like Dalglish, Robertson is a Liverpool icon having made 378 appearances for the Anfield club

‘I think when I overtook Darren Fletcher (80 caps), when I was in the 80s, it felt like ages for me to get to Jim Leighton (91). Then I thought I’ve only got one more person to go.

‘That’s when I started thinking about it a little bit more, starting to believe that it might happen, started to wind Kenny up a bit more that it was going to happen.

‘I think that was maybe when I realised that this was maybe possible if I got lucky with injuries, if I kept playing for Liverpool.

‘Sat here today, if it happens, it’s something I couldn’t be more proud of. My international career has been everything for me.’

To wear the armband at one international tournament would have been a laudable achievement in itself. To do so three times would have seemed an impossible feat in those difficult early days.

The hunger to repeat the trick at Euro 2028 is as strong as ever.

‘I’ve always kept pushing,’ he said. ‘We’re always trying something new. My wife asked me a similar question last week when we were talking about it. I was going to try and catch Gemma Fay (203 caps), but I think that might be a little bit too far from me...’

#2263. Mauricio Pochettino to sign two-and-a-half year deal to replace Michael Carrick at Man United - as it emerges he WILL take charge of USA game in early hours of Wednesday, just days before crunch Liverpool clash

Published: 10:58 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 18:28 EST, 12 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino will face a brutal start to life at Manchester United with the prospective Red Devils boss set to manage the United States days before his debut at Liverpool.

United have moved closer to putting Pochettino in charge at Old Trafford after reaching a verbal agreement with the US national team boss on Wednesday. The club then made a formal approach to US Soccer, and he is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year deal.

Daily Mail Sport understands Pochettino will take charge of both of the United States matches during the international window.

The US take on Haiti in the CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final first leg on Saturday, with the second fixture then taking place in Cincinnati on Tuesday evening, but would likely finish at around 2am GMT.

Pochettino would then have to travel to Manchester following the match to link up with the Red Devils' squad for the first time, likely on Thursday.

The Argentine would then have limited time with his players before a daunting trip to Anfield to face rivals Liverpool on Sunday in a blockbuster first match in charge.

Mauricio Pochettino will take charge of the United States upcoming matches against Haiti

Pochettino will coach in the early hours on Wednesday morning in Cincinnati before travelling to Manchester

The Argentine would have limited time to prepare his side for Sunday's match at Anfield

Man United are said to be relaxed over Pochettino taking charge of his final two matches as US boss before starting as their new head coach.

It will cost Pochettino around £7million to buy himself out of his contract with the US Soccer Federation, with United ready to foot the bill.

The cost of bringing in his back-room team, including trusted assistants Jesus Perez and Miguel D’Agostino, will take the cost above £10million.

The 54-year-old only signed a new deal after the World Cup meaning that United could have got him for nothing in the summer.

Pochettino is set to take over a Man United side languishing in the Premier League relegation zone following just one win in their opening ten matches.

The trip to Anfield would be the first of a run of eight matches in 29 days, as Man United juggle Premier League and Champions League football.

Pochettino is set to succeed Michael Carrick, whose departure as the Red Devils' boss was officially announced on Wednesday morning.

Pochettino will face a tight turnaround to prepare his new side for the daunting clash on Merseyside

Carrick arrived at United's training ground at 7.50am this morning and was dismissed. He left at 10.18am, less than two and a half hours later. He is the first Premier League manager to be sacked this season, followed hours later by bottom-of-the-table Crystal Palace boss Pierre Sage.

In a statement released at 10.37am, Carrick's sacking was confirmed. United's statement read: 'After a disappointing start to the season, the club has come to the extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary in order to achieve our ambitions.

'Michael is, and always will be, a Manchester United legend. Just as he did as a player, he has conducted himself with the highest standards of dedication and professionalism throughout his time back with the club. His leadership and achievements last season will always be fondly remembered.'

Carrick's backroom team have also left the club with Pochettino set to bring his own staff to United.

Assistant boss Steve Holland, coaches Jonathan Woodgate, Jonny Evans and Travis Binnion, and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson have all gone, although United didn't initially confirm the news because not all of them were at the training ground this morning and had to be told in person.

#2261. Approvato bilancio del Fondo di fine carriera calciatori: avanzo di 1,7 milioni

(ANSA) - ROMA, 18 NOV - Un attivo complessivo di 207,2 milioni, un avanzo di esercizio di 1.7 milioni e oltre 20,5 milioni liquidati agli iscritti nella stagione sportiva 2025/26. Sono questi i principali risultati del bilancio del Fondo di accantonamento delle indennità di fine carriera per i giocatori e allenatori di calcio, approvato oggi. Dal 1975 a oggi, informa una nota, il Fondo ha distribuito in media più dell'84% degli avanzi generati e liquidato oltre 30.000 posizioni, riconoscendo le somme dovute ai professionisti che avevano regolarmente versato i contributi previsti. Il presidente del Fondo, Leonardo Grosso, ribadisce l'impegno dell'ente nella tutela delle somme accantonate: "I risultati del 2025/26 confermano la stabilità della gestione e, soprattutto, la capacità di assicurare con continuità le prestazioni previste - afferma -.

Dietro ogni voce di bilancio c'è il futuro di un professionista. Per questo amministriamo le risorse con prudenza, responsabilità e una costante attenzione alla loro protezione. Gli oltre 20,5 milioni di euro corrisposti nella stagione 2025/26 rappresentano la dimostrazione concreta della nostra capacità di rispettare gli impegni assunti". (ANSA).

#2260. 'It's a LIE': Bullish Morgan Rogers hits back at claims Cole Palmer isn't committed to England as Three Lions star appears to suggest Thomas Tuchel is 'wrong' for questioning Chelsea team-mate

Published: 18:35 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 18:41 EST, 12 November 2026

Morgan Rogers has rubbished questions around team-mate and friend Cole Palmer's commitment to England after the Chelsea star was left out of the latest squad.

After missing out on selection for the World Cup, Palmer was called up to the group for the first international break of the season as he continued to monitor his fitness, but dropped out of the squad.

It meant he was again ignored when it came to selection for this fortnight's Nations League games against Croatia and Spain, with manager Thomas Tuchel saying Palmer 'misses too many opportunities to impress' him.

The events have led to criticism of Palmer, who scored for England in the final of Euro 2024, but Rogers has moved to defend his friend.

'It's a lie,' Rogers, who was speaking after starting and scoring for England in their 2-1 win over Croatia on Thursday, said on BBC 5 Live. 'He wants to be here. He wanted to get called up and he wanted to come and represent his country as he always has done. People just see it as news and say the easy thing.

'We all know he had a difficult year last year with injuries and I know personally he's been managing them injuries ever since. He was in quite a low place with his injuries last year and didn't feel 100 per cent himself on the pitch, so he wants to be here and he wants to be the best version of himself. Of course he does, who wouldn't?

Morgan Rogers (right) has rubbished questions around Cole Palmer's (left) commitment to the England team

Three Lions boss Thomas Tuchel (right) said Palmer 'misses too many opportunities to impress' him

'But he also has to look at his body. He wants to play for 10 to 15 years, (so) he also has to manage himself. If he has to manage himself in the short term for the greater good then he has to do that and you have to fully support it.

'It has nothing to do with England or not wanting to play with England. It's a nonsense really that people are questioning someone that wants to play for England. You see the joy and smile on his face, how much he loves the game, so I honestly don't get it and he doesn't get it. But it's something he has to deal with but I can assure you he wants to be here and he wants to play for his country.'

Tuchel has accepted that Palmer is 'no exception' to being 'desperate' to wanting to play for England.

But the German claimed he has largely opted to stick with much of the group he ran with in the last international break, despite calling up Jobe Bellingham, James Hill, and Josh King – who plays in Palmer's position – for the first time.

'It's not personal,' the Three Lions boss said on the 24-year-old's omission. 'And of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country, but the competition is on and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.'

Rogers, though, is desperate to see Palmer back in an England shirt.

'What a player he is,' he added. 'What a person he is and it's someone you want to see in an England shirt.

'He's got so much quality, so much ability. We all know what we can do, what he's done, so of course we want to see him in an England shirt and hopefully sooner rather than later.'

#2258. Conte: "Juventus? Un cerchio che prima o poi bisognerà chiudere. Prima di andare all'Inter..."

Conte: "Juventus? Un cerchio che prima o poi bisognerà chiudere. Prima di andare all'Inter..."

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Antonio Conte, ex ct della Nazionale con un passato alla guida anche dell'Inter, nel corso di un'intervista concessa a Cronache di Spogliatoio ha parlato della possibilità di tornare sulla panchina della Juventus, occupata per tre stagioni consecutive dal 2011 al 2014: "Tornare alla Juventus? Penso che prima o poi quel cerchio si dovrà chiudere. Io so di aver lasciato qualcosa, e so che quel cerchio lo dovremo chiudere. Non so se tra uno, dieci, quindici anni ma so di aver lasciato qualcosa alla Juve. Ci sono stati in passato dei momenti in cui ci saremmo potuti riunire, ed è molto difficile perchè devono capitare due situazioni: io devo essere libero, e la Juventus deve cercare un allenatore senza panchina. Ci sono state due situazioni: quando la Juventus decise di prendere Sarri io ero libero, e infatti dopo sono andato all'Inter. La seconda volta, quando ero libero, arriva Giuntoli, che però decide di prendere Thiago Motta, io sono andato a Napoli".

"Già nel 2009, dopo Bari, potevo doventare l'allenatore della Juventus, ci avevo parlato più volte, c'era Ranieri in panchina e poi presero Ferrara le ultime tre partite per andare in Champions League. Loro sapevano benissimo che giocavo con il 4-2-4: di attaccanti eravamo pieni, avrei avuto bisogno di esterni. Loro, non so per quale motivo, quell'anno presero Diego dal Werder Brema: mi chiamarono e mi dissero che Diego voleva giocare con il 4-3-1-2. Qualsiasi altro avrebbe detto di sì, sarei passato dal Bari alla Juventus, ma io non scendo a compromessi. Così andai dalla società e dissi: "Se un calciatore deve decidere come dobbiamo giocare, non sono l'allenatore giusto per voi". Sono uscito da quell'incontro consapevole che non sarei diventato l'allenatore della Juventus".

"Tante volte mi chiedono: "Quando torni?" Torno quando c'è l'opportunità, quando si incroceranno alcune situazioni. So che non sarà semplice, ma prima o poi il cerchio si dovrà chiudere".

#2257. Il Brentford piomba su Isaksen per gennaio: la Lazio prepara il rinnovo

È corsa contro il tempo in casa Lazio per il rinnovo di Gustav Isaksen. Dopo Floriani Mussolini, il club biancoceleste ha intenzione di blindare i giocatori con il contratto in scadenza nel 2028, e tra questi c'è proprio l'esterno danese, finito nel mirino del Brentford per il mercato di gennaio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Secondo quanto raccolto dal Corriere dello Sport, Isaksen avrebbe attirato l'attenzione della Premier League dopo un avvio di stagione positivo e convincente. L'ironia della sorte sta nel fatto che il giocatore era stato offerto al Brentford nel mercato dello scorso anno, quando il club inglese aveva invece preferito Cancellieri. Ora le carte in tavola sono cambiate: la società potrebbe provare a strapparlo alla Lazio mettendo sul tavolo un’offerta da 30 milioni. Dal canto suo, la Lazio vuole proseguire i discorsi sul prolungamento del contratto fino al 2031 e il conseguente adeguamento dello stipendio dagli 1,9 attuali a 3 milioni.

Al momento l'operazione resta in stand-by, in attesa dell'accordo sulla cifra. La Lazio dovrà comunque accelerare i tempi: il Brentford resta alla finestra, ma non è escluso che possano farsi avanti altri pretendenti dalla Premier.

#2254. England 2-1 Croatia PLAYER RATINGS: Whose position is under 'serious' threat? Which two regulars need to show more? And who was at the heart of Three Lions' best attacking play?

Published: 17:06 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 20:16 EST, 12 November 2026

Harry Kane scored on his record 126th appearance for England as they beat Croatia 2-1 at Wembley in the Nations League.

The Bayern Munich striker, 33, scored his 92nd goal for the Three Lions from the penalty spot to give them the victory.

Morgan Rogers had opened the scoring in the first half before Petar Sucic equalised.

Thomas Tuchel's side go to Spain on Sunday, where they will be in a shoot-out to qualify for the League A finals.

CRAIG HOPE was at Wembley to bring you our player ratings.

Harry Kane scored on his landmark 126th England outing to give them a 2-1 win over Croatia

Was left exposed for goal and not much he could do. Made four fairly routine saves and kicking was fine. Tuchel says he’s the No.1 but that will only become apparent should he start in Spain on Sunday. It feels like his position is under serious threat from James Trafford, not that he did anything wrong here.

A very bright start, including one delightful drop in behind for Rogers, but he faded thereafter. Not the creative force he should be in a game like this and questions remain over both Alexander-Arnold’s current level of performance and whether anyone will emerge as England’s first-choice right back.

He was enjoying a trouble-free night until mistake leading to Croatia equaliser with baffling decision to head back to Pickford amid crowded goalmouth. He did, in part, make amends by winning penalty for Kane goal, as well as timely late slide to block shot. Doesn’t convince as a starter, though.

Like Quansah, all was going well until Croatia goal, and he’ll be disappointed not to get the first header on the corner before ball dropped. He looked shaky after that and was nearly outdone for a Croatia equaliser late on. Could have done with a breather after 70 minutes or so given recent return from injury.

Trent Alexander-Arnold was not the creative force he should have been in a game like this

In a position that is up for grabs he did his chances no harm at all and was the pick of the backline by far. Got forward at every opportunity and rarely wasted a ball. Was also there at the other end with several key interceptions. A solid night.

A lot of huff and puff but, as too often in an England shirt, not enough to show for it by way of creation or penetration. With some bright young midfielders like James Garner and Alex Scott emerging, Rice has to do more, you feel, to make sure he stays in the XI. This was an underwhelming night in that regard.

Lots of involvement and omnipresent across the park, as ever. One superb dribble that drew applause, but we need to see more of that inspiration higher up the pitch. A good display, but no chances created. Partnership with Rice still feels a little forced at times.

Morgan Rogers was his team's brightest player when he forced the opening goal

Was already his team’s brightest player when he forced opening goal with close-range finish. Clearly a man in form and brought that brightness to this stage. With doubts over Saka’s durability, he looks the best bet as the starting right winger. Was at the heart of all good about England in final third.

Provided assist with confident cushion for pal Rogers and he was a threat throughout, even if final shot or pass didn’t quite come off, the assist apart. Felt like he was in second gear a little, especially knowing booking would have ruled him out of Spain on Sunday in his home city of Madrid.

Didn’t happen for Barcelona winger and he’ll know he should have done better when twice cutting in on right foot and having a good sight at goal. He missed the target on both occasions. Gordon can’t get cosy knowing he’s Tuchel’s first choice on the left, and more is needed.

Anthony Gordon will know he should have done better - he can't get cosy on the left flank

The quarterback! Was this a glimpse of the future? Kane operated even deeper than he usually does at times and one long-range pass from 30 yards inside his own half drew gasps from the Wembley crowd. It was his playmaker’s dink in behind that led to opener. Got his goal with penalty dink on his big night.

Ben White (on for Alexander-Arnold 78) - 6

A win, but it was not comfortable and Tuchel leaves this with yet more questions about how he builds a defence for the European Championship. A sold-out Wembley weren’t exactly thrilled by England’s attack, either. Job done, but it was dull.

Croatia (4-2-3-1): Livakovic 5; Smolcic 6 (Marco Pasalic 76 5), Stanisic 6, Vuskovic 6, Gvardiol 5; Misic 5 (Modric 76, 6), Kovacic 6; Sucic 7 (Mario Pasalic 70, 5), Baturina 6, Vlasic 5; Matanovic 5 (Budimir 70, 5)

#2253. Dall'oratorio alla Nazionale: quando Esposito era troppo piccolo anche per i tornei Figc

Dall'oratorio alla Nazionale: quando Esposito era troppo piccolo anche per i tornei Figc

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

10 gol in Nazionale a soli 21 anni, un ruolo di primo piano anche all'Inter e un futuro che appare già scritto: Pio Esposito è ormai da tempo una delle più grandi speranze del calcio italiano. Speranze che stanno diventando certezze. Una storia partita da Castellammare di Stabia e che ha vissuto una parentesi importante e fondamentale a Brescia, dove Pio iniziò a mostrare il suo talento sulla scia dei fratelli Salvatore e Sebastiano.

Repubblica racconta gli inizi, dal campo di un piccoloo oratorio: "Una volta arrivati a Brescia, i due fratelli Esposito più grandi furonotesserati nelle giovanili. Per Pio, la questione era più complicata. «A sette anni non poteva partecipare ai tornei Figc. E come lui nemmeno Cher Ndour, che aveva un anno in più. Ma erano troppo forti, dovevamo farli giocare. Ci venne l'idea del torneo degli oratori del campionato Anspi», ricorda Roberto Beccalossi, solo omonimo di Evaristo, un altro degli scout che lavorò al trasferimento degli Esposito a Brescia. Si decise infatti di creare una squadra di bambini e iscriverla a un torneo parrocchiale, per una stagione soltanto. [...] «Capitava che giocassero contro ragazzi di due anni più grandi, eppure vincevano tanto a poco. Pio segnava una valanga di gol», dice Beccalossi.

Pio era una mascotte al centro sportivo San Filippo, in città, dove si allenavano le giovanili del Brescia. «Finiti gli allenamenti, non lo mandavi più a casa. Fino a quando papà Agostino non lo veniva a prendere, correva dietro al pallone», ricorda Beccalossi. Proprio su quei campi Pio è cresciuto come calciatore, nelle strutture della Voluntas, società dilettantistica e storico serbatoio di talenti del Brescia. Negli anni hanno vestito quella maglia Corini e i gemelli Filippini, Baronio, Diana e Bonazzoli. Nel 1988 Andrea Pirlo è arrivato dal Flero, e si sono formati qui anche Bonera, Agliardi e Acerbi. Fondatore e anima della Voluntas è stato per 37 anni il mitico Roberto Clerici, scomparso nel 2018, quando Pio giocava ormai da anni nelle giovanili dell'Inter. Lì ha incontrato Cristian Chivu".

#2252. Simonelli sulla Serie A: "Abbiamo il campionato più interessante con 6-7 club che si contendono lo scudetto"

(ANSA) - MILANO, 18 NOV - "La Premier League solo di diritti domestici fattura 2 miliardi, noi 900 milioni. Stiamo facendo la corsa sulla Spagna e sulla Germania. Che il calcio italiano abbia appeal è dimostrato dal fatto che 11 investitori internazionali abbiano deciso di investire qui". Lo dice il presidente della Lega Calcio Serie A, Ezio Simonelli, durante l'evento Rcs Sport Industry Talk.

"Abbiamo il campionato più interessante - aggiunge il numero uno della Lega Serie A -, possiamo vendere 30-35 partite top tra 6-7 club che si contendono il campionato, le altre leghe invece ne vendono 3-4. La nostra forza è essere un campionato competitivo, stiamo lavorando per far sì che anche all'estero il nostro torneo abbia ancora più attrattiva". (ANSA).

#2248. Prince William leads tributes to Harry Kane as England captain breaks appearance record with 126th cap: Royal presents star with shirt marking occasion as Wembley celebrates milestone

By BEN WILLCOCKS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 14:45 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 20:17 EST, 12 November 2026

Prince William led tributes for Harry Kane as Wembley honoured the England captain on reaching his record-breaking 126th cap against Croatia on Thursday.

Kane, who became the first Englishman since Michael Owen in 2001 to win the Ballon d'Or last month, celebrated another remarkable feat as he surpassed legendary goalkeeper Peter Shilton as England's most capped player in history.

After walking out with his four children for kick-off, followed by his England team-mates, the Bayern Munich forward first held his Ballon d'Or trophy aloft to Wembley's buoyant crowd, soaking up the celebrations.

Shilton approached the England captain to present him with a medal displaying his achievement, before Kane's former Spurs team-mate Luka Modric continued the tributes by offering him another gift.

Prince William then walked on to the pitch to shake Kane's hand and present him with a framed shirt, bearing the words 'Kane 126'.

As well as holding the record for England's most capped player, the 33-year-old striker is also England's highest ever scorer with 91 goals, ahead of Wayne Rooney and Sir Bobby Charlton who netted 53 and 49 respectively during their careers.

Prince William shakes Harry Kane's hand as he celebrates the England captain's record cap

The Prince led tributes as Wembley honoured Kane ahead of England's clash with Croatia

Kane, accompanied by his four children on the pitch, held his Ballon d'Or aloft to supporters

Prince William applauded 'brilliant and remarkable' Kane on his Ballon d'Or triumph with a personal social media message after the ceremony in London.

The Prince of Wales, signing his message off with his personal 'W' initial, posted on Instagram: 'Huge congratulations to Harry Kane on winning the Ballon d'Or! What a fantastic achievement and so deserved.

'A brilliant captain, a remarkable player and someone who has given football fans across the world so many unforgettable moments.'

Kane, who has been widely regarded as a model professional and humble superstar during his career, admitted he still has an ego of sorts in an interview on the eve of his record-breaking England cap.

Asked if he has an ego, Kane replied: ‘That's a great question. I’m sure my wife will be able to say a thing or two!

'But ego is funny, because ego is many different things. I guess I have an ego to be the best, because I want to be the best. I just don't do it in a show-off way.’

On becoming England's highest appearance maker, he joked that some of his current Three Lions team-mates might catch his total by the end of their careers.

‘Jude (Bellingham) and Declan (Rice), they're on 60-odd caps already and you look at how young they are,’ he added. ‘So I'll try and give them a good number to catch! The goal record is going to be the one that's difficult to beat. I think that’s good!

He has passed Peter Shilton as England's most capped player in history - with 126 to his name

Kane receives a gift from ex-Spurs team-mate and fellow Ballon d'Or winner Luka Modric

’In football, you’ve never completed it. No matter what you’ve achieved, there is always a trophy that someone else has won or a goal record that someone else has. This is no time to rest. If anything, I've got better year on year, and the numbers have got better.

'While I'm feeling like this, why would I put any limit on myself? I'm just on the back of scoring 73 goals in one year, which is incredible, and I want to get 100 England goals as quickly as possible.

‘My biggest fear is finishing my career and thinking I could have done a bit more. I want to make sure that I cross every bridge and do everything possible so that, whenever that day comes, I can say, “I’ve done everything possible. I have this record, I don’t have this record, but it doesn’t matter, I can sleep well at night”.’

#2247. Marca: "E' emerso questo club come possibile destinazione per Ausilio dopo l'addio all'Inter"

Marca: "E' emerso questo club come possibile destinazione per Ausilio dopo l'addio all'Inter"

Quale futuro per Piero Ausilio dopo il suo addio all'Inter? Del direttore sportivo ha parlato così Matteo Moretto in un editoriale su Marca: "Il suo addio all'Inter, ufficializzato nel settembre 2026 dopo il mancato raggiungimento di un accordo per il rinnovo del contratto, ha posto fine a un rapporto che ha attraversato diverse fasi societarie, proprietarie e cicli sportivi. Ausilio è arrivato al club alla fine degli anni '90 e ha scalato le gerarchie fino a diventare direttore sportivo, ruolo che ha ricoperto per oltre un decennio.

La sua carriera è indissolubilmente legata ai successi nerazzurri, ma anche alla sua capacità di destreggiarsi in circostanze molto diverse. Dalle difficoltà finanziarie e dai cambi di proprietà alla costruzione delle squadre che hanno riportato l'Inter ai vertici del calcio italiano ed europeo. Il suo lavoro si è basato su diversi pilastri: individuazione dei talenti, negoziazione, gestione dei contratti e, soprattutto, relazioni personali. La sua conoscenza del mercato internazionale e i suoi contatti costanti con agenti e club sono tra i principali punti di forza che ha accumulato nel corso della sua carriera.

Durante la sua partecipazione al Social Football Summit di Torino, Ausilio ha parlato apertamente della fine della sua esperienza all'Inter. Ha ritenuto che fosse giunto il momento di separarsi dopo aver ottenuto risultati significativi con il club. Non ha inoltre nascosto il suo interesse ad affrontare una nuova sfida in Premier League. Il Tottenham è emerso come una delle possibili destinazioni per il prossimo futuro. Per un professionista che ha trascorso praticamente tutta la carriera nello stesso club, la sua prossima destinazione potrebbe rappresentare un cambiamento di scenario particolarmente significativo".

#2244. Supercoppa Inter-Lazio 2026: biglietti, vendita e prezzi previsti

La sfida è in programma il 22 dicembre alle 21 a San Siro; per i tagliandi sono previste prelazioni prima della vendita libera.

La Supercoppa Italiana 2026 si giocherà il 22 dicembre a San Siro: l’Inter affronterà la Lazio alle 21, ora locale. Secondo quanto riportato da Goal, Per acquistare i biglietti, i canali indicati sono Vivaticket e i portali dei club, con fasi di prelazione prima dell’eventuale vendita libera. I prezzi riportati sono invece stime, non tariffe ufficiali.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La competizione torna alla formula a due squadre e si disputa nuovamente in Italia. L’Inter partecipa da campione in carica della Serie A; la Lazio è la finalista sconfitta dell’ultima Coppa Italia.

I nerazzurri hanno preso parte alle ultime sei edizioni della Supercoppa, vincendo tre titoli consecutivi tra il 2021 e il 2023. L’ultimo successo biancoceleste risale al 2019. Lo scorso dicembre il trofeo è andato al Napoli, che ha battuto il Bologna 2-0 al King Saud University Stadium di Riad.

L’Inter di Cristian Chivu giocherà nel proprio stadio e arriva alla sfida con una serie di dieci partite senza sconfitte contro la Lazio. Tra queste, Goal indica anche il 2-0 nella finale di Coppa Italia di maggio.

I biglietti ufficiali per la Supercoppa sono venduti principalmente tramite Vivaticket, indicato come partner ufficiale della Serie A per la biglietteria, e attraverso i portali delle due società partecipanti.

La vendita gestita dai club per le gare di Supercoppa segue solitamente più fasi. Si comincia, settimane prima della partita, con la prelazione per gli abbonati. Seguono le fasi riservate ai titolari della tessera del tifoso o della CRN; se rimangono posti disponibili, si passa alla vendita libera.

Per informazioni sui tagliandi, il riferimento indicato è costituito dai siti ufficiali dei club. La disponibilità sulle piattaforme di rivendita può essere consultata separatamente.

#2242. Record-breaking Harry Kane belongs in the company of greats that he keeps. There is only one gap in his record now and he remains as central to ending 60 years of hurt than he has ever been, writes OLIVER HOLT

Published: 16:54 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 20:17 EST, 12 November 2026

Only giants have trodden the path that Harry Kane walked at Wembley on Thursday when he strode out on to the pitch on a bitterly cold winter night, his four young children by his side, and beheld the array of trophies, honours, adulation and respect that awaited him.

It is just 16 days since Kane held his hands to his face, climbed up to the stage at the London Palladium and said 'wow' when Ronaldo 'Il Fenomeno' presented him with the Ballon d'Or trophy that anointed him the best player in the world.

Now, it sat before him on a plinth and Kane broke away from the England line-up and walked forward to hoist it aloft. He showed it to all the corners of the stadium and for a moment, it was framed against the giant tifo bearing his image and the words 'England's Finest' that towered over one end of the ground.

But the Ballon d’Or is old news in the flood of honours that is coming Kane’s way in this golden summer of a great career. Kane had become the first Englishman to win the Ballon d’Or for a quarter of a century and on Thursday night he was to break the 37-year-old record held by Peter Shilton for the most England caps. He scored the winner, too. Of course, he did.

The occasion of England-Croatia in the Nations League was Kane's 126th appearance in an England shirt and Shilton stepped forward to hand him a small case containing a crest to mark the achievement. Finally, the Prince of Wales showed his appreciation to the England captain by presenting him with a commemorative shirt with 'Kane 126' emblazoned on it.

It was a night to feel the weight of football's history and the ghosts of greatness that people England's heritage. Kane belongs in the company he now keeps. He belongs in the pantheon of the game because he is one of the deadliest strikers in history. He belongs in the pantheon because of the humility he has shown and the dedication he devotes to improvement.

Harry Kane celebrated his record-breaking England appearance with a finely struck penalty

Before kick-off, Kane's history-making was celebrated with a commemorative shirt by the Prince of Wales

England (4-2-3-1): Pickford 6; Alexander-Arnold 6 (White 78, 6), Quansah 4, Burn 6, Hall 7; Rice 5 (Garner 78, 5), Anderson 6; Rogers 8, Bellingham 6, Gordon 5 (Ngumoha 78, 5); Kane 7

Croatia (4-2-3-1): Livakovic 5; Smolcic 6 (Marco Pasalic 76 5), Stanisic 6, Vuskovic 6, Gvardiol 5; Misic 5 (Modric 76, 6), Kovacic 6; Sucic 7 (Mario Pasalic 70, 5), Baturina 6, Vlasic 5; Matanovic 5 (Budimir 70, 5)

He belongs with Billy Wright, the first England player to 100 caps, and the man who set the England appearance record at 105 when he played for his country for the last time in 1959. He belongs with Bobby Charlton, who made it to 106 before the heartbreak of the 1970 World Cup quarter-final defeat to West Germany brought his international career to an end.

Kane belongs with Bobby Moore, Charlton's rival as the greatest England player of all time, who reached the new mark of 108 caps in November 1973. That was one of the great numbers in English sport until Peter Shilton finally surpassed it. He reached 125 caps by the time he played his last game for England in the third-place play-off at the 1990 World Cup.

It is quite some lineage. And now it is Kane who holds the prize. And if this was hardly a momentous match, it was a momentous occasion. If you are tired of glorying in a player like Kane, a relentless striker, a wonderful passer, the sweetest striker of the ball you will ever see, a player of vision, a man who exudes the beauty of simplicity and the wonder of modesty, then you are tired of football.

The honours are rushing at him in a giddy, babbling flood. It may be that he reaches close to 200 caps before he retires and even if it already seems likely that that record will be broken by Jude Bellingham, who has 61 caps at the age of 23, Kane's England goals tally, which stands at 92 after another goal against Croatia, will not be under threat for a generation or longer.

The gap, of course, is a tournament victory. That is what still separates Moore and Charlton from him. Kane is a dream of a player but he has not won the World Cup or the European Championship. What remains is the hope that he will put that right in the years that he has left in the game.

Winning the Nations League would not be quite the same but it would be a start and this victory over Croatia took England a small step closer to that goal. A convincing win over Spain in Madrid on Sunday would take them closer to their aim of finishing top of this group and getting a favourable draw in the quarter-finals in the spring.

Kane made his England debut in March 2015 when he was 21 and England's future looks brighter now than it did when he broke through. More than an hour before the game, the men who will follow Kane and perhaps even chase his caps record, chatted animatedly on the pitch. Bellingham, Morgan Rogers, Anthony Gordon and Morgan Gibbs-White are the new breed.

Morgan Rogers opened the scoring inside the opening half-an-hour and celebrated with his trademark cold celebration

England had found themselves pegged back by Croatia after slopping defending drew the visitors level

Referee Morten Krogh was summoned to the monitor to review the penalty shout on Thursday

Kane returned to Wembley triumphant for the first time since his historic Ballon d'Or win

After the Kane festivities, the first half was an anti-climax. Twenty-four minutes of relative drudgery ensued before Kane exhibited the breadth of his talent once again to help put England ahead. He got the ball 30 yards out, looked up and drifted a beautiful diagonal pass out to the right and into the path of Bellingham. Bellingham volleyed it across goal and Rogers forced it over the line.

The second half began slowly, too, but 15 minutes into it, Croatia equalised. England failed to clear a corner, Jarell Quansah tried to head the ball back to Jordan Pickford and, as Kane watched on, Petar Sucic stole in and hooked the ball past the England goalkeeper into the roof of the net.

Quansah made some kind of amends three minutes later when he was dragged down in the box and Danish referee Morten Krogh awarded a penalty. It was Kane-time again. Kane took the kick and lifted it gently and with a touch of curl down the centre of the goal. Dominic Livakovic dived to his left and the ball sailed softly over his trailing leg and into the net.

Croatia had chances to draw level but could not take them. England made changes and young Rio Ngumoha, a winger of rare talent, another with a rich future ahead of him, another who will help to fill the void when the day comes for Kane to bid farewell to England, came on as a late substitute for Gordon.

And so Kane's night of history began to draw to a close. In a 2-1 victory, he had made one and scored one. He remains as central to England's hopes of ending more than 60 years without a trophy as he ever has been. In a career that has festooned him with garlands and left no one in doubt of his greatness, it is the only gap that remains.

Harry Kane has set his sights firmly on rapidly reaching a century of international goals after becoming England’s most-capped player of all time.

The England skipper marked his 126th appearance for the Three Lions with the winner against Croatia in the Nations League – his first goal since picking up the Ballon d’Or last month.

Kane was afforded an emotional presentation pre-match at Wembley to mark both achievements and is already thinking of creating more history after moving within eight goals of a ton.

‘It’s achievable,’ Kane said. ‘I’m getting closer. Breaking the appearance record is a massive thing in the history of our game, seeing the tifo at the stadium and having my kids with me was beyond dreams.

‘It’s important to recognise our great players. It’s maybe something we haven’t done so well over the years with some of our other greats.

‘It's been a crazy few weeks. I don’t know if I’ve fully taken it all in. The reception has been overwhelming.

‘Maybe during the winter break I’ll be on a beach reflecting on everything I’ve achieved, having a drink with my wife.’

Croatia legend Luka Modric, 41, was part of the presentations ahead of kick off and Kane did not rule out attempting to prolong his career in a similar vein to the AC Milan midfielder.

‘He’s an inspiration for a lot of us,’ Kane added. ‘An incredible player and a great person, he’s looked after himself and still to be playing at this level is inspiring for me to push the limits. To see where your body and mentality takes you.

‘As long as I feel I can help, I’ll be here for as long as I can. If that’s until I’m 40, we’ll wait and see.’

Now qualified for the quarter-finals, England need to beat Spain in Madrid on Sunday to top their group and Thomas Tuchel believes they can topple the world champions.

‘It’s a completely different scenario, completely different setup,’ Tuchel said. ‘But I'm very positive that we will get what we want.’

#2241. Ranking FIFA: l'Italia guadagna una posizione e sale al 14° posto

(ANSA) - ROMA, 18 NOV - L'Italia targata Mancini 2.0 guadagna una posizione nel ranking Fifa, aggiornato dopo l'intensa finestra internazionale di novembre, e sale dal 15/o al 14/ posto. Le prime quattro posizioni rimangono invariate: la Spagna, campione del mondo, mantiene il comando, seguita da Argentina, Francia e Inghilterra. Nelle posizioni di vertice, spicca il Marocco che sale al quinto posto, sua miglior posizione di sempre, superando il Portogallo (6/o) e il Brasile (7/o), che scivolano entrambi di una posizione. Belgio e Olanda restano ottava e nona e nella top 10 rientra la Colombia, per la prima volta dall'ottobre 2024, a spese del Messico, che perde tre posizioni, finendo alle spalle di Germania (11/a, +2) e Svizzera (12/a, -) e subito davanti agli azzurri.

Nella top 50, crescono Canada (25/o, +2), Corea del Sud (30/a, +2), Paraguay (31/o, +2), Mali (43/o, +4) e Slovenia (49/a, +3). L'Uefa esprime la metà dei membri della top 50, la Caf ne conta dieci, la Conmebol sette, mentre AFC e Concacaf ne hanno quattro ciascuna. (ANSA).

#2240. Balotelli: “Mourinho? Vi racconto i suoi giochetti psicologici! Entravo dicendo: 'Chiudi la bocca se...'"

Balotelli: “Mourinho? Vi racconto i suoi giochetti psicologici! Entravo dicendo: 'Chiudi la bocca se...'"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Intervistato nel documentario Winning per DAZN, Mario Balotelli, ex calciatore dell'Inter, ha raccontato così il suo rapporto con Jose Mourinho: "Una parola per descrivere Mourinho? Solo una? E' impossibile. Quattro: severo, divertente, a volte era come un bambino, geniale perché era sempre un passo avanti a tutti, e vincente. La Champions? Tutti vogliono vincerla, ma al momento della semifinale era difficile crederci. Mourinho era come un mental coach: ti faceva giochetti psicologico che alla fine ti spingevano a combattere per lui.

Giocavamo col Chievo in trasferta e prima della gara ci siamo trovati per parlare di tattica: ma quando è partito il video, nelle immagini c'ero io mentre giocavo con amici in un torneo 3 contro 3 al parco. Ho pensato: "Ma che sta facendo?". Credevo lo facesse per far ridere la squadra, ma il messaggio era: "Se ti senti libero di giocare così, devi farlo anche durante la partita. Se ami il calcio così e domani non vinciamo, non giocherai più". Abbiamo vinto 1-0 e ho segnato (ride, ndr).

Con me è stato come un fratello maggiore, quindi a volte è stato molto duro. Anche perché io ho un carattere particolare. Sapeva come tirare fuori il meglio di te: non sempre nel modo migliore, a volte lo faceva facendoti arrabbiare, infastidendoti. Entravo in campo dicendo: "Oggi segno tre gol così puoi chiudere la bocca". Ma era quello che voleva, no?".

#2239. McTominay a gennaio non si muoverà, ma il Napoli deve risolvere la questione rinnovo

Il Mattino fa il punto sul futuro di Scott McTominay, centrocampista del Napoli, il cui contratto è in scadenza nel giugno 2028: “Gli agenti dello scelto sono avvisati: nessuna fretta per l’incontro con il Napoli per prolungare il contratto ma anche nessuna trattativa a gennaio. Lo scozzese non si muoverà nella sessione di invernale. E anche lui, nonostante i problemi fisici che non fa che denunciare, quando c’è da indossare la maglia della Scozia non si tira indietro E ieri sera, nella partita contro la Svizzera che doveva decretare il pass per lo spareggio per la promozione nel gruppo A della Nations League, lui è sceso in campo da titolare E con lui pure Gilmour”.

#2238. Pulisic magic moment: Chris è tornato decisivo nel Milan e in Nazionale

Lo avevamo già raccontanto, ma Pulisic ha deciso di confermarlo direttamente sul campo. Gol in rovesciata con gli Stati Uniti e un momento d'oro anche in rossonero. Chris è stato infatti decisivo nelle ultime uscite ufficiali del Milan in campionato, tra gol nel derby, a Udine e contro il Genoa. Dopo una seconda parte di stagione da incubo vissuta con Massimiliano Allegri, Capitan America sembra essersi ripreso finalmente il suo posto nel Milan. Quello da leader assoluto.

Dopo un Mondiale un po' sottotono, anche complicato dagli infortuni, e la prima sosta stagionale saltata proprio per dei problemi fisici, il giocatore del Milan in questa sosta si è ripreso anche la sua Nazionale. Dopo la doppietta nella gara di andata contro Haiti in CONCACAF Nations League, anche in quella di ritorno Pulisic ha lasciato il segno con un gol in rovesciata e una prestazione globale da applausi nelle scorse ore contro Haiti.

"Pulisic secondo me, e l'ho visto da vicino a Genova, è tornato quel giocatore del 2025. Non nella seconda parte, ma quello di inizio anno con Allegri e di fine stagione con Conceiçao. E' un giocatore dominante, il più importante della squadra insieme a Rabiot. Chris è capace di essere spesso decisivo nelle partite decisive. Lo vedo bene anche fisicamente, prima non lo era ma anche di testa. E' in fiducia e il sistema di gioco di Amorim lo aiuta, si trova il suo spazio e va a lavorare con e senza palla. Sono convinto potrà fare davvero una grandissima stagione".

#2236. Nazionale, Mandas: "Sensazione agrodolce. Lazio? Ottimo momento"

Al termine di Grecia-Serbia, terminata 1-0 ma con l'eliminazione del club greco dalla Nations League, Christos Mandas ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni

Al termine di Grecia-Serbia, terminata 1-0 ma con l'eliminazione del club greco dalla Nations League, Christos Mandas ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni dei cronisti presenti.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

È una sensazione agrodolce: abbiamo ottenuto la vittoria, ma non siamo riusciti a chiudere il girone tra le prime due. Nonostante questo, abbiamo dimostrato di potercela giocare contro chiunque. Sono felice di essere partito titolare e sarò pronto ogni volta che il ct avrà bisogno di me. La permanenza nella Lega A di Nations League? Ci siamo guadagnati questo livello affrontando grandi squadre, ce lo meritiamo e ora sta a noi continuare così.

Questa è stata la mia prima partita, le altre le ho guardate dalla panchina. Ma conosco questo gruppo e le loro qualità, per questo non sono sorpreso dal numero dei punti che abbiamo fatto. Siamo riusciti a superare i nostri stessi limiti, perché giocare contro avversari di questo calibro non è affatto semplice. E poi il popolo greco ha dimostrato di essere sempre al nostro fianco: sono la nostra forza e speriamo di renderli felici.

Dal punto di vista calcistico sto vivendo un ottimo momento alla Lazio. È una fase molto positiva per me, ma non ancora la migliore perché ho ancora tanto altro da dare.

#2235. Pat Cummins' remarkable 68-minute press conference took a clear-eyed and devastating aim at Usman Khawaja's integrity - Australia are in a civil war with no obvious resolution, writes LAWRENCE BOOTH

Published: 05:20 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 05:21 EST, 13 November 2026

Pat Cummins is either the greatest actor in world sport – or the claim that the whole Australian team knew about the sandpaper plot in South Africa eight years ago is a figment of Usman Khawaja’s imagination.

After Cummins’s remarkable press conference in Sydney on Friday morning, in which he called Khawaja’s various assertions ‘complete and utter nonsense’ and questioned the validity of their friendship, it was hard to discern any common ground, much less spot a middle way.

According to the blue corner, everyone was in on the scam, sandpaper was used not just at Cape Town but in the previous Test at Port Elizabeth, and there was a team meeting to discuss the question of collective responsibility.

According to the red, those who knew about the plan did not include the bowlers, nothing untoward happened at Port Elizabeth, and there was no meeting.

The only thing that can be said for certain after another tumultuous 24 hours for Australian cricket was that the two corners are now polar opposites, each eyeing the other with suspicion and incredulity. Barring an improbable climbdown, the twain can never meet.

Cummins, it must be said, eased into his work after an emotional start behind a lectern at the Cricket Central venue in Sydney’s Olympic Park. He began by stating his basic position, then invited questions, which for the next hour and a bit he handled with aplomb and a degree of statesmanship.

Pat Cummins held a remarkable press conference in Sydney to address the sandpaper claims

Speaking for more than an hour, Cummins dismissed Khawaja’s various assertions as ‘complete and utter nonsense’ and questioned the validity of their friendship

This will not please those who still find it impossible to believe that Cummins and his fellow seamers on that trip, Josh Hazlewood and Mitchell Starc, knew nothing of the scheme that led to bans for three batsmen – Steve Smith, David Warner and Cameron Bancroft.

After all, as any bowler will tell you, the idea that the condition of their most prized possession might be altered without their noticing is as absurd as trying to persuade a batsman to walk out without his favourite willow.

And yet. Cummins stuck to his guns, in contrast to Khawaja’s appearance on the ABC on Wednesday evening, when he repeatedly declined to name Cummins as one of the guilty party. And while Khawaja reacted to his TV grilling by cancelling media engagements, Cummins made it clear he would be answering every question that came his way.

Whichever side you believe, and whatever the doubts that remain, his press conference grabbed the momentum from Team Khawaja.

It also made the previous day’s appearance by Mitchell Marsh, Australia’s white-ball captain for the next three weeks against England, all the more bizarre. By refusing to engage with any of the questions raised by Khawaja’s autobiography, Marsh – himself a veteran of South Africa 2018 – did little to dispel the suspicion that Khawaja had a point.

And that in turn encouraged the view that Cummins, who called his own press conference without the knowledge of Cricket Australia, might be about to come clean.

Instead, he doubled down on the denial he and his bowling colleagues had issued in 2021, but now went further, taking a clear-eyed and devastating aim at Khawaja’s integrity.

‘Usman is a friend, but friends aren’t casual with each other’s lives and reputations,’ said Cummins. ‘He’s dealt with the way he’s presented his story in a very casual manner and it affects a lot of people. That’s what I’m grappling with at the moment, because I don’t think that’s OK. It’s not what friends do.’

Cummins plainly intended this to be his final word on the matter, but the problem facing Australian cricket is that the sore may never properly heal.

David Warner then hit back at Cummins and accused him of 'throwing Khawaja under the bus'

Only a couple of hours after Cummins finished, Warner – at Perth’s Optus Stadium as a TV pundit for the first ODI between Australia and England – was being asked for his view, and he didn’t hold back, repeating his belief that Khawaja’s version of events was ‘the truth’.

He added: ‘I really, really support him. I actually feel for him at the moment. I feel that the captain today threw Uzzy under the bus and I’m very, very disappointed about that.’

Disappointment. It’s mutual, and it’s everywhere. Cummins provided a brief distraction, but it doesn’t change the fact that this is a civil war with no obvious resolution.

Offered the chance to kick Australian cricket while it was down, England made humiliating contact with fresh air on the opening day of their eight-match white-ball trip.

Only hours after Pat Cummins had added a twist to the sandpaper saga by dismissing Usman Khawaja’s claims that the whole squad knew about the ball-tampering plans in South Africa eight years ago as ‘complete and utter nonsense’, Harry Brook’s side were blown away for 131 in the first ODI – having at one stage been 65 for eight.

Australia then romped home by seven wickets with more than 26 overs in hand to take a 1–0 lead into the second game of this three-match series in Adelaide on Sunday. For England, anyway, complete and utter nonsense felt about right.

It is not quite a year since they began their Ashes campaign here at the Optus Stadium by being bowled out in 32.5 overs in their first innings and 34.4 in their second – before stumbling to a 4–1 defeat.

Now, they obligingly split the difference, dismissed in 33.4 as Mahli Beardman, a 21-year-old fast bowler from Dunsborough, a couple of hours drive down the coast from Perth, picked up six for 34 – the best figures for Australia by an ODI debutant.

His first victim was Joe Root, caught behind for a painful 17 off 36 balls, and his second Brook, who had made only a single when he fidgeted fatally outside off stump.

That might have been enough to satisfy most newcomers, but Beardman had barely started. Jordan Cox made two before edging a poke, and Sam Curran top-edged a pull to fine leg for a duck.

When Liam Dawson, yet to score, squeezed a drive to backward point, Beardman had five wickets in 28 balls – not bad for a player whose only previous taste of international cricket had come in a T20 in Pakistan in January.

He then ended a lively last-wicket stand of 29 by removing Jofra Archer, who had just driven him down the ground for four. It was the only authentic boundary England managed off his bowling: the other, a streaky inside edge from Root, summed up the problems posed by his pace, bounce and accuracy. As bids for a place on next summer’s Ashes tour go, this was hard to ignore.

‘Laying in bed last night, that’s not what I had planned,’ said Beardman, who was taken under his wing by Australian fast-bowling legend Dennis Lillee after a mutual friend spotted him as a young teenager bowling at a bin down the side of a house. ‘It was an incredible game and it all happened so fast.’

If England had learned lessons from their collapses in the last year’s Test here, they showed little sign of being able to implement them. Players drove on the up, as they had so heedlessly a year earlier, and failed to cope with the Perth bounce.

England head coach Brendon McCullum was critical of his side's failure to adapt to the pitch, and called on them to learn from their mistakes going forward.

'It’s a bit like batting on a trampoline,' said McCullum. 'There are certain shots that are very difficult to execute. We talked about trying to put those away. We probably knew we were in for a low-scoring contest, but we expected to get a few more runs than 130.

'We're disappointed but there are some good lessons for us. If you look towards the World Cup in South Africa there’s going to be some pitches there that will test us from a bounce point of view.'

And all this after they had played a warm-up game at the WACA – a grievous omission before the Ashes – and trained with such intensity that white-ball mentor Kevin Pietersen had declared it the best practice session he had seen from an England team.

In the event, their top-scorer was Gus Atkinson, who hit an unbeaten 29 from No 10, including England’s only six.

Mitchell Marsh, stroke-less in the previous day’s press conference, put that into perspective by clearing the ropes four times during a 29-ball half-century, while Cooper Connolly – dropped by Tom Banton at midwicket on five – hit two sixes himself.

England’s only consolation had come at the start of Australia’s reply, when Atkinson bowled Travis Head first ball off the inside edge. Head it was who had taken care of Australia’s fourth-innings chase here a year ago, thrashing a 69-ball century that knocked the stuffing out of Ben Stokes’s attack.

Instead, it was Marsh who went for his shots, stopped only when he flicked Jamie Overton to Ben Duckett at deep midwicket, by which time Australia were already halfway there. For a few hours, at least, no one was talking about sandpaper.

#2233. Stati Uniti: Pochettino saluta Pulisic e compagni con una vittoria

(ANSA) - ROMA, 18 NOV - Il mandato di Mauricio Pochettino alla guida della nazionale statunitense si è concluso con una vittoria per 2-0 contro Haiti. L'argentino, che dal 2024 allenava gli Usa, si trasferisce al Manchester United in Inghilterra. Pochettino ha scambiato abbracci, scherzato e salutato i giocatori e lo staff sul campo dopo la partita. "Volevamo finire bene... Certo, è difficile perché è commovente.

Perché mi piacciono davvero questi ragazzi, mi piacciono i giocatori, mi piace lo staff", ha detto il tecnico. Pochettino, che aveva dichiarato di aver preso "una delle decisioni più difficili" della sua carriera, è un volto noto in Premier League, dove ha allenato il Tottenham (2014-2019), squadra con cui ha raggiunto la finale di Champions League nel 2019. Come selezionatore degli Stati Uniti, ha raggiunto gli ottavi di Coppa del Mondo 2026 dove è stato eliminato dal Belgio (4-1). (ANSA).

#2231. Sneijder: “Barca? Pensavamo di uscire, Mou ci convinse così. E che parole prima di Milan-Inter 0-4…”

Sneijder: “Barca? Pensavamo di uscire, Mou ci convinse così. E che parole prima di Milan-Inter 0-4…”

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Intervistato per il documentario Winning per DAZN, Wesley Sneijder, ex trequartista dell'Inter, ha parlato così del suo rapporto con Jose Mourinho: "Quando siamo arrivati in semifinale abbiamo pensato: "Ok, abbiamo fatto bene e ora è finita. Questa squadra, il Barcellona... Non possiamo batterli". Prima della partita ci siamo trovati, a poco a poco nella sala riunioni tutti si convinsero che avremmo potuto vincere se l'avessimo ascoltato. Ci descrisse i loro punti deboli nel minimo dettaglio. E dopo la partita, durante la cena, ho pensato: "Non sono così forti", non li faceva sembrare così forti.

E' incredibile, ne parlava come se fosse una squadra normale. Bisognava metterselo in testa. Li abbiamo battuti 3-1 in casa ma avremmo potuto segnare altre tre volte. Sapevamo che saremmo dovuti andare là al Camp Nou. Ci disse: "Dobbiamo difenderci: probabilmente segneranno un gol, ma poi basta". Abbiamo perso 1-0. Lo vedi come un allenatore difensivo, ma la settimana prima siamo stati molto offensivi: ma nessuno parla di quella partita. Ricordo partite in cui perdevamo, lui guardava la panchina e metteva tutti gli attaccanti che aveva.

Quando ero al Real Madrid mi contattò per telefono: era un periodo complicato. Fu quando arrivò il nuovo presidente, il momento in cui tutti i Galacticos andarono lì. Bisognava prendere una decisione: allora Mourinho cominciò a chiamarmi per convincermi di andare all'Inter. Perché ero il pezzo mancante della squadra, me lo diceva sempre. Quando ho deciso di accettare, le pratiche erano fatte ma il giorno dopo c'era il derby col Milan. Ci siamo detti: "Va bene", ci siamo abbracciati poi gli ho augurato buona fortuna per l'indomani e gli ho detto "ci vediamo nei prossimi allenamenti". E lui: "Come? No, tu rimani". Gli dissi: "Rimango? Ma non mi sono neanche allenato". E lui: "Non serve, so per cosa ho firmato: sei il pezzo che mancava, non posso iniziare la partita senza di te". Da quel momento ho cominciato a volare: abbiamo battuto il Milan 4-0 e ho giocato benissimo. In quella partita sono stato molto bravo".

#2230. Lazio, Agostinelli sul caso di Baldacchino: "Speravo la situazione si potesse risolvere"

Il giudizio riguarda chi è rimasto a Roma durante la sosta; Agostinelli racconta anche di aver visto Frattesi sorridente.

La Lazio non ha staccato la spina durante la sosta: è questo l’aspetto che ha più colpito il protagonista dell’intervento riportato da Radiosei e che gli dà sicurezza per il futuro. A suo giudizio, i giocatori rimasti a Roma hanno mostrato di voler restare concentrati e di meritare spazio. Nel suo intervento ha parlato anche degli infortuni, di Frattesi e della sua conoscenza con Kurt Baldacchino.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

L’intervento si apre con una precisazione: «Premesso che la mia conoscenza con Kurt (Baldacchino, ndr) risale a tre anni fa.»

Lui è il presidente del ‘Lazio club Malta’ e appunto tre anni fa mi premiò quando allenavo l’altra squadra di Malta (il Gudja United Club). Quest’anno si divide tra la mia squadra, il Mġarr United, e le giovanili. Sapevo di questa cosa e pensavo si potesse risolvere. Quando siamo arrivati, io sono andato a casa mia a Roma e con la squadra e ci siamo rivisti direttamente a Formello, incontro Kurt, bravo ragazzo e tifosissimo della Lazio, e mi dice che era rimasto fuori. Eravamo però a ridosso della gara, quindi ho raggiunto la squadra. Speravo la situazione si potesse risolvere poi invece non è andata così e mi è molto dispiaciuto perché pensavo potesse essere un ‘giorno di festa’. Ad ogni modo è un ragazzo splendido, ognuno può esprimere le sue idee non gli darei ‘la croce addosso’. Probabilmente il fatto si poteva risolvere in maniera serena.

Gli infortuni della Lazio? Avendo degli staff molto numerosi ci sono confronti continui. Oggi lo staff è molto importante. Frattesi l’ho visto sorridente. Per me un giocatore può fare mille cose atletiche ma lo giudico ‘guarito’ se fa una partita, anche solo di 15′ perché non sei frenato durante la partitella. Questo però non significa che non ci saranno rischi in gara.

Di questa Lazio l’aspetto che mi ha impressionato, e che mi dà sicurezza per il futuro, è che non hanno staccato la spina. Questo non è importante, di più. Significa che chi era a Roma durante la sosta ha voluto dimostrare di stare sul pezzo, di meritare spazio”.

#2229. Why Man United staff feel treatment of Michael Carrick was 'disgusting' and the comment from Omar Berrada that raised eyebrows as club legend is put out of his misery - and Mauricio Pochettino gets set to take over

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and DANIEL MATTHEWS, US SENIOR SPORTS WRITER

Published: 14:25 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 07:15 EST, 13 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino would have been fast asleep in his hotel room in the quirky American town of Trilith when Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox sat down with Michael Carrick at Carrington Thursday morning and confirmed the worst-kept secret in football.

It was time for Carrick to vacate the head coach’s office at Manchester United, and safe to say Pochettino will have his feet under the desk this time next week.

Carrick arrived at 7.50am but, unlike the last two days, he was no longer wearing his United training gear.

The agonising wait to be put out of his misery was over, and the club’s chief executive and director of football duly administered the last rites before Carrick left at 10.18am.

The decision was communicated to staff and Under-21s boss Adam Lawrence took training with the players who have remained at Carrington during the international break.

The first reports of Carrick’s sacking broke at 9.30am, but United insisted that they were premature and it wasn’t until 10.37am that the news was confirmed in a 142-word statement.

Michael Carrick finally left Carrington on Thursday morning after an agonising wait to be put out of his misery

Staff at Manchester United believe that the former club captain's treatment at the hands of their Ineos paymasters has been 'disgusting'

It's understood that his backroom staff - assistant Steve Holland, coaches Jonathan Woodgate, Jonny Evans and Travis Binnion, and goalkeeper coach Craig Mawson - have also gone. When Evans left on Wednesday night with his car piled high with cardboard boxes, it didn't bode well.

The treatment of Carrick and Evans, in particular, has upset staff. Two highly-respected figures at United, whose association with the club goes back more than 20 years, have been left hanging while their employers stumble over a succession plan.

Members of staff share Gary Neville’s view that Berrada and Wilcox meeting with Sir Jim Ratcliffe in Monaco, and making a move for Pochettino while Carrick had to keep driving past the cameras on his way into Carrington for the last three days, was ‘disgusting’.

It has also reignited anger over the 450 job cuts carried out by Ratcliffe and Ineos over the past three years while pouring millions down the drain in compensation.

The £10million to buy Pochettino and his staff out of their contracts with the US Soccer Federation, and the pay-off owing to Carrick and Co, will push the cost of hiring and firing coaches in the Ineos era close to £50m.

No wonder eyebrows were raised when Berrada spoke at Sir Alex Ferguson’s 40th anniversary dinner at Old Trafford on Friday night and talked about building a culture at the club.

The feeling is that changing United’s head coach won’t make a shred of difference if there isn’t significant change higher up the chain.

It has once again turned the spotlight on the relationship between Ratcliffe and the Glazer family, who from February can force him to sell his 28 per cent holding to a third party for less than the £25-per-share he paid in 2024 unless he can match the offer and buy the club himself.

However, United sources say they had little choice but to sack Carrick with the club in the relegation zone, having decided to give him a stay of execution at the end of last month.

Since then, the club had carried out a comprehensive review and come to the conclusion that a record of one win from 10 Premier League games was unacceptable.

The sources indicated that the United board shared the frustration and anger felt by fans over the situation, and believe that a change of head coach will bring about an improvement from a squad that is far better than results would suggest.

While United praised Carrick for his ‘highest degree of professionalism’, there was only silence from the players on social media.

Many of them are on international duty, including Bruno Fernandes whose criticism of Carrick’s tactics after the Champions League defeat to Como proved so damaging.

Following the announcement of Carrick's dismissal none of his former players - including Bruno Fernandes - were quick to say their goodbyes publicly

Meanwhile, his would-be successor Mauricio Pochettino is set to face the media on Friday

Pochettino will inherit this squad of players once he has completed his duties as US national team coach with the upcoming CONCACAF Nations League double-header against Haiti.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is due to speak at 2.15pm Friday ahead of the first leg in Miami on Saturday night when he will face questions over the United job for the first time.

Pochettino is expected to stay in charge for the second leg in Cincinnati in the early hours of Wednesday morning before flying to Manchester to meet the players and prepare for the crunch clash with Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday.

The US have been staying in Trilith, near their new training headquarters in Fayetteville south-west of Atlanta, where they were also based before the World Cup.

It’s a unique place designed for the film industry and built around the Trilith Studios which are the largest outside Hollywood. The ideal place for Pochettino to prepare for life at Old Trafford.

He has embraced the American lifestyle since he was appointed US coach in September 2024, a few months after leaving Chelsea, and will be sorry to leave.

Pochettino is said to have developed a love of bourbon whiskey, country music and Nashville, with Ella Langley’s Choosin’ Texas one of his favourite tunes.

He has admitted crying over Miracle, the movie about the US hockey team made up of college kids famously beating the Soviet Union to the gold medal at the 1980 Winter Olympics.

But the pull of Manchester United was always going to be too great, and very soon he will be swapping Trilith for Old Trafford.

#2228. Se insegui la Champions servono due top acquisti. Il precedente di Pairetto

Parliamoci chiaro a proposito del prossimo mercato di gennaio: se il Milan vuole potenziare la rosa e provare a inseguire il posto in Champions (la concorrenza questa volta è ancora più accreditata rispetto a un anno fa e allo stesso tempo più numerosa) deve rispettare alcuni parametri. Il primo: prendere un difensore che colmi la lacuna principale attuale rappresentata non tanto dalla marcatura (De Winter è migliorato in questo senso) ma dalla capacità di procurare l’uscita del gioco da dietro. E qui le indicazioni dei cronisti di mercato portano al portoghese Gonçalo Inacio, 25 anni dello Sporting oggi allenato da Fonseca. Non ho da eccepire, metto solo sull’avviso: al numero dei portoghesi a disposizione di Amorim deve corrispondere una qualità indiscutibile. Il secondo: per l’attacco è preferibile la pista del brasiliano Endrick, anche se molto complicata, a quella di Tresoldi per almeno due motivi -il costo del cartellino preteso dal Bruges, le caratteristiche di centravanti puro, doppione di Ramos.

Condivido invece l’idea di mandare a giocare i due ragazzi Camarda e Comotto. Su Camarda aggiungo una riflessione: è il caso di ascoltare il giudizio che di lui ha dato Baldini, ct Under 21, per capire qualcosa di più del giovanotto di appena 18 anni. È psicologicamente a terra perché poco utilizzato e messo in discussione sia dalla critica che dalla tifoseria. Ha bisogno di riguadagnare autostima. A Comotto farà sicuramente bene l’eventuale trasferimento dei 5 mesi alla corte di DDR a Genova ma va sostituito numericamente in rosa.

GIORNO DI RIPOSO- Ho chiesto informazioni sul giorno di riposo decretato da Amorim nella settimana che precede il ritorno del campionato dopo la sosta del campionato. Mi è stato risposto: perché poi comincia la giostra e non ci si ferma più. Verissimo. In effetti bisognerà attendere il ritorno dei nazionali dai rispettivi paesi nella speranza che da Pulisic in avanti recuperino tutte le energie spese. Già perché ripeto ancora una volta il monito: ci sono stati calciatori che, senza avere alcunché, sono riusciti -invocando un generico affaticamento- a saltare le convocazioni e adesso, freschi e riposati, si ripresenteranno in campionato e diventeranno decisivi. Da qui a marzo prossimo non ci sarà più stop e quindi le eventuali contromisure sono da rinviare al 2027!

PRECEDENTE- Non ci sono motivi per discutere le designazioni di Orsato che riscuote la mia personale fiducia se non censurare qualche prestazione discutibilissima (tipo Massa in Genoa-Milan). Il nuovo designatore non risponde a nessun potere federale se non al proprio metodo di conduzione. Per Milan-Frosinone c’è Pairetto che ha un paio di precedenti chiacchierati: il primo si perde nella notte dei tempi (Tomori espulso in Empoli-Milan nonostante il suo avversario fosse in fuorigioco!), il secondo è più recente e si riferisce a Milan-Parma dello scorso anno (0-1) quando l’arbitro di campo annullò il gol di Troilo per un precedente blocco fatto su Maignan. Richiamato al VAR, ascoltammo l’intervento di Pairetto che invadendo il campo del varista (era avar nell’occasione) impose la propria opinione facendo confermare il gol. Con una clamorosa coda: la settimana successiva, in Parma-Cagliari, Pellegrino provò a ripetere il blocco sul portiere del Cagliari ma l’arbitro intervenne per far sapere che non era pratica consentita. Il tutto dovuto grazie all’intervento della Fifa sul tema (Collina; ndr).

#2222. Inter, Calhanoglu fa causa a una società di criptovalute: chiesti 2,5 mln di lire turche. Ecco perché

Inter, Calhanoglu fa causa a una società di criptovalute: chiesti 2,5 mln di lire turche. Ecco perché

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Hakan Calhanoglu porta una società di criptovalute in tribunale. Il centrocampista dell'Inter e della Nazionale turca ha avviato un'azione legale contro Velvet, piattaforma specializzata nella compravendita di criptovalute, accusandola di aver utilizzato alcune sue fotografie sui social network senza autorizzazione e per finalità commerciali.

Secondo quanto riportato dalla stampa turca, in particolare da Günaydın, Calhanoglu ha chiesto un risarcimento di 2,5 milioni di lire turche ( circa 45.000 euro), sostenendo che la società abbia violato il suo diritto all'immagine.

Il centrocampista ha inoltre evidenziato come i contenuti pubblicati non avessero alcun collegamento diretto con la sua attività sportiva. Secondo la ricostruzione contenuta nel ricorso, la società avrebbe sfruttato la popolarità del giocatore, acquisita anche grazie alle prestazioni con la maglia dell'Inter, per attirare l'attenzione degli utenti e ottenere vantaggi commerciali.

Da qui la richiesta di risarcimento: 2,5 milioni di lire turche per il presunto utilizzo illecito della sua immagine.

Adesso si apre un nuovo procedimento. Secondo le notizie provenienti dalla Turchia, il tribunale di Istanbul ha accettato il fascicolo relativo alla causa contro Velvet, che sarà esaminata prossimamente.

L'accettazione del fascicolo non rappresenta una decisione sul merito della controversia: resta infatti da accertare se le fotografie siano state utilizzate senza autorizzazione e se sussistano i presupposti per il risarcimento richiesto dal calciatore.

Fonte: Günaydın / Sabah, 18 novembre 2026.
