#2218. France star Desire Doue is left with gruesome tooth injury after PSG youngster suffered elbow smash to the face during Les Bleus' dismal defeat by Italy

Published: 06:41 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 07:35 EST, 13 November 2026

France star Desire Doue was left with a loose front tooth after being hit with a nasty elbow in France's 3-1 defeat to Italy in the Nations League.

The PSG winger was left in need of some emergency dental work by Italy's Davide Frattesi who caught the 21-year-old in the mouth while shielding the ball.

In his post match interview French boss Zinedine Zidane, when asked if Doue had lost a tooth, replied: 'Yeah I think so. We’re going to take him straight there, a tooth has come loose I think, we’ll take care of him starting tomorrow.'

It was a poor day all round for the Frenchman who not only had his tooth dislodged but was also unable to impact a poor game for Les Blues in which the Azzuri beat them at the San Siro.

To compound Doue's misery it was the offender Frattesi who scored Italy's second goal in the 3-1 win just six minutes before he performed some unorthodox dentistry.

The Lazio midfielder's goal completed the Italians' comeback after going 1-0 down to a Michael Olise goal just ten minutes in before Inter Milan's Francesco Esposito levelled things up just 16 minutes later.

Desire Doue was left with a loose front tooth after receiving an elbow in the mouth from Davide Fratessi

Fratessi got away with just a yellow card as Italy beat the French 3-1 at the San Siro

Frattesi gave Italy the lead early on in the second half before Esposito netted his second from the spot to put the game out of reach.

Speaking after the game Zidane was surprisingly positive about his team's performance despite the loss, saying that losing is, 'part of the process.'

The French then went onto say: 'The most important thing is creating chances. Hitting the post had an impact, but I'm not disappointed.

'I would have been if we hadn't created good possession. What we need to analyse is why we weren't at the right level off the ball.'

The World Cup winner was not entirely positive however, later saying: 'in the second half we ran into difficulties, especially defensively.'

The victory put Italy level on points with the French in Group A with both sides having won three drawn one and lost one.

But France will have an opportunity to right their wrongs and return to the top of the group on Sunday as they welcome Turkey.

But if Italy beat Belgium on the same day the French will have to settle for second.

#2212. Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Presentata in Consiglio regionale l’iniziativa del 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare. Una giornata ad ingresso libero tra campioni, scuole, associazioni e cittadini.

Una giornata per riunire il movimento sportivo campano, valorizzarne i protagonisti e promuovere una pratica sportiva sempre più accessibile e inclusiva. È “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport 2026”, l’iniziativa promossa dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, presentata questa mattina nella Sala Caduti di Nassiriya del Consiglio regionale.

L’appuntamento è per domenica 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare, Padiglione 3B , con incontri, premiazioni, attività dimostrative e occasioni di confronto tra istituzioni, federazioni, associazioni, scuole, atleti e cittadini.

Alla conferenza stampa hanno partecipato l’assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta, il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi, Sandro Cuomo, campione olimpico nella spada a squadre ad Atlanta 1996, e l’atleta paralimpica Angela Procida.

“Vogliamo che sia una grande festa dello sport campano, un’occasione per dare spazio a ogni disciplina, a ogni talento e a ogni storia. Lo sport è passione, sacrificio e inclusione, una palestra di vita che unisce generazioni e crea comunità. Con questa iniziativa, unica nel suo genere in Campania, vogliamo riunire chi vive lo sport ogni giorno: dagli atleti alle associazioni, dalle scuole alle federazioni, fino alle famiglie. Il nostro obiettivo è costruire uno sport davvero accessibile, perché non deve essere un privilegio legato alle possibilità economiche, ma un diritto e un’opportunità per tutti”, ha detto l’ assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta.

”Gli Stati generali dello sport devono segnare l’avvio di una nuova stagione di impegno e di politiche concrete per la Campania. Lo sport non è un lusso né un privilegio, ma un diritto e un fondamentale strumento di crescita, salute, educazione e inclusione sociale. La nostra sfida è garantire a ogni cittadino, indipendentemente dal territorio in cui vive o dalle proprie condizioni economiche e personali, la possibilità di praticarlo. Servono investimenti negli impianti, sostegno alle associazioni e alle società sportive e una particolare attenzione ai giovani, alle persone con disabilità e alle fasce più fragili. Il Consiglio regionale è pronto a fare la propria parte: dobbiamo trasformare le tante energie del mondo sportivo in una visione comune e in azioni concrete. Perché investire nello sport significa investire nel futuro, nella coesione e nella qualità della vita della nostra comunità. La Campania ha bisogno di una politica dello sport che non lasci indietro nessuno”, ha aggiunto il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi.

Il programma del 22 novembre inizia alle 9.30 von l’accoglienza dei partecipanti; alle 10.30 apriranno gli stand delle Federazioni sportive campane. Alle 11 il convegno “Sport e scuola: educazione, inclusione e benessere” darà il via alla sessione mattutina, che proseguirà con le premiazioni di “Napoli Capitale Europea dello Sport” e i riconoscimenti alle scuole, alle associazioni e alle realtà che si sono distinte nella promozione della pratica sportiva.

Alle 18 si terrà il convegno “Presente e Futuro dello Sport in Campania”, seguito dalle premiazioni della Presidenza del Consiglio regionale e dai riconoscimenti ai grandi protagonisti dello sport, tra cui la campionessa olimpica di ginnastica artistica Manila Esposito, altri atleti e rappresentanti delle Federazioni sportive.

Per tutta la giornata sarà possibile visitare gli stand, conoscere e provare diverse discipline sportive e partecipare alle attività dimostrative. Alle 20 è previsto lo show cooking “La dieta dello sportivo”.

L’iniziativa è organizzata dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, con la partecipazione del CONI Campania, del Comitato Italiano Paralimpico (CIP), di Sport e Salute e di Padelness e Sport, con il supporto operativo di Sviluppo Campania. L’ingresso è libero .

#2211. Why England were unremarkable on Harry Kane's big night, the Ben White problem - and established star whose place could be under threat from Morgan Rogers, writes IAN LADYMAN

Published: 02:00 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 07:49 EST, 13 November 2026

England will head to Spain on Sunday for a Nations League shoot out with the World Champions. A win in Madrid could see Thomas Tuchel's team progress to the quarter-finals next March as group winners.

It wasn't always easy on Harry Kane's big night at Wembley against Croatia, though. Indeed this was probably England's worst performance since losing to Argentina in the World Cup semi-final last July.

Here Daily Mail Sport looks at the key issues emerging from last night's tight squeeze.

Self-defeating abuse of White is madness and must stop

In case nobody has noticed, England are not over stocked with competent right-backs.

Trent Alexander-Arnold isn't wholly trusted in big games by Thomas Tuchel, Reece James is always injured and Tino Livramento has also struggled to be available. Why, then, is a significant section of the England support so determined to drive Ben White away from the international scene?

White plays for Arsenal, the champions of England. He is a fine, versatile defender who would be gratefully received and appreciated by fans of most national teams in world football. He had issues with the previous England management regime and does not apologise for that.

Ben White was booed when he came on. The abuse towards the defender must stop

He spoke about it this week and made it clear he didn't wish to reopen old wounds. All that matters now is that he is willing and available. But – despite a plea from Tuchel before this game – England fans still booed him when he came on as a substitute last night.

It's a disgraceful way to treat a footballer who can be an asset to his country going forwards and it's something the England manager cares deeply about. Do we want to win the next Euros or not? Do we want our best players motivated and driven and part of the squad? Or do we want to faff about harbouring old grudges related to a situation in Qatar four winters ago that none of us really know that much about?

The choice is clear. If we are not careful, we will drive a good footballer away from the England set-up for good. It's madness.

Big night for Kane but questions for England

They came to celebrate Harry Kane and the England captain got his goal. Number 92 for the striker. Beneath all the fuss over England's talisman, however, this came perilously close to being a step backwards for Thomas Tuchel's England.

The England manager has been saying since the last international break in September and October that he wanted his team to go to Madrid on Sunday in with a shout at knocking Spain from the top of this Nations League Group. Had Croatia been a little smarter in front of goal at Wembley last night, England would have been heading for a dead rubber against the world champions.

Harry Kane scored on his big night as England edged out a 2-1 win over Croatia at Wembley

It was a night to remember for Kane as he was honoured after receiving his record 126th cap

Three times in the last ten minutes of this game, Croatia had good opportunities to score a second goal and take a point from the game that would have been perfectly justified. So England crawled over the line but there was little here that was impressive with questions emerging once again about how capable really are when they don't have the ball.

Kane put it well himself afterwards when he said: 'You can't always expect things to be perfect. Of course, we know we can improve on a few bits and we will need to if we want to win on Sunday.'

Tuchel himself will perhaps have quiet concerns. One of the German's worries about this England team is that they sometimes struggle to take training performances on to the field and this was another case in point.

The ultimate target for this team is to win the home Euros in 2028 and the pressure will need handling in front of their own supporters if they are to do that. With that in mind, it's good to know that the FA are interested in hosting the finals of this Nations League competition next summer.

If England make it through next March's two-legged quarter-finals, they could do with some practice of playing high-stakes games at Wembley.

Asked last week on the day he announced his squad whether Jordan Pickford was still his number one goalkeeper, Tuchel was unequivocal.

'He is,' said Tuchel. 'But the competition is on there as well. Like for the outfield players. And James Trafford will get regular starts now, that's what we wanted. They shared the matches last time. There's an opportunity that we maybe do this again.'

Morgan Rogers impressed after opening the scoring inside the opening half-an-hour

Jordan Pickford starting against Croatia could lead to James Trafford playing against Spain

With all this in mind, it was expected that Trafford would start this game with Pickford getting the gloves for the one that really matters in Spain on Sunday. That wasn't the case, so we wait with interest to see what the England manager does in Madrid.

What nobody really wants is for a return to the situation we had in the 1980s when Ron Greenwood used to rotate between the late Ray Clemence and last night's guest of honour Peter Shilton.

England's back four is the one area of this team that can look unconvincing – they did again last night - and the last thing they need is not to know who their goalkeeper is from one match to the next. Tuchel is going to have to make a decision.

Interesting that Tuchel chose to put Dan Burn into his team for this game, despite the Newcastle defender not being in the original squad.

With Marc Guehi and Ezri Konsa both absent with injuries, Everton's Jarrod Branthwaite would have expected to play on the left of Jarell Quansah here. Instead, Burn leapfrogged him in the queue and maybe we should have paid more attention to something Tuchel said the day before the game.

'Jarrad Branthwaite is doing very well but it's a very different style of play for him from Everton towards us,' said Tuchel.

Jarrad Branthwaite clearly has a way to go before he's fully trusted to start by Thomas Tuchel

'You can see that Dan Burn is straight away a big voice in training and is more used to the style with Newcastle from the last two seasons with Eddie Howe where they followed in a high press and to man-man. And of course he was with us at the World Cup so he is used a bit more to our style of play.'

That's all very well and logical but – that being the case – why was Branthwaite chosen ahead of Burn in the original group of 25 last Friday? Burn, as it happened, had a very good game last night.

Tuchel talks always about the value of players being in camp. 'The train is leaving the station,' is one of the England manager's more memorable quotes of recent times. Players like Cole Palmer, for example, have learned to their cost that missing meet-ups with injuries can be costly.

Bukayo Saka is not in that category and has plenty of credit in the bank with the England manager. However, Tuchel's fondness for Morgan Rogers is well known and Saka's absence does give him a way of getting the Chelsea player into his team. Rogers may be a number ten at heart but he was effective once again here out on the right side.

The 24-year-old was one of England's better players on a muted night against Croatia and he deserved his first half goal. He gives England a more direct, athletic dimension on the opposite flank to the more balletic offerings of Anthony Gordon. Food for thought for Tuchel at least as Saka continues his own battle against persistent injuries.

Bukayo Saka's long-term place in England's team could be under threat from Morgan Rogers

Newcastle United were pretty much an open door in the summer as they sold players left right and centre. Gordon to Barcelona to make sure the club was the right side of the financial guidelines and then Sandro Tonali to Tottenham and Bruno Guimaraes to Arsenal simply because the players made it known they wanted to go.

Strange, then, that Manchester United didn't make a proper move for left-back Lewis Hall. Admittedly this wasn't the most difficult match in which he will have ever played, but Hall was impressive once again.

With Luke Shaw so injury prone, Hall pretty much has the left-back berth sewn up for England but it feels as though he now needs to make the next step in his club career. United retain a nominal interest but who knows what incoming manager Mauricio Pochettino may think? It's hard to work out why United didn't try harder to sign him last summer. He has subsequently signed a new contract at Newcastle, of course.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2210. Napoli, De Laurentiis acquista i terreni per il nuovo centro sportivo del Napoli

(ANSA) - NAPOLI, 18 NOV - Aurelio De Laurentiis ha firmato in uno studio notarile il contratto di acquisto di un terreno a Qualiano, in provincia di Napoli, della superficie di 12,5 ettari, sul quale sarà costruito il centro sportivo della società Calcio Napoli. I lavori per realizzazione dell'opera cominceranno nel prossimo mese di gennaio. Il centro sportivo sarà costituito, inizialmente, da tre campi di calcio, spogliatoi, palestre, piscine, aree per i massaggi, un centro media e gli uffici della società. In un second momento sarà costruita anche una foresteria e un convitto per i giovani. De Laurentiis è anche in trattativa con i proprietari di altri terreni confinanti perché ha intenzione di ampliare ulteriormente l'area a disposizione, da destinare alla costruzione di altri campi da gioco per le formazioni giovanili, compreso un piccolo stadio da tremila posti a sedere per ospitare le partite della Primavera. La nuova cittadella del calcio sorgerà a una ventina di chilometri dal Centro Tecnico di Castel Volturno, a breve distanza dallo svincolo di Qualiano dell'Asse Mediano, la strada statale a scorrimento veloce che collega il Lago Patria ad Acerra, attraversando l'area a nord di Napoli. La durata dei lavori è di 12-14 mesi, trascorsi i quali ci sarà il trasferimento dal Centro Tecnico di Castel Volturno nel nuovo impianto. "Sicuramente - ha detto De Laurentiis - oggi è un nuovo inizio, perché abbiamo fatto una prima fase e adesso passiamo alla seconda fase.

Sperando che la salute ci accompagni!". "Non sapete mai - ha aggiunto il presidente del Napoli - la totalità di ciò che passa nella testa di un imprenditore? Un sogno? La sua casa il Napoli ce l'ha sempre avuta a Castel Volturno, dove abbiamo vinto due scudetti. Parlate sempre del Napoli come se l'avessi preso in Serie A, in tal caso avreste ragione. Voi avete visto dove si allenava Maradona?". De Laurentiis avrebbe anche l'intenzione, una volta realizzata l'opera, di creare anche un settore di calcio femminile. L'investimento complessivo è di circa 10 milioni di euro. (ANSA).

La situazione tra il Milan e il talento del Real Madrid Endrick non è ancora definita, ma una cosa è certa: i rossoneri sono interessati non poco al gioiello dei blancos. E deve essere a titolo definitivo: il Real Madrid non vuole un nuovo prestito e nemmeno Endrick vuole un nuovo prestito. Abbiamo parlato diverse volte negli ultimi giorni del fatto che un nuovo prestito è scartato da entrambe le parti. Oltre alla cifra intorno ai 70 milioni di euro per una cessione, il Real Madrid vuole anche inserire un'opzione di riacquisto intorno agli 80 milioni di euro. Ora resta da vedere se i colloqui embrionali passeranno a una proposta definitiva concreta".

#2206. Mauricio Pochettino is CONFIRMED as new Man United manager: Departing USA boss speaks out as he agrees to succeed Michael Carrick - but faces race against time for first game

Published: 07:02 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 07:51 EST, 13 November 2026

Manchester United have confirmed that Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become their new head coach on a two-and-a-half-year contract at Old Trafford.

Pochettino will replace Michael Carrick who was sacked following 10 months in charge on Friday, after United agreed to pay more than £10million to buy him and his backroom staff out of their contracts with the US Soccer Federation.

The 54-year-old Argentine will take charge of the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League double-header against Haiti in Miami on Saturday and Cincinnati on Wednesday before beginning his new job as United boss on Wednesday. His first game will be against Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday.

There were no comments from Pochettino or United officials in Friday’s press release confirming his appointment because he remains contracted to US Soccer.

Pochettino said in the USMNT's statement: 'The opportunity to represent the USA crest was one of the greatest honors of my career. We are so grateful to the players for their commitment, to the fans for their belief, and to the Federation for always supporting our staff and creating the opportunities to impact the culture of soccer in this great country.'

Pochettino is due to give a pre-match press conference at 2.15pm on Friday, but is not expected to speak about United until he arrives next week.

United sources say that Pochettino’s experience, philosophy, personality and skill-set make him well suited for the job, as well as his record of developing individual talent and building excellent team cultures.

Mauricio Pochettino has been appointed as the Manchester United manager

Michael Carrick was sacked after the club made their worst start to the season in 40 years

A statement from the club read: ‘Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become the Manchester United men’s head coach from 18 November.

‘The club has reached an agreement with the US Soccer Federation for Mauricio and his staff to be released after the conclusion of their responsibilities with the US men’s national team.

‘The Argentinian is renowned for his high-intensity, attacking style of football, his dedication to building strong, cohesive teams and his outstanding record of developing individual players.

‘Pochettino has consistently performed at the highest level of football across multiple leagues. He began his coaching career with Espanyol in 2009 before joining Southampton and achieving a club-record Premier League points tally in 2014.

‘During five full seasons at Tottenham Hotspur, Pochettino established the club as regular UEFA Champions League contenders, securing qualification in four consecutive seasons and leading the club to its first final in the competition in 2019.

‘In 2021, he joined Paris Saint-Germain, where he won the Ligue 1 title, the Coupe de France and the Trophée des Champions before joining Chelsea in 2023. Most recently he led the U.S. Men’s National Team to the Round of 16 at the FIFA World Cup.’

Earlier in the week, the club's chief executive Omar Berrada and Wilcox flew back from Monaco, where minority owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe ratified their decision to sack Carrick with the blessing of co-chairmen Joel and Avram Glazer.

Carrick arrived at United's training ground at 7.50am on Thursday morning and was dismissed.

He left at 10.18am, less than two and a half hours later. He became the first Premier League manager to be sacked this season.

United's statement read: 'After a disappointing start to the season, the club has come to the extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary in order to achieve our ambitions.

'Michael is, and always will be, a Manchester United legend. Just as he did as a player, he has conducted himself with the highest standards of dedication and professionalism throughout his time back with the club.

'His leadership and achievements last season will always be fondly remembered.'

Sources close to United say the club's leadership believe their squad of players is far better than the performances and results under Carrick indicated.

United feel that the squad are far better than their position in the Premier League shows

They gave the under-fire manager a chance to turn things around against Chelsea, Roma and Aston Villa but ultimately felt the team were not on the right path.

It has been a campaign in which United won just three matches, one of which came against Azerbaijani minnows FK Sabah, and they are yet to keep a clean sheet in the league.

Pochettino's United travel to Anfield on November 22 before a Champions League clash at Sporting Lisbon, Pochettino's first home game against Brentford and then away match at Newcastle.

#2205. Frattesi si allena con la Lazio dopo gli esami: può esserci col Lecce

La risonanza ha evidenziato un affaticamento, senza lesioni. Rovella e Gudmundsson hanno lavorato con il gruppo.

Davide Frattesi è tornato ad allenarsi regolarmente con il gruppo della Lazio dopo il problema ai flessori che lo aveva costretto a lasciare in anticipo il ritiro della Nazionale. Gli esami hanno escluso lesioni muscolari e, salvo imprevisti nelle prossime sedute, il centrocampista sarà a disposizione di Gennaro Gattuso per la gara contro il Lecce.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il classe ’99, ex Inter, aveva lasciato Coverciano nei giorni scorsi per un fastidio accusato durante gli impegni con l’Italia. Aveva così saltato la sfida di Bruxelles contro il Belgio, mentre alla Lazio cresceva la preoccupazione per le sue condizioni.

Gli accertamenti strumentali e la risonanza magnetica effettuati nelle ultime ore hanno evidenziato un affaticamento, senza lesioni muscolari. Nella seduta odierna Frattesi ha lavorato con i compagni, mettendosi a disposizione dello staff tecnico dopo il rientro anticipato dal ritiro azzurro.

Se non arriveranno ricadute o altri imprevisti durante la preparazione, il centrocampista sarà convocabile per l’anticipo della 12ª giornata di Serie A contro il Lecce. La partita è in programma sabato 21 novembre alle 15:00 allo Stadio Olimpico.

Il recupero di Frattesi conta per la Lazio anche alla luce del suo rendimento recente. La mezzala è andata a segno nell’ultimo turno di campionato sul campo del Napoli. I suoi inserimenti e la sua freschezza atletica sono risorse per affrontare la difesa del Lecce alla ripresa del torneo.

La seduta ha dato a Gattuso altre indicazioni in vista della sfida con i salentini. Nicolò Rovella, Danilo Cataldi, Albert Gudmundsson, Tijjani Noslin e Kenneth Taylor hanno lavorato regolarmente in gruppo. Il loro coinvolgimento offre al tecnico più soluzioni a centrocampo e nel reparto offensivo.

In particolare, la presenza di Rovella e Gudmundsson amplia le possibilità di rotazione dell’allenatore in vista dei prossimi impegni ravvicinati. Anche per loro, dunque, l’allenamento con i compagni è un passaggio nella preparazione della gara contro il Lecce.

Repubblica analizza questo fenomeno: "Arrivano le nazionali, aumentano gli infortuni. Ormai è una tendenza consolidata nel tempo. #2201. Michael Carrick speaks out on his Man United sacking for the first time - and admits 'we've not had the results lately' - as Mauricio Pochettino replaces him

Published: 07:48 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 08:08 EST, 13 November 2026

Michael Carrick has released his first public statement on his Manchester United sacking, calling the job an 'immense privilege' in a classy 411-word tribute to the club's supporters.

The statement, released a little over 24 hours after his dismissal was confirmed, read: 'Dear United Fans, I've had the immense privilege of leading Manchester United, the football club that I love.

'There are very few who get to live that and feel it, and for that I'm incredibly grateful.

'With that comes huge responsibility, and to be at Manchester United, you need to understand exactly what that means. It's different to anywhere else.

'Ultimately, you need to push to be the best, but there is a culture, there are values and there are behaviours that you have to live by and respect, which have been built over a long period of time.

'It's a football club that is unique, with substance and history that cannot be ignored. This is what inspired me every single day. I want to say a big thank you to the supporters. I must say, you are a special, rare breed.

Michael Carrick has broken his silence on his sacking by Manchester United

'We've had some great times this year and moments we'll all remember. The best thing about being in my position was seeing so many of you lose yourselves in the emotion of those moments, leaving the stadium desperate to come back again next time.

'I just wanted to give you something back. That love never dies. I know we've not had the results lately, but you've shown me nothing but love and support in times when the easiest thing, and what most end up doing, would be to turn the other way.

'That means more than a win or two. That's what makes you who you are. That's why I fell in love with this club all those years ago.

'A special thank you to my coaching staff. You gave me everything you had, every single day, and I couldn't have asked for any more. I'm incredibly grateful for your loyalty, hard work and support throughout.

'To all the staff behind the scenes, thank you for everything you do for this football club. So much of your work goes unseen, but it never goes unappreciated.

'And to the players, thank you for your efforts and the moments we've shared. I wish you all the very best for what's ahead.

'I want nothing but success for Manchester United. This club has been such a big part of my life and that will never change. I'll always be proud to have played my part and I'll always want the very best for it.

'I'll be right there with you from now on, watching from the sidelines, cheering the lads on. Michael.'

The statement follows his former team-mate Gary Neville suggesting that Carrick had grounds to sue United for their 'disgusting' treatment of the United legend.

Carrick was left in the lurch by the club's hierarchy - but continued to attend training on Tuesday and Wednesday – amid widespread reports that he would imminently be relieved of his duties.

The classy statement follows Carrick former team-mate Gary Neville suggesting that he had grounds to sue United for their 'disgusting' treatment of his dismissal

Neville told Sky Sports News: 'In the last 24-48 hours it's been spectacularly bad to be in Monaco as an executive and an owner whilst your manager, who's a club legend, is taking training on the pitch and simultaneously approaching the US Soccer Federation to approach Mauricio Pochettino.

'It's just quite simply disgusting. That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you'd be getting sued.

'The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it's just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership.

'I don't think anybody comes out of this well at all.'

Less than an hour prior to Carrick's statement United confirmed his replacement, Pochettino, will take the reins after agreeing a two-and-a-half-year contract at Old Trafford.

United have agreed to pay more than £10million to buy Pochettino and his backroom staff out of their contracts with the US Soccer Federation.

The 54-year-old Argentine will take charge of the US’s CONCACAF Nations League double-header against Haiti in Miami on Saturday and Cincinnati on Wednesday before beginning his new job as United boss on Wednesday.

His first game will be against Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday.

Pochettino is due to give a pre-match press conference at 2.15pm on Friday but is not expected to speak about United until he arrives next week due to his remaining contracted to US Soccer.

United have agreed to pay more than £10million to buy Pochettino and his backroom staff out of their contracts with the US Soccer Federation

A statement from the club read: ‘Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become the Manchester United men’s head coach from 18 November.

‘The club has reached an agreement with the US Soccer Federation for Mauricio and his staff to be released after the conclusion of their responsibilities with the US men’s national team.

‘The Argentinian is renowned for his high-intensity, attacking style of football, his dedication to building strong, cohesive teams and his outstanding record of developing individual players.

‘Pochettino has consistently performed at the highest level of football across multiple leagues. He began his coaching career with Espanyol in 2009 before joining Southampton and achieving a club-record Premier League points tally in 2014.

‘During five full seasons at Tottenham Hotspur, Pochettino established the club as regular UEFA Champions League contenders, securing qualification in four consecutive seasons and leading the club to its first final in the competition in 2019.

‘In 2021, he joined Paris Saint-Germain, where he won the Ligue 1 title, the Coupe de France and the Trophée des Champions before joining Chelsea in 2023. Most recently he led the U.S. Men’s National Team to the Round of 16 at the FIFA World Cup.’

#2197. 'I was one day away from falling into a coma': JAKE O'BRIEN, the Premier League's only diabetic player, on his brush with death, managing his condition in the dressing room - and why he was 'never close' to leaving Everton last summer

Published: 21:00 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 08:25 EST, 13 November 2026

There are few sights more incendiary to the modern manager than a footballer reaching for his phone as soon as he walks through the dressing room door. So you can imagine the reaction Jake O’Brien received when he pulled out his mobile just before one half-time team talk.

But the Everton and Republic of Ireland defender had a good excuse: he’s the only Type One diabetic playing in the Premier League.

O’Brien’s body is unable to create insulin, the hormone that regulates the amount of glucose in your blood.

Loaded on his mobile is an app linked via Bluetooth to a circular device on his arm which gives a reading of his blood sugar levels. It is then that he takes out a needle and injects the necessary insulin dose into his thigh or stomach.

‘There was one guy who didn’t react too well. Mentioning no names! He was saying: "What are you doing on your phone?"' O’Brien recalls. ‘But he soon realised what it was for.’

It can also come as a shock to the uninitiated among his team-mates.

‘One or two are taken aback at first,’ he says. ‘It can be a bit confusing. “What’s he doing?’’ Or maybe those that are a little squeamish, but they soon get used to it. It's only a small needle.'

Everton and Ireland defender Jake O'Brien is the only diabetic footballer in the Premier League

O'Brien (pictured attending an awareness workshop put on by Everton) revels in the role of being a diabetes ambassador

O'Brien (pictured with his partner Sophie) says some of his team-mates are 'taken aback' when he injects himself with insulin (Source: Instagram/Obrienjake_)

Tracking blood glucose levels is now an everyday part of O'Brien's life but it is a life that was unwittingly put in jeopardy prior to his diagnosis at 16.

O’Brien was later told he had been just one day away from slipping into a coma when his concerned parents ushered him to the doctors near the family home in Youghal, County Cork, for a check-up.

Their sports-mad son, who had won national titles at boxing and hurling, was tired, losing weight and had an insatiable thirst despite drinking eight to nine litres of water a day. He had lost seven kilograms in just over a week. A blood test revealed his blood sugar was ten times what was considered stable and he was rushed to hospital.

‘I was told I was a day away from a coma,' says O'Brien. 'If I had gone to sleep that night without having gone to the doctor, who knows?’

He went into hospital that night weighing 53kg, or just over eight stone. Two weeks post-diagnosis he was back up to 60kg, and within two years he had put on a whopping 30kg.

'Yeah, 30kg,' laughs O'Brien. 'I know it's not normal. It must have been some grocery bill for Mum, but I was motivated to put on the weight, going to the gym all the time.

‘I was a bit naive about it all,' he deadpans. ‘I never expected to have anything but once I knew what diabetes was, I didn’t wallow in it, I took it as a positive. Everything just clicked for me physically. I just thought, "Right, now I know what’s wrong, I can get on with my life again".

‘It was a pain going in and out of hospital, but I just took it as education and wanted to get my sport back on track.

'My family are... I wouldn't say cold, but matter of fact. Supportive, obviously, but they are all sporty too, so it was just, "Get on with it".'

O'Brien's life in the Premier League would have seemed inconceivable when he fell down to 53kg as a teenager and was hospitalised

He is now an imposing 6ft 6in tall, weighing around 96kg. His physique offers no outward indication of the daily challenge he is dealing with

Today, relaxing in the cafe at Everton’s Finch Farm training complex, O’Brien is an imposing 6ft 6in tall, weighing around 96kg. His physique offers no outward indication of the daily challenge he is dealing with.

There’s a Cork-lilt to his softly spoken Irish tone. He’s polite and engaging and it’s clear he revels in the role of being a diabetes ambassador, spreading the message that diagnosis doesn’t have to end your dreams.

Such has been his impact, he has even attracted support from fans of the other club in the city.

One, six-year-old Liverpool fan Louis, reached out via Instagram to ask O’Brien how he kept up his glucose levels during a game and whether he suffered any ‘hypos’ - the expression for hypoglycemic episodes, when the blood sugar drops too low and leaves you faint or dizzy.

Louis, whose siblings Paisley and Evie also have Type One diabetes, followed up by challenging O’Brien to a penalty shootout. If the Everton man lost, he faced a forfeit, he would have to shout ‘Up the Reds’ on social media.

‘You play for the wrong team but you're still a Type One hero, and Mum says you're proof that diabetes doesn't stop you from playing at the top level,’ posted Louis.

Touched by the message, O’Brien asked Everton to invite the youngster and his family to training for a kickabout, although ultimately, we are assured they reached a compromise on the penalties forfeit.

‘I’ve struck up a rapport with some kids and groups here and in Ireland,' says O'Brien. 'They often have the same story as mine of not being sure what the future holds.

‘There’s a big misconception about diabetes over what it is and the limitations it brings.

‘Just because you have diabetes doesn’t mean your dreams are over.’

‘Adrenaline is higher on match days so that can change how you feel. I may inject four times before a night game and a bit more at half-time'

‘Just because you have diabetes doesn’t mean your dreams are over,’ says O'Brien, who also excelled at boxing and hurling as a boy (Source: TikTok/Everton)

The centre back credits boxing for helping discipline with his diet but underlines ‘no food is off limits, it’s just about applying common sense'.

A typical day sees him eat eggs, salmon and dates for breakfast to match his insulin doses to the carbohydrates he’s taking on around 9.30am before training. Afterwards he’ll have a light lunch at 2pm then take something back from the training ground for a light evening meal.

‘I just have to watch carbohydrates. I’ll eat more of them on match day but then work out my insulin ratio to the amount I eat and whether I need to inject more.

‘Adrenaline is higher on match days so that can change how you feel, I may inject four times before a night game and inject a bit more at half-time. If it’s not quite right you may get a bit heavy-legged or if I get a few cramps I may need a gel. But I've had no dangerous episodes.'

When World Diabetes Day dawns on Saturday, O’Brien will be fully focused on preparing to play for Ireland in Austria in the Nations League.

It will give him a chance to revert to his favoured centre back position having been asked to fill in predominantly at right back under David Moyes.

He acknowledges it's not his preferred role but dismisses rumours that he wanted to leave as a result in the summer.

'There was nothing in that,' O'Brien affirms. ‘I was never close to leaving.

'People know I'm not a natural right back but I'll play wherever I need to, to help the team. We're only a small squad so it’s important everyone chips in.

‘There’s a lot of different right backs. I see Arsenal have centre backs playing at right back so I can take pointers from them but then again, I am 6ft 6in so I’m not the average shape.’

O'Brien insists there was 'nothing in' reports that he wanted to leave Everton last summer to get more minutes at his preferred position of centre back

At least O’Brien has former Everton captain Seamus Coleman on hand for advice.

‘Seamus is a good friend. He still gives me pointers of where to be, positioning wise and seeing the game differently.'

Ireland assistant John O'Shea, a versatile defender himself in his Manchester United days, is another mentor. O’Shea and Ireland boss Heimir Hallgrímsson were at Hill Dickinson Stadium to watch O'Brien play his part in Everton's recent Carabao Cup win over Newcastle.

'Heimir is a good guy,' says O'Brien. 'Very understanding. It's always a nice change of scenery to link up with Ireland. We've got a difficult couple of games (Austria and Kosovo) but hopefully if we get two wins it’s within reach.'

Indeed, if there's a mantra that personifies O'Brien's outlook on life, no matter what knocks you take, it is: always believe you can still achieve.

'Don’t be afraid and don’t let anything hold you back, that's what I say.'

#2192. Harry Kane reads letters from his kids: England captain close to tears as his four children write to him after he broke the Three Lions appearance record

Published: 05:47 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 11:12 EST, 13 November 2026

Harry Kane had to compose himself and hold back the tears while reading beautiful letters from his children after breaking the England appearance record.

The 33-year-old earned his 126th cap and scored in the 2-1 win over Croatia on Thursday night, with the Wembley crowd celebrating his achievement and Ballon d'Or triumph.

And on the following morning, there was a surprise in store for the England skipper, who was visibly taken aback on camera when presented with letters from his four children, nine-year-old Ivy, eight-year-old Vivi, five-year-old Louis and three-year-old Henry, who he shares with wife Kate.

'Wow, that’s uh a bit of a surprise,' Kane said as he examined the letters and artwork. 'I think you guys are trying to make me cry on camera.'

He opened the first one before reading aloud: 'To Daddy, I love you very much and I want you to know that I’m very proud of you and also you are the best footballer in the world, the most kind Daddy in the world and the best striker in the world.

'I would like to celebrate you playing the most games for England by holiday together and eating ice cream.

Harry Kane was on the verge of tears as he read touching letters from his four children

'My favorite thing about you is you never give up and I love all things you do for us. Love Ivy.'

Dewy-eyed Kane then reflected: 'I wasn’t expecting this beautiful letter. Thank you Ivy. I love you. I've got one here from Vivian as well.'

'Dear Daddy, I’m so happy and proud of you. I love you because you are hard working and make the best fried eggs. I want to celebrate with you by going fishing, going down the water slides and having a cinema trip as well. Love from Vivi.'

Kane then said: 'Yeah, you’ve caught me by surprise here to be honest with you. I’m trying to just uh, stay strong for the camera. Thank you Vivi, love you.'

Then came the letters from Kane's two sons, Louis and Henry.

Louis' read: 'To Daddy, I love you so much because you train and work so hard. My favorite thing we do together is play football and cricket together. I love when you play England football because you score goals and we get to sing Vindaloo. I want to celebrate with you by making an ice cream house with sweets inside. Love Louis.'

'Thank you Louie, love you, boy. This last one is from Henry,' Kane said.

The short and sweet note from his youngest went: 'Daddy, you are the winner. Vindaloo! Love Henry.'

Kane walked onto the pitch with his children, (L-R) Henry, Ivy, Vivi, Louis on Thursday night

Kane showed the artwork and hand-written letters to the camera as he read them

Kane then pointed the endearing scribble to the camera, saying: 'He’s drawn me a lovely picture there. Love you, Henry. Thank you, beautiful messages from my wonderful family.'

Kane, who lives with his family in Bavaria and recently signed a new deal worth £400,000-a-week at Bayern Munich, brought the whole clan, including his parents, to Wembley for his big night on Thursday.

He walked out onto the hallowed turf with his four children, admired a gigantic tifo of himself in the crowd, posed for pictures with the gleaming Ballon d'Or trophy and was given a commemorative shirt by Prince William.

Kane is a devoted family man in addition to being the world's best footballer and took a moment to say a few words about his children at the Ballon d'Or ceremony last month.

He said: 'To Ivy, Vivienne, Louis and Henry. You're the reason I do this.

Prince William presented Kane with a commemorative framed shirt with 126 on the back

'I don't think you'll fully maybe appreciate bringing this trophy home. I'll bring a few others home, but this one is a special one, so I can't wait to see you and celebrate with you.

'I want to set a good example being a father but also being a professional and what it means to work hard, what it means to believe in yourself. I love you very much.'

Kane passed Peter Shilton as the record appearance-maker for England on Thursday and took his tally of goals for the national team to 92.

His penalty put England 2-1 up and they would not relinquish their lead. He'll next be in action against Spain on Sunday.

#2191. Lazio, mercato di gennaio: Lotito lavora da due mesi per il saldo zero

Gli agenti hanno sondato i big: il Brentford ha chiesto Isaksen e la Juventus è pronta a offrire 40 milioni per Taylor.

Per la Lazio resta aperta la possibilità di operare nel mercato di gennaio a saldo zero, anziché subire un nuovo blocco totale. Lotito lavora da due mesi per trovare una soluzione. La decisione arriverà a dicembre dalla Commissione di controllo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

A settembre il blocco invernale sembrava inevitabile, dopo gli acquisti estivi e la crescita del monte ingaggi. Ora nei conti biancocelesti si intravede uno spiraglio, seppure stretto. Lotito procede senza annunci, tra trattative, incastri e verifiche dei conti; a Formello restano prudenza e speranza.

La Commissione di controllo, che ieri ha firmato un protocollo d’intesa con la Consob, darà la risposta definitiva a dicembre. Fino a quel momento, l’ipotesi del saldo zero rimane aperta, ma non è una certezza.

Nel frattempo è pronto un piano alternativo. Gli agenti hanno sondato il mercato, raccolto offerte e valutato i big della Lazio. La linea del club, però, è quella promessa a Gattuso: nessuna cessione a gennaio.

Il Brentford ha chiesto Isaksen. Anche la Juventus potrebbe scontrarsi con la stessa posizione: è pronta a mettere sul tavolo 40 milioni per Taylor. La linea sulle cessioni è riassunta così: «In uscita non si muove nessuno». Almeno per ora, in attesa della decisione prevista prima di Natale.

#2188. Fighter who ambushed UFC star in bloody hotel brawl is punished: Abu Dhabi court dishes out six-month prison sentences and fines - including £61k for filming shocking attack

Published: 09:58 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 11:57 EST, 13 November 2026

Usman Nurmagomedov and his entourage have been swiftly punished with prison sentences and fines in Abu Dhabi after their shocking hotel attack.

Nurmagomedov set upon UFC star Merab Dvalishvili on Wednesday, slapping him in the lobby of Yas Hilton hotel in a revenge mission filmed by his friends.

The Georgian-American Dvalishvili then retaliated and fought back with his cameraman as the melee rapidly descended into complete mayhem. Dvalishvili was left bloodied and bruised and the attackers were quickly detained by the authorities.

In just two days, their punishments have been decided by the Abu Dhabi Judicial Department, who published the findings on social media.

Six of the attackers were given six-month prison sentences and ordered to pay £61,000 each in fines. They will also be immediately deported  when their sentences end.

They were described as having 'caused panic, disturbed public security and  endangered lives'.

Two of Nurmagomedov's associates were fined £10,000 each for 'disturbing public security and assaulting others'.

Usman Nurmagomedov (left) was the one who initiated the brawl by slapping Merab Dvalishvili - his brother Umar (right) lost a title shot against the Georgian last year

The Abu Dhabi court shared this post online confirming the punishments for the attackers

And the other two members of the group who recorded the assault were given six-month sentences, £61,000 fines and confiscation of the devices used.

Dvalishvili had been due to fight in a RAF wrestling event on Saturday. CEO and co-founder Chad Bronstein released a statement confirming the fighter is back.

He said: 'I am pleased to share that Merab Dvalishvili is back with us in Abu Dhabi. We would like to express our sincere appreciation to the relevant authorities in Abu Dhabi for their professionalism and support.

'We remain focused on moving forward and are pleased to have Merab back with us.'

Nurmagomedov and his team's violent ambush was four months in the making.

Back in July, Dvalishvili was competing in a RAF event in Georgia, which is a freestyle wrestling show the UFC allow their fighters to compete in outside of the day job.

While milling around at the event, a Dagestani wrestler and friend of Umar, Ahmed Gadzhimagomedov, confronted Dvalishvili about refusing to entertain a rematch of any kind with Umar.

UFC star Merab Dvalishvili was attacked in a five-star hotel in Abu Dhabi on Wednesday

'I was super relaxed, super chilled and then somebody (Gadzhimagomedov) came and grabbed my neck and pulled me over and started yelling at me in Russian language, which I hardly understand,' Dvalishvili told Ariel Helwani.

'And he mentioned Umar. It took me a couple of seconds to realize, "Who is this guy to tell me what to talk about him?"

'It’s between me and Umar… I have so much respect for all Dagestani people, especially fighters. The only guy who I don't like from Dagestan is Umar and his sneaky manager, Rizvan. The guy who was helping Umar disrespect me on X and Threads.'

After Gadzhimagomedov squared up to him, Dvalishvili shoved him in the neck, shouting: 'What's your problem, b***h!'

The situation, unlike the hotel brawl that escalated into a complete bloodbath, was quickly defused and there was a child in close proximity.

Usman wanted to take revenge on behalf of fellow Dagestani Gadzhimagomedov and also his brother Umar, who lost against Dvalishvili in a title shot last year.

In the footage of the hotel attack, the group appeared to approach Dvalishvili, 35, from behind, before one man said a couple of words to him and slapped him in the face.

Dvalishvili was rocked, then kicked, and was held back by a man he appeared to be with.

He tried to fight back but a number of men overwhelmed him as his companion was attacked too. Eventually, the groups were separated, and Dvalishvili took to social media to respond to the incident.

The 35-year-old was blind sided while carrying out an interview

He said in a video, bloody and bruised as he received treatment: 'What a sneaky way guys. Just two of us, me and my friend, came here in Abu Dhabi and Usman and his friends came like snakes from behind. What kind of fighters are you guys? What kind of man does this? If you want to fight, let's do it the real way.

'You guys came when there was two of us, you were prepared. What the f***.'

His companion then added: 'From behind? You call yourselves men? You're a little b***h.'

Dvalishvili continued: 'You are sneaky guys. You are not real men. It shows your guys' character, what the f*** guys? If you want to fight, let's fight. We are fighters. I talk straight, but you guys came from behind. I don't know how many guys, all your friends waiting for us in a sneaky way, wow.

'I knew guys, I knew what kind of guys you were. Usman, Umar and his friends came sneaky from behind, so many guys. Wow. I am fine guys. All my friends and family, I am f***ing fine. I am a fighter. This is nothing for me, but it shows what kind of people you guys are. P***ys do this bro. We are warriors.'

#2186. Ronaldinho, la nostalgia vigliacca e il richiamo irresistibile del pallone

Ho seguito tutta la vicenda. Non perché dovessi documentare il “grande ritorno” del Dentuco in capo ma perché ero, sono e resterò sempre un fan del brasiliano. A 46 anni, tanti suoi colleghi dal passato prestigioso si godono la pensione calcistica tra un evento e l’altro. C’è chi gioca a padel e chi sorseggia Montenegro con ghiaccio… Ronaldinho , invece, ha deciso di rimettersi maglietta, pantaloncini, parastinchi, calze e scarpe per tornare su quel rettangolo verde in cui ha incantato per anni.

Ovvio, parliamo di un’operazione di marketing eccezionale (anche se le cifre non sono confermate, l'ingaggio del brasiliano pari si aggiri attorno ai 500.000 euro, più bonus vari). Per giorni non si è parlato d’altro e, il Ravenna , di colpo, è finito sulla bocca di tutti. Tra scettici e nostalgici, il ritorno dell’ex Milan è stato un successo clamoroso. Tutti si sono interessati all’evento, confermando come, in questa era calcistica avara di fuoriclasse (almeno nel nostro calcio), la nostalgia sia davvero canaglia. Ci piace rivedere gli eroi che abbiamo ammirato durante il loro prime tornare a brillare o, almeno, provarci. Penso a Mike Tyson, uno che attira le folle anche da anziano. Anche lui è un mito nostalgico che continua a far presa su tutto e tutti. Lindsey Vonn è tornata dopo un lungo periodo di stop... Beh, pure Serena Williams, no? Pure una leggenda come Jordan, quando è tornata sul parquet, ha infiammato il cuore di ogni suo fan, nonostante non fosse più l’MJ in grado di volare…

Tra ritorni reali e comparsate, questi campionissimi hanno voluto sfidare la carta d'identità, anche perchè invogliati da ragioni economiche piuttosto tangibili. Tuttavia, c’è dell’altro. Ronaldinho, in campo, si è divertito. Esatto, si è divertito. Certo, la motivazione economica aiuta ma, per uno come lui, innamorato pazzo del pallone, poter tornare a giocare una partita professionistica è stato speciale. Chiaro, non è sceso al Camp Nou o a San Siro ma, alla fine, sempre di un campo di calcio stiamo parlando. Non appena gli è riuscito qualcosa di buono, ecco che il suo viso si è illuminato a giorno. Sapete perché? Perché, anche a 46 anni, si può tornare a divertirsi come quando si era bambini, ovvero rincorrendo quel benedetto pallone. Non so se tornerà nuovamente in campo ma, alla fine, che importa. Rispetto chi ha parlato di buffonata ma, personalmente, mi sono emozionato quando ho rivisto il Dentuco muoversi, seppur al rallentatore, come quando dominava quel terreno verde che non riesce proprio a dimenticare. Nostalgia vigliacca, non ci abbandona mai…

Ecco, ora l’importante è che non tutti si sentano in diritto e dovere di tornare. Ronaldinho fa parte del gruppo degli eletti, ovvero di coloro che hanno insegnato calcio. Lui poteva permettersi questo ritorno al passato. Lui e pochi altri…

#2181. 19 novembre 2005, la gara perfetta di Ronaldinho. Che esce dal Bernabeu fra gli applausi

Il 19 novembre del 2005, Ronaldinho è senza pietà, ma con una bellezza estrema. Nulla a che vedere con i 45 minuti del brasiliano contro il Forlì, ovviamente, ma ventun anni dopo era difficile anche chiedergli una prestazione del genere. La sfida è il Clasico fra Real Madrid e Barcellona, dove Dinho diventa leggenda ancor più di quanto era precedentemente. Poi vincerà addirittura la Champions League, qualche mese dopo, nella finale contro l'Arsenal di Arsene Wenger.

Da una parte ci sono i Galacticos: Beckham, Zidane, Raúl, Robinho e Ronaldo tutti in campo. Nessuno regge il confronto con l'extraterrestre con la maglia numero dieci e che gioca dall'altra parte. Eto'o, ex di giornata, va a segno di punta dopo quindici minuti, sigillando un'azione di Lionel Messi - altissima qualità nell'attacco del Barça - e firmando l'uno a zero. In panchina c'è Frank Rijkaard, dall'altra il brasiliano Vanderlei Luxemburgo. Nel 2005 Dinho vincerà anche il Pallone d'Oro e probabilmente anche la serata del Bernabeu ha messo un mattone nel muro.

All'ora di gioco il Gaucho prende palla sulla trequarti, salta Sergio Ramos come se fosse un birillo, poi salta anche Helguera e spedisce sul primo palo appena prima dell'intervento di Roberto Carlos, due a zero. Un quarto d'ora dopo la stessa cosa, Sergio Ramos umiliato da Ronaldinho e poi piattone verso il secondo palo. Casillas non può niente, il labiale lo tradisce: “Yo flipo”, cioè “Allucinante”. Il Bernabeu si alza in piedi e gli concede un lunghissimo applauso.

#2180. Lazio, Lotito e il mercato: le mosse segrete

L'accordo tecnico c'è, ma Mizuno può pareggiare l'offerta danese, superiore ai cinque milioni a stagione.

Lotito ha ottenuto una proroga per dilazionare le vecchie imposte degli anni del Covid, che pesavano per molti milioni sulla liquidità della Lazio. Secondo Il Messaggero, il presidente prova ora ad anticipare i crediti futuri degli sponsor e a inserirli nella documentazione per la Commissione, nel tentativo di trovare risorse per il mercato.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La dilazione delle imposte alleggerisce un peso sulla liquidità del club. Il passo successivo a cui lavora Lotito riguarda i crediti legati alle future sponsorizzazioni: anticiparli potrebbe trasformarli in risorse da presentare alla Commissione.

Il presidente ha cercato diverse soluzioni, ma non ha certezze e non vuole alimentare aspettative. Non ne ha parlato neppure con Gattuso, per evitare di illudere il tecnico.

Per Hummel il discorso è distinto da quello dei crediti degli sponsor. L'accordo tecnico c'è, ma occorre attendere che Mizuno rinunci alla prelazione che le consentirebbe di pareggiare l'offerta danese, superiore ai cinque milioni a stagione.

La situazione resta quindi legata alla scelta di Mizuno. Lotito, intanto, mantiene il silenzio anche su questo fronte.

#2178. Former Old Firm rivals reveal how clubs walked a fine line between rivalry and respect...and even stopped by for tea and sympathy in times of trouble!

Published: 12:20 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 12:29 EST, 13 November 2026

They have reached an age when they would once have been categorised as the old infirm. Yet in the autumn of 2026 they cavort around Toryglen with an almost juvenile vigour.

They have reached the age when they can reflect on being stalwarts of the Old Firm. Yet they are outlining plans and strategy for a vital, growing movement.

The two meetings are separated by two sets of stairs. On the ground level, men of pensionable age and beyond play walking football. Just above sit Campbell Ogilvie, former secretary of Rangers and president of the Scottish FA, and Eric Riley, once company secretary and director of Celtic.

They are both on the board of Walking Football Scotland, an organisation that now has more than 5,000 people playing the game across 160 clubs, with participants ranging from 40 to 93.

The venerable statesmen of the Scottish game may be perceived to be rivals, but only in matters on the pitch. They sit in an office in the south side of Glasgow and reminisce about how they first met 50 years ago when playing five-a-sides and then renewed that rivalry in their professional life.

As the Glasgow rivals now seem regularly at odds, both men recalled a time when friendship was paramount amid the sporting mayhem.

Former Rangers secretary Campbell Ogilvie and ex-Celtic director Eric Riley are firm friends

‘The two clubs always had a good relationship back in those days,’ said Ogilvie, now 76 and still playing football. ‘We wanted to beat each other — naturally. But off the field we had a very close relationship.’

He told a remarkable story that backs that assertion.

‘There was one day I was at Ibrox in the early 1980s when the team was not doing so well. The phone rang and it was Sean Fallon, Jock Stein’s former Celtic assistant. I had known Sean from my days with the Scottish League and he said: “I know you’re going through a rocky spell, so I’m coming over for a cup of tea”.

‘Over he comes. So there was a very close relationship in those days. I’m not there now and for all I know there still is. But certainly, when I was there and Eric was at Celtic, we could talk to each other.’

Riley, 69, agreed: ‘Absolutely. John McClelland (former Rangers chairman) was one we could talk to. And Martin (Bain, former chief executive) I suppose, when he came along a bit later. Between myself and Peter Lawwell (former Celtic chief executive and chairman) the relationship between the clubs off the field was actually quite strong. But on the field we still wanted to win.’

Ogilvie added: ‘There was a respect. And I go back to the Willie Waddells and the Willie Thorntons, there was that respect. More recently that was there with the likes of Jack McGinn and so on.

‘We had a good relationship but it’s fair to say that there was no social media in those days. You know all the toxic stuff that can bring.’

The traits of respect and sporting competitiveness have been obvious in both men since they first met half a century ago. ‘We played five-a-side football at Scotstoun before the football careers started. It was just casual games,’ said Ogilvie. ‘We were in our early 20s. Eric was an accountant and I think those games were arranged through accountants.

‘I played youth football when I was 16 and, 60 years down the line, playing football is still a major part of my life.’

Ogilvie began walking football eight years ago and Riley soon after. ‘I suffer from Parkinson’s, amongst other things,’ said Riley. ‘I was getting assistance through Stobhill Hospital and the physiotherapist there, who specialises in Parkinson’s patients, got in touch to say there was a project going on at Toryglen where they were playing walking football and assessing you to see what the benefits may or may not be.’

Ogilvie and Riley are both on the board of Walking Football and have played a big role in its rise

Riley agreed to take part. ‘I really enjoyed it because you are back playing football again, you have the camaraderie and you have the home baking which I never really get at home.’

Riley had a hip replacement in March and admitted: ‘I have not played since then. I miss it. I play a few other things which are more and more difficult. I play tennis.’

His tennis history is illustrious as he played with Judy Murray in Scottish championships. ‘She went off to the tour and I went off to university,’ says Riley. He is unstinting in his praise of the woman who became the mother of the No1 in men’s singles and men’s doubles.

The repartee between Riley and Ogilvie is relaxed but both men have a resolve to grow walking football. Their task has been helped by bookmakers William Hill stepping in with sponsorship.

The benefits of the sport stretch beyond the physical. Ogilvie gave his own experience.

‘When you have finished working, there is a void, not mixing with people, not being with people, and walking football certainly has helped me fill that. Hopefully it helps me keep a bit fitter too,’ he said.

Ogilvie pointed out: ‘The issues you tend to encounter are health. Grief is another one. There are quite a few of the guys who have lost their wives and quite a few who are just sitting on the sofa watching Homes Under the Hammer.’

Walking football, too, has attracted a large cadre of women. ‘One of the things that stood out for me was the number of females playing,’ said Riley. ‘I went along to my first session and there were four female players, three of whom had never kicked a football in their life previously. Walking football got them involved and they really enjoyed it.’

Walking football has made giant leaps in Scotland but the next step is to collate data so cases for funding will be made unanswerable.

‘We need facts or what is called data,’ said Ogilvie. ‘We are trying to do some studies with universities to get that data — physical and mental — and get it to the government. We want to prove the benefits and then we could make this prescriptive through surgeries.

‘We see the benefits day to day anecdotally but that doesn’t bring you the data. Sponsorship helps.

Walking Football attracts thousands of participants at clubs across the country

‘The university studies won’t come overnight because they need to be detailed. But if we could get to the stage where we could prove the benefits, then the NHS and the government would see that proof in black and white.’

This is all serious and proper. But both men were keen to emphasise that the game was fun. Ogilvie said: ‘When I started, I asked someone: “How would you sum up walking football?” He said to me: “It’s a cross between watching Barcelona and the Gurkha warriors”.’

He added: ‘I loved when at the end of an over-75s tournament one player said to another: “Good game, son”.’

The football world has thus moved into a new era. But some things cannot change. Ogilvie said: ‘One of my grandsons, who is 10, came along to watch a session and said two things. Firstly, it is a bit more intense than he thought and second there are no pink boots.’

Riley chimed in: ‘That would be an affront, that would be unacceptable.’

Even in walking football some things are a step too far.

■ Eric Riley and Campbell Ogilvie were speaking at the announcement of William Hill as official sponsor of Walking Football Scotland. To find your nearest game visit walkingfootballscotland.org

#2175. GTA 6 è realtà! Com'era il mondo del calcio (e non solo) quando uscì GTA 5

"Sono passati 84 anni…". No, in realtà ne sono passati decisamente meno rispetto alla storia del Titanic raccontata da una Rose Dawson anziana nel film campione d'incassi di James Cameron, interpretato da Leonardo DiCaprio e Kate Winslet. Di anni ne sono passati 13, e sembrano un'eternità.

Di cosa stiamo parlando? Dell'evento mondiale: l'uscita di GTA 6, arrivata a distanza di 13 anni dal suo predecessore, quel GTA 5 che è stato uno dei prodotti più redditizi della storia dell'intrattenimento.

All'uscita di GTA 5, il 17 settembre 2013, in Serie A si era appena giocata la terza giornata. In testa c'erano Napoli e Roma a 9 punti, davanti a Inter, Juventus e Fiorentina con 7.

A fine anno la Juventus si laureò campione d'Italia con 102 punti, il secondo titolo consecutivo di quella clamorosa striscia di nove titoli di fila. Roma e Napoli conquistarono la qualificazione in Champions League, Fiorentina e Inter in Europa League.

Ciro Immobile era ancora l'attaccante del Torino e chiuse la stagione con 22 gol, laureandosi capocannoniere. Carlos Tevez guidava l'attacco della Juventus, Gonzalo Higuain era al Napoli, mentre la Fiorentina sognava in grande con la coppia Giuseppe Rossi-Mario Gomez, anche se alla fine fu una stagione decisamente travagliata per entrambi, con tante assenze per infortunio.

L'Italia guidata da Cesare Prandelli arrivò a giocarsi la finale dell'Europeo del 2012, perdendo in maniera roboante con la Spagna. Sempre con la Spagna perse ai rigori nella semifinale della Confederations Cup e nel 2013 fu impegnata nelle qualificazioni al Mondiale del 2014, l'ultimo a cui l'Italia ha partecipato.

In Champions League c'erano Juventus, Napoli e Milan, con le prime due che uscirono ai gironi. La coppa fu vinta dal Real Madrid, che vinse la finale contro l'Atletico alzando al cielo la Decima Champions della sua storia. Era il Real Madrid di Cristiano Ronaldo, Karim Benzema e Gareth Bale, quello che si giocava la Liga con il Barcellona di Messi e Neymar.

Lamine Yamal aveva appena 6 anni e non era ancora entrato nel settore giovanile del Barcellona. Erling Haaland di anni ne aveva 13 ed era ancora nel settore giovanile del Bryne.

Kylian Mbappé aveva 14 anni e anche lui muoveva i primi passi nelle giovanili del Monaco, squadra con cui due anni più tardi sarebbe arrivato in prima squadra. Michael Olise, 12 anni, giocava nelle giovanili del Chelsea, mentre il Pallone d'Oro Harry Kane non era ancora l'uragano di oggi e giocava la prima stagione con la maglia del Tottenham.

Ousmane Dembélé era ancora nelle giovanili del Rennes. E gli italiani? Prendiamo Pio Esposito: il bomber azzurro aveva appena 8 anni e giocava ancora nelle giovanili del Brescia.

In Italia il Presidente del Consiglio era Enrico Letta (PD), in carica dal 28 aprile 2013. Il Capo dello Stato era Giorgio Napolitano, mentre Papa Francesco era stato nominato Papa solo pochi mesi prima.

Negli Stati Uniti Barack Obama era al suo secondo mandato, mentre in Russia Vladimir Putin era presidente da poco più di un anno.

Facebook era il social network che dominava la scena mondiale, Twitter e Instagram erano molto meno diffusi. WhatsApp era già l'app di messaggistica più usata, mentre il mercato degli smartphone era, come oggi, dominato da Android e iOS.

A settembre 2013 fu lanciato l'iPhone 5s, mentre il modello di punta di Samsung era il Galaxy S4. In Italia ancora non c'erano le piattaforme di streaming: Netflix arrivò nel nostro Paese solo due anni più tardi. Al cinema dominava già il Marvel Cinematic Universe: gli Avengers si erano già formati e avevano già sconfitto Loki nella battaglia di New York. I botteghini erano dominati da Iron Man 3, Frozen, Hunger Games - La ragazza di fuoco e Lo Hobbit - La desolazione di Smaug.

In Italia il singolo più venduto del 2013 fu "Get Lucky" dei Daft Punk, feat. Pharrell Williams, rimasto 11 settimane in vetta alla classifica.

Marco Mengoni vinse il Festival di Sanremo 2013 con "L'essenziale", davanti a Elio e Le Storie Tese e ai Modà.

Visto che parliamo di GTA 5, all'epoca il gioco uscì su PlayStation 3 e Xbox 360, le console della generazione precedente a quelle uscite qualche mese più tardi. La PlayStation 4 uscì il 15 novembre 2013, mentre Xbox One una settimana più tardi. Fra i videogiochi più venduti del 2013, alle spalle ovviamente di GTA 5, troviamo titoli come Pokemon X/Y, Call of Duty: Ghosts, The Last od US e l'immancabile, almeno in Italia, Fifa 14.

Per oltre un decennio GTA 5 è stato uno dei giochi più giocati, vendendo oltre 235 milioni di copie e incassando oltre 10 miliardi di dollari, diventando uno dei prodotti di intrattenimento più redditizi della storia.

E GTA 6 ora è arrivato per battere tutti i record.

#2174. Approvato bilancio del Fondo di fine carriera calciatori: avanzo di 1,7 milioni

Approvato bilancio del Fondo di fine carriera calciatori: avanzo di 1,7 milioni

Sono questi i principali risultati del bilancio del Fondo di accantonamento delle indennità di fine carriera per i giocatori e allenatori di calcio, approvato oggi.

#2173. Pinamonti e la prima da titolare in casa: Lazio-Lecce per il tris

Senza Zaccagni, Gattuso prepara Noslin sulla sinistra; Frattesi si è allenato in gruppo, ma resta il dubbio sul suo impiego.

Andrea Pinamonti giocherà per la prima volta da titolare all’Olimpico con la Lazio nella sfida di sabato contro il Lecce. Secondo quanto riportato da La Repubblica, L’assenza di Zaccagni gli darà ancora spazio al centro dell’attacco, con Noslin sulla sinistra al posto del capitano. Per il centravanti è un’occasione per cercare il terzo gol in biancoceleste.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Pinamonti sa di non aver ancora conquistato i tifosi laziali. La fiducia di Gattuso nei suoi confronti, racconta La Repubblica, alimenta però la sua voglia di riscatto e può aiutarlo a sbloccarsi anche dal primo minuto.

Nelle due precedenti gare iniziate da titolare, a Udine e Napoli, il numero 9 arrivato in estate non ha convinto. È stato sostituito nella ripresa, rispettivamente al 52’ e al 61’. Sabato spera che la prima partenza dall’inizio in casa segni un cambiamento.

Proprio all’Olimpico, entrando nel secondo tempo, ha segnato i suoi due gol con la Lazio. Il colpo di testa contro il Cagliari è stato decisivo; il piattone del 3-1 contro il Monza ha chiuso una partita rimasta fino a quel momento in bilico. Ora Pinamonti va a caccia del tris.

Contro il Lecce, Noslin prenderà il posto di Zaccagni sulla fascia sinistra, mentre Pinamonti agirà al centro. L’intesa tra l’attaccante italiano e l’olandese è ancora da affinare, ma Gattuso è convinto che la formula possa funzionare.

Il tecnico ha ricevuto segnali positivi anche da Frattesi. La mezzala ha svolto con il gruppo il primo allenamento settimanale. Il lavoro in squadra non scioglie ancora la riserva su un suo impiego dal primo minuto contro il Lecce, ma cresce l’ottimismo sulla sua presenza.

#2172. If Spanish refs can speak up so can ours... over to you, Willie

Published: 12:39 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 12:46 EST, 13 November 2026

Since the beginning of the month in Spain, referees have started making themselves available for interview after matches to explain decisions and improve transparency.

It has led to people here asking why Scotland’s whistlers aren’t doing the same — and those questions are perfectly valid.

Back in August 2023, leading official John Beaton addressed calls for greater openness around VAR and gave the impression the refereeing firmament were in favour of a new spirit of glasnost.

‘Anything to explain how we are arriving at decisions and anything we can do to show people what we do on a Saturday and what happens in the VAR centre, we absolutely welcome,’ he said.

‘The more transparency, the better. The more questions we get, the more answers we can provide and, we hope, get to a better place,’

That was followed by new head of refereeing Willie Collum’s VAR show on YouTube the following season. At that point, there seemed a real move to lift the lid and build confidence in our men in black. There was talk of Collum doing his programme more than once a month and suggestions referees might even be open to being interviewed post-match in the right circumstances.

Unfortunately, though, all that has come to nothing and last term — in which controversial decisions had a huge effect on the title race — feels like it could become an obstacle in the way of progress.

Head of Referees Willie Collum should be applauded for speaking up on the SFA VAR show

It needn’t be, though. There has always been a feeling among referees that being put in front of the cameras carried danger. SFA Ian Maxwell has also spoken about the game being subject to ‘fairly hysterical commentary’ when something bad happens.

The media environment here is nowhere near as febrile as in Spain, though. It has four newspapers dedicated to sport alone. There are countless chat shows on council telly full of talking heads. Ages ago, news bulletins there had lipreaders telling viewers what coaches and players were saying on the park. Jose Mourinho is also back ruffling officials’ feathers at Real Madrid.

Getting Spanish refs to a place where they feel ready to be interviewed has taken time. It has also taken extra cash for them from TV money and it can still go a lot further.

A selected referee will speak to broadcast rights-holders only. And it’s not after every game. Only selected fixtures.

The SFA made real progress a couple of years back in lifting the curtain and should seek to rediscover that momentum — even though it won’t make the key issue of having to improve overall standards go away.

Since last year, the Spanish FA have been broadcasting their own YouTube show ‘Tiempo de Revision’ to address flashpoints. It should be said that it’s inferior to Collum’s broadcast in terms of content.

It replays incidents against a musical backdrop with captions explaining the decisions. There is no in-game audio, no one like Collum putting himself out there.

Although some of his reasoning for big calls was a bit suspect last term, that programme remains a platform to build from.

There is no reason why referees shouldn’t explain their decision-making after games here. Criticism they may or may not receive in the aftermath shouldn’t come into it.

If Spanish whistlers can go public, so can they.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2171. Mercato, Di Livio alla Roma: "Prendi questo giocatore dell'Inter a gennaio, è perfetto"

#2170. UFFICIALE – Napoli, sottoscritto i contratti preliminari per l’acquisto del Centro Sportivo: il comunicato

SSC Napoli comunica che, in data odierna, sono stati sottoscritti davanti al Notaio, Francesco Coppa, di Napoli, i contratti preliminari di acquisto per la realizzazione del nuovo Centro Sportivo in Qualiano. La società ringrazia per il supporto e l’assistenza l’Avv. Arturo Testa, il Prof. Arch. Bruno Discepolo, nonché il Sindaco di Qualiano, Dott. Raffaele De Leonardis, per aver creduto pervicacemente nella realizzazione del progetto, quale occasione di sviluppo territoriale. Forza Napoli Sempre.

#2169. Modena, la carica di Chichizola: "Siamo una squadra importante per la B, ma piedi per terra"

"Abbiamo lavorato bene in questi giorni, già questa settimana più con la testa alla Juve Stabia: dobbiamo provare a fare il meglio possibile per portare a casa i tre punti": così, parlando a parlandodisport.it, il portiere del Modena Leandro Chichizola, che è stato testimonial della 31ª edizione del Torneo Boldrini.

Al netto di ciò, l'estremo difensore ha poi aggiunto: "Siamo consapevoli di essere una squadra importante in questo campionato, ma non abbiamo ancora fatto nulla, quindi dobbiamo solo pensare a lavorare, continuando sulla strada intrapresa. Io ho vissuto alcune settimane difficili nel mio personale, ma ora mi sono ripresa, sono a 3000 e credo proprio di poter dare una mano alla mia squadra: di base, comunque, siamo una squadra che può fare la differenza, ci impegniamo sempre a mille, e anche la nostra gente lo sta vedendo".

Conclude: "Sarà la classifica gara di Serie B, un confronto difficile. Ma non dobbiamo guardare la classifica, dobbiamo solo lottare con le nostre armi, per fare la partita che vogliamo fare noi".
