#2168. Serie A, chi rientra dopo la sosta e gli squalificati del turno

Gudmundsson è atteso contro il Lecce; Bracaglia e Ndiaye devono fermarsi per una giornata, così come il tecnico Juric.

Gudmundsson è atteso contro il Lecce e Rovella torna tra le opzioni a disposizione della Lazio. È uno dei recuperi nel quadro della Serie A aggiornato alla mattina del 18 novembre 2026, in vista della dodicesima giornata in programma dal 21 al 23 novembre. Secondo quanto riportato da DAZN, Tra gli squalificati del turno figurano Gabriele Bracaglia del Frosinone e Ndiaye del Parma; deve fermarsi per una giornata anche l’allenatore del Monza Ivan Juric.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La sosta ha restituito alle squadre alcuni giocatori, lasciando aperte altre valutazioni dopo gli impegni con le Nazionali. Le finestre di rientro restano indicative e le convocazioni per il prossimo turno non sono ancora definitive.

Gudmundsson ha completato il recupero ed è atteso per la partita contro il Lecce. Anche Rovella rientra tra le opzioni a disposizione.

Tra gli altri recuperi, Bellanova e Raspadori sono tornati ad allenarsi in gruppo il 17 novembre e sono attesi tra i disponibili contro l’Inter. Mina e Obert hanno svolto parte della seduta con la squadra, mentre Nzola, Aurelio e Trepy hanno ripreso il lavoro collettivo e si avvicinano al ritorno tra i convocati. Resta da confermare la disponibilità di Sugamele, già segnalato alle prese con un problema fisico.

Chalobah è atteso a disposizione contro il Cagliari. Liberali ha superato l’infortunio, ma il suo impiego è ancora da verificare. Va valutata anche la piena disponibilità di Ranieri dopo il problema al piede. Gabriele Bracaglia, invece, salterà il Milan per una giornata di squalifica.

Nell’ultimo aggiornamento sull’infermeria rossoblù, Frendrup è indicato come unico indisponibile. Venturino non compare più tra le assenze segnalate.

In casa Inter, Lautaro Martinez e Marcus Thuram si sono allenati con il gruppo il 17 novembre. Gli esami di Akanji hanno dato esito negativo dopo l’affaticamento accusato con la Svizzera; il suo rientro ad Appiano è previsto il 18 novembre. I fastidi al piede di Zielinski non destano particolare preoccupazione, ma saranno ricontrollati al ritorno dalla Nazionale.

Cambiaso, Celik e Nico Gonzalez hanno lavorato a parte anche il 17 novembre e restano in dubbio. Cambiaso è alle prese con un affaticamento alla coscia, Celik con un fastidio accusato in Nazionale; Gonzalez sta recuperando dal problema al soleo. Zhegrova ha ripreso parte del lavoro con il gruppo, mentre Conceição ha superato il problema alla spalla ed è tornato ad allenarsi con la squadra.

Berisha ha ripreso il lavoro con i compagni: il sovraccarico muscolare non lo colloca più tra le assenze certe. Per Maignan, gli esami del 17 novembre non hanno evidenziato lesioni nella regione pubo-adduttoria. Il portiere resta monitorato, ma può essere disponibile contro il Frosinone. Pavlovic ha ripreso parzialmente il lavoro in gruppo e punta alla convocazione; Terracciano è ancora da valutare dopo il trauma alla spalla.

Non sono uniformi le ricostruzioni disponibili su Ziolkowski, Pessina, Ciurria, Mout e Ngonge, presenti nelle precedenti segnalazioni. Non vengono quindi considerati assenze certe: per la loro situazione farà fede l’aggiornamento del club prima delle convocazioni. L’allenatore del Monza Ivan Juric deve scontare una giornata di squalifica.

Neres, Favasuli e Spinazzola hanno ripreso almeno parte delle sedute con la squadra e puntano alla convocazione contro il Torino. Per loro non sono più attuali le precedenti indicazioni di assenza certa fino alla fine di novembre o a dicembre.

#2167. Hristov: "Pio Esposito? Allo Spezia non parlava dell'Inter! Ma si vedeva che..."

Hristov: "Pio Esposito? Allo Spezia non parlava dell'Inter! Ma si vedeva che..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Petko Hristov, ex difensore dello Spezia oggi al Levski Sofia, intervistato da La Gazzetta dello Sport, ha parlato così di Pio Esposito, suo compagno in Liguria: “Mostravamo i muscoli per vedere chi fosse più grosso. All’epoca vincevo sempre io. Voleva allenarsi sempre insieme a me, gli mostravo come fare alcuni esercizi. Aveva 18 anni, era il primo anno lontano dall’Inter. A fine seduta, stanchissimi, ci mettevamo davanti allo specchio per capire chi avesse lavorato di più o chi avesse fatto più pesi. Da lì a qualche settimana iniziò a esultare in quel modo”.

“Un diciottenne con la fame di arrivare, di restare un’ora in più dopo l’allenamento. Nel 2023-24 iniziai a correre con la squadra dopo tre mesi, a dicembre. Pio aveva qualche difficoltà a capire la Serie B, a trovare i giusti spazi, almeno all’inizio. Con l’arrivo di Luca D’Angelo è cambiato tutto. Nel girone di ritorno centrammo una salvezza meritata”.

Era già difficile marcarlo in partitella?

"Cavolo, sì. Lo soffrivo, ma mi piaceva. E anche a lui. Me lo diceva sempre”.

“Il suo modo di difendere il pallone e girarsi nel modo giusto. Ha tutto: tecnica, fisico, colpo di testa, quella fame che ti permette di ringhiare su ogni pallone”.

“Uguali. Ha segnato alcune volte, altre invece l'ho fermato. E poi chi segnava un gol in più esultava in faccia all’altro”.

“Riguardo il campo, poco o nulla. Solo di non discutere con gli altri compagni di squadra o di non cadere in facili provocazioni. In palestra, invece, gli dicevo come impostare gli esercizi. Per tutto il resto, c’erano l’allenatore e suo fratello Salvatore, con cui si trovava a memoria”.

“In realtà no, anzi, era straconcentrato sullo Spezia e sull'obiettivo. Nel 2024-25 segnò 19 gol. Onestamente non me l’aspettavo, anche se dopo un paio di mesi, giocando in partitella, si vedeva che rispetto all’anno prima ci fosse qualcosa di diverso. Era molto più concreto”.

“Una volta mi riaccompagnò a casa in macchina. Aveva preso da poco la patente, ci fu qualche manovra un po' così, quindi un signore dietro di noi suonò il clacson. Per scherzo, gli dissi che se fossimo scesi non ci avrebbe detto nulla. Siamo pur sempre alti più di un metro e 90…”.

“Ogni tanto sì, gli faccio i complimenti dopo ogni gol e sono fiero di ciò che sta facendo all'Inter e in Nazionale, dov'è sempre più decisivo. E' rimasto lo stesso ragazzo di La Spezia, quello che pianse sotto la curva e che tornò a salutare i suoi tifosi. Spero che mantenga questa umiltà”.

E se dovesse tornare a marcarlo? Magari in nazionale, Italia-Bulgaria…

#2166. Boston was a lovely holiday romance, but let's leave it there. Talk of taking Tartan Army back there every two years is just cringeworthy

Published: 11:18 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 13:03 EST, 13 November 2026

Boston was the loveliest little holiday romance. An unforgettable moment in time. But let’s just leave it at that, shall we?

Can’t we just allow those involved in the shenanigans around the World Cup last summer to reflect misty-eyed on doorways to Xanadu being opened by the tilt of the kilt and maybe even the heady nights they can’t remember much about at all?

Or does everything have to be milked to the max these days? Flogged like a dead horse to benefit someone or other. Ruined, really. Played out until every scintilla of fun has been drained out of it.

A bit like Yes, Sir, I Can Boogie. That started out as a bona-fide hoot. A half-cut Andy Considine dressing up in drag with his friends and family during his stag do in London in 2015 and making a music video around Baccara’s 1977 hit. It went viral. Obviously. It was – and is – hilarious.

When Scotland’s players resurrected it in the dressing room in Serbia five years later – with Considine as part of the squad – after the national team had ended a 23-year wait to make it to a major competition, it was simply inspired.

Every second of those infectious scenes exuded elation, joy, togetherness and pin-sharp humour, a killer recipe for the interludes that make life and living so great.

Now? Even the opening bars of the song bring on a sense of dread. It was splattered over T-shirts, played everywhere on repeat and still gets aired at every Scotland home game.

The Tartan Army march to Boston's Fenway Park baseball stadium

It’s boring. It’s been done to death and now lives firmly in that grim, stagnant swamp known as Scottish mainstream culture along with munchy boxes, Bitz N Pieces, theatre shows about the Old Firm and ex-footballers suddenly developing a personality and getting involved in stage shows to tell the world how Wee Dazza once did Big Jazza a beauty in the dressing-room by leaving a squelchy banana in his shoe. The banter anawrat.

Everything ends up spoiled here and it looks like we’re already well down that road with the Tartan Army thanks to the news breaking that the Scottish FA are in talks with the State of Massachusetts over a biennial friendly match at the Gillette Stadium, the black hole amid the festivity at the finals thanks to struggling past Haiti and embarrassing ourselves against Morocco.

State senator Paul Feeney has been across the pond to press the flesh. Massachusetts senate president Karen Spilka is already talking about staging Tartan Day – presumably some rip-off of that twee, shortbread-tin abomination in New York – and ‘other events’.

The heart sinks. Listen, the minute politicians get involved in any kind of ‘happening’, the best thing to do is give it a wide berth.

Mind you, before the Big Swerve, it might be worthwhile having someone remind these characters from The Bay State that Massachusetts’ first contribution to Scotland supporters hitting town in summer involved hiking the cost of the 25-mile train ride from Boston to the ground from $20 to a ludicrous $80.

What unfolded there in mid-June was nothing to do with them. It was everything to do with spontaneity. As it ought to be. A glorious coming-together of all sorts of folk up for a right, royal carry-on.

There were so many glorious vignettes. Scotland punters taking over Red Sox games. Bars running out of beer. Mass marches. Local women, doped-up on Outlander, telling TikTok how a bunch of jakeys intent on drinking anything within arm’s reach – except Bud Light, of course – were making them feel ‘seen’.

The Tartan Army make their presence known in Boston during the summer's World Cup

It was brilliant. A short spell in which stars aligned and magic happened. Boston had everything the Tartan Army required. And going by the testimony of Bostonians, the Tartan Army brought badly needed vitality back to the streets of a city quietened by Covid and other factors.

Everybody won. Everyone there still dines out on the memory. But that’s where it should stay. In the memory. These conversations about trying to recapture that spirit, recreate it every two years around some meaningless friendly against opposition unconfirmed and possible business meetings on the periphery, feel quite sad. How can it have any legs long-term?

Joe and Josephine Punter have moved on. They’re on the batter in other places now. Next week, it’s Switzerland for a Nations League game. After that, it’s wherever the draw for the Euro qualifiers throws them. New cities. New adventures. As football away days ought to be. In, out, shake it all about.

Away days are not about teaming up with suit-wearing dignitaries to be paraded at the town hall, like performing seals in plaid – even though there does appear to be an element of the Scotland support that enjoys that kind of thing.

Away days are not about being used as political capital or forming part of some constructed ‘festival’.

Look, in most aspects of life, it’s generally best not to go back. Sticking traffic cones on top of statues all over town won’t be as funny next time. There won’t be a 20,000-strong travelling support spending their life savings on games an entire generation had been waiting for. There won’t be anything like the same buzz, if any.

Some folk might fancy returning once, just to see if it feels the same. The idea that this is going to be a big deal every two years, though, feels deeply misguided.

Boston’s Irish. Not Scottish. That Tartan Week nonsense in New York is a toe-curlingly awful melange of kilts, Scottie dogs and actors living in America telling everyone back home how to vote.

No self-respecting Scot should give it house room. If there is a plan for the same thing in Boston, the same rules apply.

Like NYC, it’ll suit those from the political class in the mood for a junket, of course. John Swinney will already be counting the air miles if there’s another game in Massachusetts to go to.

The public, of course, is still counting the cost of the First Minister’s last trip there for the World Cup. The bill came in at £45,000 for him and two senior aides to fly business class. The entire trip for government staff came to more than £98,000.

Swinney plays the role with aplomb at these affairs, supping a pint in his Scotland top. The Scottish Football Association need to ask themselves this, though: why should they put themselves at the heart of some kind of cock-eyed project to build bridges politically?

The Tartan Army leave their signature traffic cone in a park in Boston

First up, the players are hardly likely to welcome a pointless week in America being added to a jam-packed schedule. More importantly, though, what exactly has this SNP-led administration, outwith giving a few bob to grassroots, done for Scottish football down the years?

From this vantage point, they’ve been no friend to the national game or its followers. The Offensive Behaviour at Football Act, repealed in 2018, was a scandalous piece of legislation that criminalised fans and made going to football unpleasant.

Clubs have had to fight tooth and nail to secure trials to sell pints inside stadiums when other sports are allowed to do what they like. After trouble in Glasgow city centre ahead of the Old Firm League Cup final two years ago, Swinney stated that ‘the clubs have got a responsibility to make sure their fans behave properly’.

Inside their grounds, yes. What are they meant to do, though, about teenagers running amok on Argyle Street?

Looking after that is the job of the police. Of course, anyone reading 1919 magazine, funded by the Scottish Police Federation, earlier this year will have noted an article about policing around football being ‘chaotic’ and understaffed because of SNP Government budget cuts.

Just like everything else in this sorry little nation built on buying votes for ‘free stuff’, Police Scotland is creaking at the seams. Why would anyone want to get involved in another transatlantic piece of theatre selling some false, outdated, vaguely comical vision of it to foreigners?

A pointless Scotland friendly – served up as part of some forced, hackneyed ‘cultural exchange’ or not – is hardly an idea to be getting on board with.

Pure dead brilliant, it won’t be. More like pure cringe.

#2165. Osmajic show nel Genoa, Colombo: "Mi piacerebbe molto giocare in coppia con lui"

"Sono contento perché è una società, una città, una squadra in cui è bello mettere radici, è bello perché c'è una passione fuori dal comune, è bello perché c'è uno stadio fuori dal comune, è una città in cui in cui si vive bene". Parla così l'attaccante del Genoa, Lorenzo Colombo, ai microfoni di Radio Sportiva commentando il momento personale che lo vede finalmente far parte in pianta stabile di una squadra dopo tanti prestiti dal Milan: "In squadra ci sono tanti giocatori importanti, poi, il capitano Vasquez, El Shaarawi, Messias e poi c'è Sabelli che comunque dentro lo spogliatoio è sempre un uomo importante. Perché dà sempre consigli, è sempre vicino al gruppo e tutti questi senatori, chiamiamoli così, a noi servono tanto. C'è qualcuno che dà l'esempio più con le parole, qualcun altro più con i fatti e dobbiamo cercare di di seguire questi esempi positivi. Tra i giovani, cito Wiafe, perché mi ha colpito fin dal primo giorno la sua attitudine, la sua mentalità ed è davvero un ragazzo speciale e diciamo che è un giovane...non giovane perché su tante cose calcisticamente lo vedi già molto avanti".

Su Osmajic, attaccante rivelazione del Genoa fino a questo momento, Colombo dice: "Ha avuto un grandissimo impatto, è davvero bello condividere lo spogliatoio con lui perché è un grande attaccante. Da lui c'è molto da imparare perché ogni cosa che fa, la fa al massimo e con una cattiveria agonistica incredibile e questa cosa spinge poi anche tutti gli altri compagni a a fare qualcosa in più. Mi piacerebbe molto giocare in coppia con lui, quando abbiamo giocato insieme in poco tempo gli ho fatto anche due assist. Mi piace di più giocare a due punte, perché occupiamo meglio lo spazio e e comunque giocando insieme riesci a impensierire meglio i centrali. E' altrettanto vero che gioco da prima punta da anni e lo so fare, per cui va sempre bene...".

#2163. Colantuono: "Oggi si è persa un po' l'attitudine a difendere. Un allenatore mi diceva..."

Colantuono: "Oggi si è persa un po' l'attitudine a difendere. Un allenatore mi diceva..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

C'è un aspetto nel calcio moderno che rischia di perdersi: il mestiere di difendere. È questa la riflessione di Stefano Colantuono, intervenuto ai microfoni della trasmissione Tutti Convocati. "Coi difensori si è perso un po' il lavoro preventivo, le coperture, quando devi scappare, devi scappare...".

"Un allenatore mi diceva voi dovete essere negativi, sempre pronti alla disgrazia, sempre pronti a poter prendere gol. Non si sa più difendere? Si è persa un po' di attenzione. Oltre alla fase difensiva ai difensori si chiede qualcosa in più, si è persa un po' l'attitudine a difendere. Ai miei tempi si marcava a uomo, seguivi il tuo avversario per tutto il campo, un duello continuo".

#2161. Arsenal, Zubimendi può partire: Arteta apre alla cessione per 70 milioni

Martin Zubimendi potrebbe lasciare l'Arsenal già nella finestra di mercato di gennaio. Il centrocampista spagnolo fatica a trovare spazio nella squadra di Mikel Arteta e starebbe valutando l'ipotesi di cambiare aria per avere maggiore continuità. Secondo AS, i Gunners hanno fissato il prezzo del cartellino a 70 milioni di euro.

Arrivato dalla Real Sociedad, Zubimendi è scivolato nelle gerarchie di Arteta, alle spalle di Declan Rice, Bruno Guimaraes, Martin Odegaard e Myles Lewis-Skelly. Il suo impiego è stato così limitato soprattutto alle competizioni di coppa.

Nonostante lo scarso minutaggio, il centrocampista continua a godere di grande considerazione in Europa. Liverpool e Chelsea hanno già avviato i primi contatti per valutare un possibile trasferimento. I Blues sarebbero al momento la società più vicina ad avviare una trattativa, mentre i Reds, pur apprezzando da tempo il giocatore, starebbero dando priorità ad altri obiettivi, tra cui Alex Scott e Lamine Camara.

Lo spagnolo aveva manifestato pubblicamente la volontà di restare a Londra, ma sarebbe ora più aperto a una partenza per trovare maggiore continuità. L'Arsenal, forte delle alternative a disposizione di Arteta, potrebbe quindi prendere in considerazione la cessione già a gennaio.

#2159. Former England star Dele Alli in line for huge £8.3m payday after selling his image rights company

Published: 10:30 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 13:07 EST, 13 November 2026

Former Tottenham star Dele Alli is set for a mammoth £8.3million payday as he plans to close down his image rights company, Dele Alli Promotions.

The now 30-year-old may be without a club but was not so foolish as to not prepare for life without a bumper contract, with his company boasting the sizeable sum in assets.

However, Alli will have to hand over £685,362 to the tax man - but this is a drop in the ocean in relation to the eyewatering £7.6million he is set to receive.

The former England international has been without a club for over 19 months, having last been in action for Cesc Fabregas' Como side in Serie A but only played one game, coming on as an 81st minute sub against AC Milan and receiving a red card just nine minutes later.

But Alli has several projects alongside Dele Alli Promotions keeping him busy while he is attempting to get back to full fitness and find a new club.

He owns and manages a private residential real estate holding company named Future Homes Investments, which owns and rents six properties worth more than £1.5million.

Dele Alli is looking at a very healthy payday after closing down his image rights company

The Englishman has played just ten minutes of professional football since February 2023

The Former Spurs man was forced to wear an Arsenal shirt on his Instagram story after losing a game of EAFC 27 to a fellow streamer

And his smallest company, Dele Investments still holds £336,267 in assets.

Most recently the former English international has branched out into livestreaming on Twitch with a skateboard, an array of expensive designer bags, and at least 48 pairs of shoes providing his backdrop.

He has already picked up traction on his channel by calling up old teammates such as Harry Kane, Eric Dier and Jack Grealish while live.

And while playing EAFC 27 with other steamers such as IShowSpeed, who recently forced Alli to wear an Arsenal shirt on Instagram after losing a wager to the 21-year-old American.

It is a far cry from his heady days as a player, when Dele twice picked up the PFA Young Player of the Year award and starred for England on their way to the semi-finals of the 2018 World Cup.

During an interview in 2023 on The Overlap, he opened up about his harrowing past, having to smoke and sell drugs as young as seven years old and being hung over a bridge by a man from neighbouring estate when he was just 11.

This resulted in the midfielder turning to sleeping pills and alcohol to suppress his feelings and help him through his trauma.

#2157. Lazio da scudetto, Sensini ci crede: «Ha tutte le qualità»

L’ex biancoceleste elogia il gioco di Gattuso e la forza del gruppo, ma indica nella prima sconfitta una prova da superare.

Secondo quanto riportato da La Gazzetta dello Sport, Nestor Sensini crede nella possibilità che la Lazio vinca lo scudetto. L’ex biancoceleste considera il titolo una sorta di miracolo sportivo, ma vede nella squadra di Gattuso le condizioni per provarci: «Ha tutte le qualità per centrare l’impresa.» Cita l’ambiente, la grinta e la preparazione tattica dell’allenatore, oltre alla consapevolezza che il gruppo sta acquisendo partita dopo partita.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il legame di Sensini con la Lazio risale al titolo conquistato in biancoceleste. Pur essendo affezionato anche a Udinese e Parma, ricorda soprattutto la festa vissuta a Roma: «Alla Lazio ho conquistato il mio scudetto. Quell’esperienza si è appiccicata alla pelle, la festa di un popolo intero. Come si fa a dimenticare una cosa così?»

Per Sensini, il gioco è uno dei punti di forza della Lazio. Gattuso ha dato alla squadra un’identità precisa: sa difendere e attaccare coinvolgendo l’intero organico. L’ex calciatore attribuisce questo risultato alla capacità del tecnico di convincere tutti della validità delle proprie idee.

Richiamando la sua esperienza in diversi spogliatoi, anche in quelli dove giocava Maradona, Sensini osserva che può esserci qualche scontento perfino quando arrivano i risultati. Alla Lazio, invece, gli sembra di vedere un gruppo unito, senza prime donne né i fuoriclasse della squadra con cui vinse lo scudetto, ma disposto a lavorare per un obiettivo comune. «In questa squadra vedo il carattere del suo allenatore», dice.

Tra i singoli cita Zaccagni, Noslin e Frattesi, pur insistendo sul collettivo e sulla disponibilità a fare una corsa in più per aiutare un compagno in difficoltà.

Sensini riconosce che Inter e Roma hanno rose superiori per qualità e quantità e che i mezzi finanziari della Lazio non sono paragonabili a quelli degli altri club. Per questo fa i complimenti ai tecnici e ai dirigenti che hanno individuato i giocatori adatti al progetto di Gattuso. Ritiene comunque meritata la posizione della Lazio: «Nove vittorie, due pareggi e zero sconfitte: il cammino è fantastico.»

Il suo dubbio riguarda la risposta della squadra a un risultato negativo: «Prima o poi arriverà una sconfitta e bisognerà vedere come il gruppo reagirà alla caduta.» Sensini è convinto che Gattuso saprà affrontare anche quei momenti, purché l’ambiente resti vicino ai giocatori. Sulla corsa al titolo si sbilancia: «Lo stesso dovranno fare i suoi giocatori, e mi sbilancio: alla fine, ce la faranno. E io sarò a festeggiare con loro».

L'ex dirigente di Napoli, Juventus, Inter e Milan, Marco Fassone, ha rilasciato alcune dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Stile TV nel corso della quale ha parlato del rendimento di Kevin De Bruyne, che ha mostrato un rendimento differente fra club e nazionale: "De Bruyne? Lo vediamo giocare in Nazionale in un modo e sembra sano e integro, a Napoli invece è su un tono diverso. Poi ha aggiunto: "Ma un giocatore di quel livello non sta garantendo al Napoli ciò che il Napoli si aspetta ed ha un costo significativo. Sicuramente quindi il club sta facendo le sue valutazioni e agirà per il meglio".

Fassone ha anche analizzato: "Quando ci sono stagioni come quelle del Napoli in cui la squadra non si esprime come dovrebbe, c'è qualcosa di più grande del semplice infortunio che sicuramente influenza. Ma c'è qualcosa di più e credo che il presidente stia facendo riflessioni dal punto di vista societario. E quindi costruire un progetto di ripartenza che non sia un retaggio della politica utilizzata da Conte. Gli ibridi funzionano sempre poco e c'è bisogno di costruire il Napoli del rilancio".

#2150. ‘Nothing more important’: Birmingham City fans will love Phil Neumann’s response to recent transfer rumours

Birmingham City have seen Phil Neumann star in their rise to the top of the Championship table. The German defender has grown into a key figure in his second season with BCFC, and his performances are not going unnoticed.

Amid links with Nottingham Forest and Fiorentina, Neumann has now been quizzed on his future in an interview with Transfermarkt. While maintaining the belief that he can play at a higher level, he insisted that his full focus is on the Blues’ promotion push. He said: “Of course I hear about those kinds of reports, but I don’t pay much attention to them. I have my advisors for that, people I trust who handle those matters.

“My full focus is on Birmingham City and what we’ve set out to achieve as a team. There’s nothing more important to me than doing everything I can to achieve our goals with the club. We have a big task ahead of us, and that’s where I’m concentrating all my energy. Everything else is secondary at the moment.”

Neumann is under contract at St Andrew’s until 2028. He later went on to say that if he was to leave the Blues, “the whole package would have to be right”.

Standout players in the Championship often have eyes on moves to the next level. In recent years, those to establish themselves in the second tier before making notable steps up include Hayden Hackney, Shea Charles and Borja Sainz.

The level of Neumann’s performances for Birmingham City suggests that he may be one of the next Championship aces to earn a move to a higher level. But, his words show that he has nothing on his mind other than the Blues’ ongoing promotion push.

The commitment to the cause he has shown in his response to rumours is aligned with the heart he’s showing on his pitch. Neumann embodies how this BCFC side are all pulling in the same direction to achieve their goal after a challenging 2025/26 season.

It is exactly what the fans were asking for as the club look to reach their lofty targets.

At 29, Neumann will feel that he needs to take a chance to play in the top flight sooner rather than later. The clear aim is to do so with Birmingham City, but interested clubs could offer him a route to a higher level if the Blues can’t make it.

Given the centre-back’s comments, it would be a surprise – and a big blow – to see him leave mid-season. You have to feel it would be the perfect package for both him and BCFC to sway them into a deal.

If Chris Davies‘ side can’t make it to the Premier League this season though, the chances of Neumann leaving will grow. He will be in the last year of his contract, and there will be sides ready to offer him a shot at a higher level.

For now though, Neumann’s full focus is on the promotion push at St Andrew’s.

#2148. Egypt sack their manager for 'negative impact on team image' after he fainted when fight broke out between his TWO wives with punches and hair-pulling in a bar

Published: 13:16 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 13:48 EST, 13 November 2026

Egypt will begin their African Cup of Nations (AFCON) qualifying matches with a new manager after their previous head coach was sacked amid a torrid breakdown of both of his marriages.

Hossam Hassan was handed his marching orders on Saturday after an incident in August which saw his two wives embroiled in a vicious fight, captured and shared on social media.

Hassan's first wife Howaida El-Gharbawy and his second Dina 'Dan Adam' Mustafa started the argument while at a hotel bar in the upscale Porto Marina resort on Egypt's north coast.

El-Gharbawy and Mustafa were videoed arguing with Hassan in the middle attempting to cool tensions, but the duo came to blows, with punches thrown and hair pulled as screams filled the air.

Such was the stress of the incident that Hassan is believed to have fainted as he tried to separate the two women.

Horrified public onlookers watched as the physical fight grew with relatives of the trio getting involved, and security was called before Hassan was taken to hospital.

En Égypte, un violent différend a éclaté entre... les deux femmes du sélectionneur égyptien, Hossam Hassan. 🇪🇬😳Le conflit a dégénéré en échauffourée avec coups et tirage de cheveux, avant que des jeunes et des passages n'interviennent dans la bagarre. pic.twitter.com/pvRUSMtAsD

Egypt head coach Hossam Hassan was sacked this week after a fight broke out between his two wives

Hassan's first wife Howaida El-Gharbawy and his second Dina 'Dan Adam' Mustafa started the argument while at a hotel in the upscale Porto Marina resort

The 60-year-old is believed to have fainted while trying to separate the two women

Both women are believed to have been left with cuts and bruises following the vicious scrap, while Hassan, despite briefly losing consciousness, was left unharmed.

His lawyers later told Sky News Arabia, via the Sun, that he was not suffering a health crisis during the incident, and that he remained in good health.

But in the days that followed, Hassan started proceedings to divorce El-Gharbawy, who he has been married to for decades. The couple also share three children.

The 60-year-old has a further two children with second wife Mustafa, who Le Parisien alleges also filed for divorce in the days after the footage went viral on social media.

The Egyptian Football Association ensured that Hassan's professional life took a similar nosedive, sharing in a statement that his dismissal was due in part to reputational damage the scandal might be doing to the national team.

'After analysing all the circumstances and events of the past few days, as well as their consequences and the negative impact they have had on the positive image and stability of our national team, the Board of Directors has decided, within the framework of its responsibilities, to dismiss coach Hossam Hassan and his entire technical staff and to end his tenure,' their statement read.

Mustafa's lawyer Mohamed Shams gave an interview on Sky News Arabia claiming that his client had been the victim of a 'premeditated attack'.

'We hoped it wouldn't come to divorce, but things escalated to this level.'

Hassan married his second wife in 2015, with Egyptian law allowing Muslim men to marry up to four women, providing that any wives have been formally informed that another marriage is to take place.

The law mandates that the husband is also obligated to treat all of his wives equally with regard to housing, financial support and standard of living.

Hassan had been married to his first wife El-Gharbawy for decades, with the pair sharing three of his five children

Hassan and Mustafa briefly separated four years ago before recoupling before the spat that ended both of his marriages.

As well as being relieved of his duties, Hassan was also sanctioned by the Supreme Council for Media Regulation, leaving him unable to publicly address the media following his sacking.

Mohamed Salah and Co will now play their first qualifier of the international break against Malawi under new head coach Ali Maher.

The former Liverpool star could be among those Pharaohs stars privately pleased with the departure of Hassan, after he was benched by his ex-manager for the last two international clashes.

When asked why the superstar and national team captain had been left on the sidelines for the ties, Hassan had said: 'It is not right for a country of 120 million people for the national team to depend on one player.'

#2143. Bruno Fernandes snubs second new contract offer from Man United in early headache for incoming manager Mauricio Pochettino - with star free to talk to foreign clubs as early as January

Published: 18:28 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 14:42 EST, 13 November 2026

Bruno Fernandes has rejected a new contract at Manchester United against the turbulent backdrop of the departure of head coach Michael Carrick.

The Manchester United captain publicly questioned the former manager's tactics after his side's Champions League thrashing at the hands of Como, and has struggled to find form this season.

Discussions are believed to have started between Fernandes and Man United over the summer, on the heels of a strong campaign which saw him named Men's PFA Player of the Year.

But after an initial proposal from the club is thought to have been rejected by the midfield talisman, Fernandes has now snubbed a second deal with improved terms, in what could be an early headache for incoming manager Mauricio Pochettino.

The Portuguese international's contract is set to expire in June 2027, which means that if the club and player fail to agree fresh terms, Fernandes will be free to discuss a summer move with clubs abroad as early as the January transfer window.

The club may yet seek to trigger the 12-month extension in Fernandes' contract, but will continue talks over aspects including his salary and the length of his new deal as they are keen for him to stay.

Bruno Fernandes has rejected a second set of terms from Manchester United as the club seeks to pin him down to a new deal

The club captain inked his latest terms in 2024 with his contract set to expire next June

Man United currently sit in the relegation zone after a 40-year worst-ever start to the season, and announcing a new deal for Fernandes might have given the club an important boost of morale during a difficult international break.

Fernandes was brought back into the fold after going through a brief period of being frozen out of Carrick's squad following his incendiary comments following defeat in Italy last month.

Fernandes, who is usually very deliberate in what he says in the media, was scathing in his criticism of coaches after the Como debacle.

'It's a decision from the staff to not press man-to-man and sometimes leave a little space in behind and try to get the ball in the first moment, to wait for them, to give them the ball,' he said.

'But sometimes when you give too much the ball to these teams they get confident, they have so many bodies behind the ball that make you run a lot, make people get frustrated, and then obviously when you recover the ball the legs are, and the brain, much more difficult to make it work.'

His post-Como interview was widely condemned by fans and pundits alike, with Gary Neville lamenting: 'If that's your captain saying that, imagine what they're saying in the dressing room behind closed doors when no one's listening. That's horrific.'

He also told Fernandes to 'read the room' and accused him of being an 'excuse merchant' on his podcast.

Fernandes was left on the bench for Man United's next Premier League appointment, a loss to Bournemouth, before returning to the starting XI against Chelsea the following matchday.

After Man United's comeback win against Roma, Fernandes was defiant in his post-match remarks as he stressed that he had not been sanctioned for undermining Carrick with his initial interview.

'The club needed that win. The players needed that win. It is something we needed as a group of players and staff,' he said. 'We have been working so hard to get out of this situation together. It has been tough. There has been a lot of speculation in the media.

Fernandes was publicly critical of his former head coach Michael Carrick last month but was later adamant that he had not held clear-the-air talks with him

'A lot of lies around many things that have happened at this club, between me and the manager, like that we have cleared the air. Nothing like that happened.

'The manager took a decision of making myself on the bench because I wasn't performing. And that's fair.

'Everyone felt like that was a punishment for the words that I had [after Como]. And if it was, it was right.

'The manager makes the decisions. I have to accept it. I have to go forward with that. It doesn't change the belief I have in the manager, the staff and every player at this club.

'And it doesn't change my aim to win every f***ing game that I have ahead of me.'

Fernandes' sabre-rattling was ultimately unable to keep Carrick in his job however, with the former club captain finally sacked on Thursday after an agonising three-day wait.

Man United's hierarchy, notably chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox, have come under fire from the fanbase and within the club for their treatment of the departing coach, with Carrick taking two days of training at Carrington as they mulled his sacking.

Wilcox and Berrada jetted to Monaco earlier this week to meet with Ineos chief Sir Jim Ratcliffe and discuss the departure of Carrick, with the club's Glazer ownership ratifying the decision to replace him with United States national team coach Pochettino.

Daily Mail Sport reported on Thursday evening that staff members inside the club have been upset with the protracted sacking, which has been deemed disrespectful of Carrick's status as a club legend.

#2141. Conte: “Penso che prima o poi il cerchio con la Juventus si dovrà chiudere”

“Penso che, prima o poi, il cerchio con la Juventus dovrà chiudersi” ha dichiarato Antonio Conte, ex allenatore del Napoli, durante un’intervista a Cronache di Spogliatoio. “So di aver lasciato qualcosa e quel cerchio lo dobbiamo chiudere. In passato ci sono stati momenti in cui avremmo potuto ritrovarci, ma è molto difficile, perché devono verificarsi due condizioni: io devo essere libero e la Juventus deve essere alla ricerca di un allenatore. Ci sono state due occasioni concrete. La prima quando la Juventus scelse Maurizio Sarri: io ero senza squadra e, alla fine, andai all’Inter. La seconda quando arrivò Giuntoli, che decise di puntare su Thiago Motta: in quel caso io finii al Napoli. Dopo Bari potevo diventare l’allenatore della Juventus, avevo parlato più volte con Castagnini e con Secco, c’era Ranieri in panchina, poi presero Ferrara per fare le ultime partite per andare in Champions. Loro sapevano benissimo che avrei fatto il 4-2-4, avendo attaccanti come Amauri, Iaquinta, Treseguet e Del Piero, dissi “con gli attaccanti siamo a posto, dobbiamo prendere degli esterni”. Loro, non so per quale motivo, presero Diego dal Werder Brema e mi chiamarono per avere delle rassicurazioni. Io vado, entro da allenatore della Juve e mi chiedono come volessi giocare e io dico “ancora? con il 4-2-4”. Mi chiesero dovre avrei messo Diego e io dissi “lo avete comprato, anche se abbiamo 4 attaccanti, lo metto al fianco alla punta”. Mi sento dire “Diego vuole giocare con il 4-3-1-2” e in quel momento pensa alla mia follia che fa capire che sono una persona dritta che non scende a compromessi. Un altro avrebbe detto “te lo faccio giocare anche in porta, basta che mi fai questo contratto”. Io forte delle mie idee e delle mie convinzioni, perchè non voglio scendere a compromessi, gli dico “se il giocatore deve decidere come giocare, non sono io l’allenatore giusto per voi”. Vado via da li ed esco che non sono più l’allenatore della Juventus”.

#2140. Sebastian Berhalter makes ‘promise’ to Middlesbrough fans amid £4.3m man’s tough start to life on Teesside

Middlesbrough signed Rokas Pukstas from Hajduk Split for £4.3m in the summer transfer window. Boro saw off interest from Birmingham City to land the attacking midfielder, but he has not had the impact hoped with only two senior appearances before injury hit.

The slow start is an obvious source of frustration, but one man backing Pukstas to come good is fellow summer signing and compatriot Sebastian Berhalter. As quoted by The Northern Echo, he expressed that Pukstas needs time to adjust and develop at only 22 before making a promise to the Riverside Stadium faithful.

“Once he gets back from his injury, he’s only going to come back better,” Berhalter said. “He works so hard; he’s one of the hardest-working guys I’ve ever met and he’s going to be just fine, I promise you that.”

Pukstas’ two substitute appearances have brought just 12 minutes on the pitch for Middlesbrough. He was unused on the bench in four Championship games before his hamstring injury hit, so supporters haven’t really had a chance to see what he’ll bring to Kim Hellberg‘s side.

The 22-year-old mainly operates in attacking midfield, offering a box-crashing threat as he powers into the box with well-timed runs to have efforts on goal. He has played in a deeper role before too though, as his physical traits translate to duel success and ability on the ball means he can be trusted in possession.

His well-rounded skillset and physical profile mean he is ideal for Hellberg’s flexible tactics. Expect to see him covering plenty of ground and popping up in both thirds, much like his supportive compatriot Berhalter.

His age means there is plenty of time for Pukstas to grow too. He needs time to get back up to fitness and settle into the Boro side, but Berhalter’s faith is well placed.

Understandably. Pukstas’ limited impact has brought some pessimism from sections of the Middlesbrough support. The fact he barely played before picking up an injury means they are yet to see why £4.3m was spent on him.

However, the success of Boro’s recruitment means that they have good reason to have faith he will come good on his price. Berhalter’s message should only increase the confidence that the American ace can deliver, but it also serves as a timely reminder that he may need time to show his best as he develops as well.

At present, the expectation is that Pukstas will not be able to return from his hamstring injury until December. Hellberg will likely offer a fresh update ahead of Boro’s clash with Bristol City this Saturday.

#2137. Swansea City in talks with 42-year-old over coaching return

Swansea City are working on a deal to bring their former defender back to South Wales. The Swans are set to lose head coach Vitor Matos to Ligue 1 side Strasbourg and they have a big decision on the direction they want to take next.

Williams, 42, spent nine years as a player with the Welsh outfit from 2007 to 2016 and helped them rise from League One to the Premier League, making over 350 appearances in all competitions. He is currently part of Craig Bellamy’s coaching staff of the Wales national team along with Alan Sheehan.

In this latest update regarding the managerial situation at the Swansea.com Stadium, reporter O’Rourke has claimed on X that he is in discussions over a potential return. He has been targeted as a candidate to work alongside Leon Britton and Kristian O’Leary as part of a caretaker trio whilst the club search for Matos’ permanent replacement.

Swansea City are positioned in 6th place in the Championship table after 15 fixtures. They are in the play-offs along with Stoke City, Wolves, West Brom, Southampton and QPR.

Swansea City could see Williams as someone to help steady the ship alongside Britton and O’Leary. He knows the club inside out and has just started his coaching journey with Wales.

The Midlands-born man was a hit during his playing days with the Swans and was popular among their fans. He also had spells at Stockport County, Everton and Bristol City before he hung up his boots in 2020.

Williams, who made 86 caps for Wales during his playing days, played with Britton so they know each other well already. Prior to linking up with Bellamy’s backroom staff team, he did some punditry work.

Swansea City will be weighing up their options as they prepare for Matos’ departure. They will be considering candidates and won’t want to rush into a decision.

In the short-term, Williams could join up with Britton and O’Leary to lead Swansea City into their upcoming matches. They face West Brom at home this weekend before an away trip to Middlesbrough next Tuesday.

In terms of who the Swans turn to next, it seems likely they will go down the abroad route and identify managers via their Jamestown Analytics model. There are plenty of available managers with EFL experience right now including Steve Cooper, Carlos Corberan, Rob Edwards, Sean Dyche and Steven Gerrard.

#2136. "Accetto la decisione, ma non la condivido". Cosmi risponde ai 3 mesi di squalifica con una lettera aperta

Era il 4 ottobre, e Serse Cosmi allenava allora la Salernitana, che era impegnata nel match casalingo contro il Bari: vittoria granata, 2-1 il finale, ma lo stesso confronto è costato caro al tecnico. No, non è stato esonerato in quel momento. Ma in quel momento ha rimediato tre mesi di squalifica e 10mila euro di ammenda, notificati nella giornata da poco trascorsa.

Cosa è accaduto? Molto semplice. Cosmi, rivolgendosi al calciatore serbo del Bari Karlo Butic, ha proferito le parole "Sei uno zingaro", che sono state udite anche da un collaboratore della Procura Federale, che ha testimoniato in merito alla faccenda accaduta al termine del primo tempo del match. Non ha negato Cosmi, che ha però detto di aver utilizzato sì il termine contestato dalla giustizia sportiva, ma in una frase differente. La sua versione, però, non ha convinto il giudice sportivo, che ha riconosciuto nelle parole di Cosmi, "un comportamento discriminatorio nei confronti di un calciatore avversario proferendo nei suoi confronti un epiteto comportante offesa, denigrazione e insulto per motivi di razza, nazionalità e origine anche etnica" (CLICCA QUI per leggere il comunicato integrale).

Non ci sta però il tecnico, che, in una lettera aperta inviata anche alla redazione di TuttoMercatoWeb.com, ha detto che accetta la decisione, pur non condividendola. "Non è il momento delle polemiche - si legge -, però è il momento di ricordare chi sono. Sono stato un insegnante, un istruttore e poi un allenatore. Ho passato la mia vita sui campi, nelle scuole calcio, nei settori giovanili, accanto a ragazzi e ragazze provenienti da ogni parte del mondo, anche da terre meno fortunate, da situazioni difficili. Li ho accolti, li ho allenati, li ho aiutati a crescere. Non solo come calciatori, ma come persone. Per me ogni ragazzo è sempre stato prima di tutto un ragazzo, senza mai distinzioni di provenienza, di colore, di nazionalità, di origine etnica, di religione. Chi mi ha conosciuto davvero, chi è stato allenato da me, chi ha condiviso con me uno spogliatoio, un campo di periferia lo sa bene". Non manca poi un ringraziamento a tutti coloro che in questo momento gli sono vicini.

#2134. Jurgen Klopp reveals he confronted Germany star over controversial crown celebration with links to 'anti-LGBTQ+ group' - and promises he won't repeat it

Published: 12:42 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 15:09 EST, 13 November 2026

Germany manager Jurgen Klopp insists midfielder Felix Nmecha will not repeat a controversial goal celebration after opposition from supporters.

Nmecha, a Christian player for Borussia Dortmund, was criticised last weekend for celebrating scoring by lowering an imaginary crown from his head onto the pitch.

The celebration - called 'The King's Return' - was popularised by an evangelical network of footballers called Ballers in God, which counts prominent Premier League players among its members. It is supposed to represent the idea of submission to God.

But an investigation by German outlets found that the campaign website for 'The King's Return' linked to a chat service run by an organisation called Groundwire, where counsellors shared anti-homosexual views and described abortion as 'Satan's Plan'. Daily Mail Sport encountered similar views when consulting the service.

Nmecha has maintained that his message was one of love, but after a huge outcry in Germany - and talks with Klopp and national team sporting director Rudi Voller - he will not adopt the celebration again.

Klopp said: 'It was a big ⁠issue. I saw Felix score two great goals and saw the celebration and then the gesture. I thought nothing of it at the time. But then it all kicked off.

Felix Nmecha has vowed not to repeat his 'King's Return' celebration after a huge outcry

He was called into a meeting with Jurgen Klopp and Germany's sporting director Rudi Voller for the celebration which is connected with evangelical Christian group Ballers in God

'As the controversy escalated, I started looking into the details of the subject and trying to form a clear picture of it.

'I have known Felix Nmecha for a few months, and I can say that he is a very friendly young man, who is warm and open, and always spreads positive energy when he is with us.

'I confronted him with all the things you might have confronted him with. He told me that it had nothing to do with that at all, and that he loves all people and does not discriminate against anyone.

'For me ⁠the most important issue was settled. Then I said "how do we do it with the goal ⁠celebration?" He will not be celebrating like that.'

A vocal section of Dortmund fans opposed the club signing Nmecha from Wolfsburg in 2023 after he had shared anti-homosexual and anti-transgender posts.

Ballers in God was founded in 2015 by former Tottenham midfielder John Bostock and brings together a wide range of Christian players from across the pyramid with weekly sessions.

While the organisation does not hold an explicit stance on LGBTQ+ issues, their Instagram account did celebrate the end of the Rainbow Laces campaign in the Premier League last year.

In response to the Nmecha story, they wrote: 'We are encouraged by player-led movements such as The King's Return, where footballers themselves take the initiative to express their faith in Jesus.

Klopp says Nmecha told him he 'loves all people and does not discriminate against anyone'

'Our desire is not only to share our hope, but to demonstrate love and respect towards everyone, including those who do not share our beliefs.

'Our focus has always been, and will always be, to point people to Jesus with grace and love.'

Nmecha said: 'My message has never been about promoting intolerance, discrimination, or hate in any form.'

Back at Dortmund, Nmecha, 26, still faces scrutiny. A supporters' group claimed that his celebration clashed with club values and he has been reprimanded by senior figures.

The club's president, Hans-Joachim Watzke, said: 'The fan department has every right to start a discussion like this. I was at their annual general meeting on Monday. It was clear that those who were bothered by parts of his celebration were in a significant majority. So you have to take it seriously and discuss it.

'If he scores a goal and points his index fingers upwards, everyone knows he wants to thank God. He is also welcome to make the sign of the cross.

'But placing the crown on the grass - that is, of course, somewhat linked to Ballers in God. I don’t even want to judge it. When I look at what they stand for, Borussia Dortmund’s code of core values and Ballers in God’s fundamental outlook are not entirely compatible. We don’t want that in our living room.

'I told him: "Felix, if you sense that it is dividing the fans, perhaps you will also realise that you shouldn’t do it any more." He wants to think about it. We had a wonderful conversation with him. We had a one-hour video call. That is where we are now, and he will think about it. I hope we can find a sensible solution.

'I like Felix Nmecha very much. We have a very, very good relationship and speak frequently. As a practising Catholic, I am the last person who would want to forbid someone from praying. That is not what this is about at all. It is legitimate, too. The only question we are discussing with him is the second part of his celebration.'

#2133. Verso Lazio-Lecce, svelato il direttore di gara: ecco i precedenti

In vista dell'incontro tra Lazio e Lecce di sabato 21 novembre alle ore 15:00, ecco a chi stato affidato l'incontro

#2132. West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City among clubs interested in League Two loan star

Clubs up and down the country will be preparing for the January transfer window, and among those likely to be active are Championship trio West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City. It has been said that WHUFC boss Scott Parker is prioritising a new striker and a left-back, but TEAMtalk states the club have eyes on centre-back Jack Taylor.

It is reported that the Hammers are alongside the Canaries and arch-rivals Millwall in tracking Taylor, who has thrived on loan at Exeter City from Stevenage this season. A host of clubs scouted the 21-year-old in action against Huddersfield Town earlier this month – a game in which he scored.

Taylor has Premier League admirers too though, with Brighton & Hove Albion, Everton, Brentford, Crystal Palace and Fulham taking an interest. Clubs are considering winter moves with the possibility of keeping him on loan at Exeter City for the remainder of the season.

Taylor, born in Bexhill-on-Sea in 2005, has risen through non-league to the EFL. He started out in the Eastbourne Borough setup before moving to Sutton United, and a string of loans in the lower leagues ultimately earned him a first-team spot at Gander Green Lane.

Stevenage came calling in January 2026, but limited opportunities prompted his summer loan to Exeter City. The 21-year-old has had to wait for his chance, but Taylor has now started the last eight League Two games on the trot. The Grecians have won five of those and kept five clean sheets in the process.

Taylor’s per 90 stats show just how effective he has been since breaking into Matt Taylor’s side. And, it is little wonder that sides like West Ham, Millwall, Norwich City and Premier League clubs are taking note.

No League Two centre-back betters Taylor’s 17.27 defensive contributions per 90, and he ranks top for his 79.2% duel win rate as well. He also averages 6.24 aerial duel wins, 11.51 clearances and 2.69 tackles per game (all stats via FotMob).

It isn’t often that young players rise through non-league and reach high levels anymore, such is the level of competition throughout the English pyramid. So, for Taylor to emerge as he has and draw interest from top clubs speaks volumes about his potential.

However, given his steep trajectory and age, admirers like West Ham, Millwall and Norwich City will have to be patient with his development. Taylor could yet benefit from seeing out a full season in League Two or playing some League One football before moving to the Championship – or even the Premier League.

Unfortunately for Exeter City, they are passengers when it comes to a decision over Taylor’s future. The call will be Stevenage’s to make, and they may well be inclined to cash in if the opportunity arises amid interest from WHUFC and more.

#2130. Le elezioni federali, il mercato della Juve, il modello Inter: tutte le dichiarazioni dal RCS Sport Industry Talk

Le elezioni Figc del 14 dicembre, il futuro degli stadi italiani e le prospettive economiche dei club. L'ottava edizione dell'RCS Sport Industry Talk, andata in scena a Milano, ha riunito alcuni dei principali dirigenti del calcio italiano. A partire da Giovanni Malagò, commissario straordinario della Federcalcio e candidato alla presidenza: "Sarebbe stato meglio non farlo del tutto, ma apprezzo", ha dichiarato a proposito della rinuncia di Adriano Galliani al ricorso contro la sua candidatura. Sul fronte dei consensi, Malagò ha aggiunto: "Forse recuperare deve farlo qualcun altro". Il presidente del Coni, Luciano Buonfiglio, non ha voluto esprimere preferenze: "Neanche sotto tortura". Il ministro Andrea Abodi ha invece accolto positivamente il "fair play necessario e indispensabile" tra i candidati, ribadendo di attendere dalla nuova Figc soprattutto le riforme.

Ezio Simonelli, presidente della Lega Serie A, ha ricordato che undici club sostengono Malagò e tre Galliani, auspicando un maggiore dialogo tra le componenti dopo le elezioni. Urbano Cairo ha confermato il proprio sostegno a Malagò, pur definendo Galliani un amico e una persona capace. Il presidente del Torino ha anche criticato i tempi della crisi federale: "Gli ultimi due mesi e mezzo sono stati buttati". Tra gli indecisi resta il Sassuolo, come ribadito dal direttore generale Andrea Fabris: prima di schierarsi, il club neroverde vuole confrontarsi con entrambi i candidati sui temi dei giovani e del format del campionato.

Sul fronte dei club, Giovanni Carnevali ha spiegato le difficoltà della Juventus sul mercato: "Oggi una delle difficoltà principali per noi è legata al settlement agreement, se vogliamo fare investimenti importanti sugli atleti non possiamo farli". L'amministratore delegato bianconero ha riconosciuto gli errori commessi negli ultimi anni, sottolineando la necessità di accelerare il ritorno ai risultati sportivi. Il presidente dell'Inter, Beppe Marotta, ha invece rivendicato il primo bilancio in utile della storia nerazzurra e la strategia della proprietà Oaktree: "Proseguiremo su questo modello, fatto di investimenti importanti come quello per lo stadio, ma anche nelle strutture del club".

Proprio il nuovo stadio di Milano è stato al centro dell'intervento del presidente rossonero Paolo Scaroni, che ha indicato settembre 2027 come termine entro il quale ottenere tutte le autorizzazioni. Scaroni ha inoltre evidenziato il divario tra Serie A e Premier League nei ricavi televisivi internazionali. Un tema condiviso da Simonelli e Cairo, che hanno insistito anche sulla necessità di valorizzare i settori giovanili. Il presidente della Lega Serie A ha chiesto al governo maggiori tutele per gli investimenti nei vivai, rilanciando inoltre le richieste del calcio italiano sui ricavi delle scommesse e sulla protezione dei dati utilizzati dagli operatori di betting.

#2129. Mauricio Pochettino breaks down in TEARS as he speaks out on 'most difficult decision' to leave US for Man United after agreeing to replace Michael Carrick at Old Trafford

Mauricio Pochettino broke down in tears as he began his farewell as US national team boss, and admitted that he couldn’t turn down the ‘dream’ job of Manchester United head coach.

United confirmed that Pochettino has agreed a two-and-a-half-year contract to replace Michael Carrick, and will start work on Wednesday after staying on for a double-header against Haiti.

Flanked by US Soccer Federation bosses at the team’s headquarters in Georgia, an emotional Pochettino wiped away tears as he thanked the players and staff.

‘I haven't prepared a speech, I'm talking with my heart,’ he said. ‘It's difficult for me to talk now because I am a very emotional guy.

‘When these moments arrive, it is always tough. Normally it is the opposite – you have been sacked! You cry because you want to stay.

‘I think it was one of the most difficult decisions of our career, but you have to move forward.

Mauricio Pochettino broke down in tears as he began his farewell as US national team boss

Pochettino spoke at a USMNT press conference after his move to Man United was confirmed

‘Not too many opportunities can happen in your career with a side like Manchester United. That is difficult to turn down when it arrives.

‘In some part it’s a very sad decision, in another part we are professional and we are trying to make our dreams come true. We are excited for that step forward.'

The 54-year-old Argentine revealed that United first made contact on Sunday after a 4-4 draw in Carrick’s final game left the club in the relegation zone.

United are paying US Soccer more than £10million in compensation to buy Pochettino and his backroom staff out of their contracts, and have allowed him to stay in charge for two more matches.

‘I never said I wanted to go back in the Premier League,’ said the former Tottenham, Chelsea and Southampton boss, whose first game in charge of United will be against Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday.

‘All of this was so quick because we arrived here last Friday and on Sunday afternoon this type of situation started to appear. There was some contact. When things became bigger, they are very sensitive.

‘We were very transparent with the federation and they understand the situation. The decision between US Soccer and Man United to agree to continue for these two games will not change our commitment.

‘That decision was between the three parties but, of course if Man United agreed different terms, I need to respect that.’

Pochettino, who will oversee his side's Concacaf Nations League quarter-final tie against Haiti over the next week, insisted it was the 'most difficult decision' to leave the USMNT

Meanwhile, Carrick issued a statement following his sacking on Thursday after 10 months in charge.

Posting on social media, he said: ‘I’ve had the immense privilege of leading Manchester United, the football club that I love.

‘There are very few who get to live that and feel it, and for that I’m incredibly grateful.

‘I want to say a big thank-you to the supporters. I must say, you are a special, rare breed.

‘I know we’ve not had the results lately, but you’ve shown me nothing but love and support in times when the easiest thing, and what most end up doing, would be to turn the other way.

‘That’s what makes you who you are. That’s why I fell in love with this club all those years ago.’

#2128. Cosmi, squalifica per le parole a Butic: «Sei uno zingaro»

L’allenatore ha sostenuto di aver usato una frase diversa; il Giudice Sportivo ha ritenuto attendibili le testimonianze raccolte.

Tre mesi di squalifica e 10mila euro di ammenda per Serse Cosmi. Secondo quanto riportato da SportMediaset, Il Giudice Sportivo della Lega Pro ha sanzionato l’ex allenatore della Salernitana per un’espressione discriminatoria rivolta all’attaccante croato del Bari Karlo Butic durante la gara dello scorso 4 ottobre allo stadio Arechi. La decisione arriva dopo la conclusione dell’incarico di Cosmi sulla panchina granata.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

la sanzione resterà congelata fino al prossimo incarico ufficiale dell’allenatore.

L’episodio risale all’intervallo di Salernitana-Bari, partita poi vinta in rimonta per 2-1 dai campani. Nel primo tempo Butic aveva portato avanti il Bari trasformando un calcio di rigore. Aveva quindi festeggiato sotto la Curva Sud, settore del tifo granata.

L’esultanza non era piaciuta a Cosmi. Al duplice fischio, mentre le squadre rientravano negli spogliatoi, l’allora tecnico della Salernitana si era avvicinato all’attaccante per parlargli. Secondo la ricostruzione accolta dalla giustizia sportiva, gli avrebbe detto «Sei uno zingaro».

Butic avrebbe cercato di spiegare che il festeggiamento era una dedica alla compagna incinta. Cosmi, sempre secondo la ricostruzione dei fatti, avrebbe risposto: «Che me ne frega della tua ragazza».

La decisione è arrivata dopo gli accertamenti svolti dalla Procura Federale al termine della partita. L’arbitro, il primo assistente e il quarto ufficiale hanno dichiarato di non aver sentito le parole scambiate tra i due: si trovavano a circa venti metri di distanza. Un collaboratore della Procura Federale, che era invece a circa un metro da Cosmi e Butic, ha confermato di aver udito distintamente le parole denunciate dal calciatore del Bari.

Ascoltato durante le indagini, Cosmi non ha negato né il confronto con Butic né l’uso del termine contestato. Ha però sostenuto di aver pronunciato una frase diversa: «Ho detto a Butic: 'Non ti lamentare se poi ti chiamano zingaro'».

Il Giudice Sportivo non ha accolto questa versione. Ha ritenuto pienamente attendibili il racconto del calciatore e quello del collaboratore federale, qualificando l’espressione come discriminatoria per motivi di razza, nazionalità e origine etnica.

#2126. Napoli, nuovo centro sportivo: acquistati i terreni a Qualiano. Le parole di De Laurentiis

Il Napoli avrà un nuovo quartier generale. Il presidente Aurelio De Laurentiis ha sottoscritto oggi l'atto di acquisizione dei terreni nel comune di Qualiano, in località Pozzo Nuovo, dove edificherà il futuro centro sportivo del club azzurro. Un’operazione di circa 13 ettari di terreno.

Questa la nota del Napoli: "SSC Napoli comunica che, in data odierna, sono stati sottoscritti davanti al Notaio, Francesco Coppa, di Napoli, i contratti preliminari di acquisto per la realizzazione del nuovo Centro Sportivo in Qualiano. La società ringrazia per il supporto e l'assistenza l'Avv. Arturo Testa, il Prof. Arch. Bruno Discepolo, nonché il Sindaco di Qualiano, Dott. Raffaele De Leonardis, per aver creduto pervicacemente nella realizzazione del progetto, quale occasione di sviluppo territoriale. Forza Napoli Sempre".

Fermato dai cronisti presenti nel luogo, ADL ha rilasciato qualche rapida dichiarazione: "Un sogno? La sua casa il Napoli ce l'ha sempre avuta a Castel Volturno, dove abbiamo vinto due scudetti. Parlate sempre del Napoli come se l'avessi preso in Serie A, allora avreste ragione. Voi avete visto dove si allenava Maradona? Sicuramente oggi è un nuovo inizio, perché abbiamo fatto una prima fase e adesso passiamo alla seconda fase. Sperando che la salute ci accompagni!".

"Fatto! Avanti tutta!!". Questo il testo, accompagnato da due foto durante le firme dei contratti, del tweet di Aurelio De Laurentiis, dopo aver siglato l'atto di acquisizione dei terreni nel comune di Qualiano in cui intende edificare il futuro centro sportivo del Napoli.

Il sindaco della città Raffaele De Leonardis ha fatto il punto della situazione da via dei Mille dove si sta firmando l'atto notarile per il passaggio di proprietà dei terreni: "E’ un lavoro che va avanti da due anni che stiamo facendo con il presidente De Laurentiis e stamattina siamo arrivati alla conclusione - riporta TuttoNapoli.net -. Credo sia un’ottima occasione di sviluppo per la nostra città nonché tutta l’area flegrea e giuglianese. Ci sarà molto da lavorare dal punto di vista urbanistico in seconda battuta, con il presidente abbiamo parlato del miglioramento dell’asse viario ed anche della ferrovia che collega la zona. La nostra amministrazione è disponibile. Se ci sono degli interventi da fare sotto quella tipologia di situazione, se dovremo farli non ci saranno problemi. Credo che il Napoli abbia già la possibilità di presentare la pratica alla Zes che ha tempi di lavorazione della pratica di circa 45 giorni. Dopo l’atto vedremo che tipologia d’acquisto che verrà fatto. Il Comune non è coinvolto nell’operazione: la fanno il calcio Napoli ed un privato. De Laurentiis vorrebbe iniziare i lavori l’1 gennaio ma la vedo molto difficile. Più probabile verso marzo".

#2125. Derby County will hope influential star can build on Bolton Wanderers display and hit form after a tough season: Opinion

Derby County remain rooted in the Championship relegation zone after a tough season to date, but they enjoyed a much-needed win over Bolton Wanderers before the break. Not only did the 3-1 triumph mark the end of a 10-game winless streak for DCFC, but it might just have kept John Eustace in his job.

Ryan Nyambe made headlines for scoring his first career goal in the win, while Jack Robinson and Rhian Brewster also got on the scoresheet. They were among the standout players against the Trotters, while Mo Fuseini and Jacob Widell Zetterstrom also impressed.

Carlton Morris was another man to play a central role in the win. The Rams striker has come under fire amid his poor form at the start of the 2026/27 season, but after a much-improved display against Bolton Wanderers, Morris will be eager to kick on and get back to his dangerous best in front of goal.

Although some have overstepped the mark, criticism of Morris has been justified this term. He hasn’t been helped by a Derby County side desperately lacking in creative spark and quality in possession, but one goal in 12 games across all competitions marks a big drop-off from last season.

His sole goal has come from only 2.38 xG in the Championship too, so not only has he struggled to convert his opportunities, he isn’t getting many high-quality chances either (FotMob).

Morris’ wait for a first Championship goal since August continued against Bolton Wanderers, but the all-round play of the 30-year-old striker was far better. It was his header across the box that Robinson scored the second goal from. And, after he held up the ball well, his cross was flicked onto Nyambe by Fuseini for the equaliser.

The DCFC man created three chances in total and won 11 aerial duels (FotMob). He was tireless in attack for Eustace as they fought to secure a key win, and it showed why the boss has retained faith in Morris.

There have been criticisms about his mobility from supporters. But, in Morris, Eustace has a powerful outlet at the top of the pitch who will run and battle for his team. He leads from the front, and if he can rediscover his goalscoring touch, he can play a key role in turning around the Rams’ fortunes.

Morris’ display against Bolton Wanderers showed how he brings other players into the game and offered a timely reminder of how his aerial ability and physical traits can be used with better service.

But, ultimately, Morris needs to start scoring to really silence doubters and keep his place. Lars-Jorgen Salvesen has found game time hard to come by but his two goals equate to 0.75 goals per 90. It has raised fair arguments that the Norwegian should be given a starting chance over Morris.

Morris scored 12 goals in 31 games last season and he was excellent before injury ended his campaign early. His Bolton Wanderers performance was a much-needed reminder of just how influential he can be, and it can be a catalyst for the striker as he looks to inspire DCFC’s rise away from the Championship relegation zone.

#2124. England's new plan of attack: Thomas Tuchel has switched style since World Cup but clunky win over Croatia showed growing pains - Spain will put it to severe test, writes JACK GAUGHAN

Published: 07:00 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 16:17 EST, 13 November 2026

It was gone 11pm when Thomas Tuchel was shaking the hands of Harry Kane’s parents on the Wembley pitch while the children of England’s record appearance maker enjoyed the run of the national stadium in front of 90,000 empty seats.

This was time for Kane to fully take in the emotion of an historic night alongside his family and that Tuchel was out there too felt instructive of their relationship.

Kane and Tuchel had frank conversations last month about the World Cup fallout, on how England might be best tinkering with their selection when chasing games.

The manager respects and values his captain’s opinion on these things, recognising that he is almost completely agenda-free, as they attempt to better plot a route to silverware in readiness for the European Championship in 18 months’ time.

If there was uncertainty about identity and style when the World Cup crunch arrived, when Enzo Fernandez was pulling stupid faces and the colour drained from England faces, then players have felt that the detail on how Tuchel wants to move forward has been clear across the first two camps since America.

Thursday night was not vintage against a Croatia team they battered 7-0 a few weeks back. Tuchel and Kane both admitted that afterwards but it is noticeable that there is a defined plan on how they intend to attack in a slightly different way.

Thomas Tuchel embraces Harry Kane's wife Katie at Wembley on Thursday night

Harry Kane (centre) and Co are changing the way they attack after the World Cup

Were Tuchel to have been able to pick a first-choice back four then the win would likely have been more comfortable, Croatia benefiting from individual errors. And a lack of cutting edge inside the box hindered the hosts too.

The idea behind what they were doing appeared well thought out though and that should bring about some gentle encouragement.

The full backs, Trent Alexander-Arnold and Lewis Hall, were both consistently worked into hugely dangerous areas in the inside channels, while the make-up of Tuchel’s midfield felt very deliberate as well.

It is no coincidence that England enjoyed the highest amount of possession in the half spaces ten yards inside Croatia’s half. That is a clear directive, with both Elliot Anderson and Declan Rice drifting wider to create overloads and the full backs pushed into threatening spaces.

‘Declan understands the game like he’s almost a coach on the field and in a perfect position to manage all these things from behind,’ Tuchel said this week. To have a constant interchanging unit, he does need brains.

These are the rotations that the Three Lions didn’t engineer enough of over in the United States, with Kane dropping deep as he does and completing that wonder crossfield pass for the Morgan Rogers opener.

They are the moments that Tuchel is preaching, Jude Bellingham taking up an unusual position in order to stretch Croatia as he squared for the Chelsea forward.

What Tuchel needs now is to work on how that can be translated into making sure his very best players can affect matches more regularly inside the opponent’s box.

Jude Bellingham only touched the ball four times in Croatia's box in the 2-1 win

England manager Thomas Tuchel will speak to his players about what to do if racial abuse mars Sunday’s Nations League group decider against Spain in Madrid.

The England team have been on the wrong end of racism in Madrid before – back in 2004 – and refused to play a friendly there in 2008, insisting the fixture be moved to Seville.

In March this year, meanwhile, Spanish fans chanted anti-Muslim songs during a game with Egypt in Barcelona and the World champions have been subsequently ordered by FIFA to play their next friendly in a partially-closed stadium.

There will be a full house at Athletico Madrid’s Riyadh Air Metropolitano stadium as England try to topple Spain from the top of their group on Sunday and Tuchel said: ‘I think I hear about it [the Barcelona incident] for the first time that this happens. I think it's unacceptable.

‘In any stadium we will never accept racism.

‘I expect full support from the Spanish crowd for the Spanish team. But we will not expect anything else. And I think the referee in the games and the officials know about that.

‘I think we had a same discussion before the Serbia game [last year]. There is a protocol in place.

‘Yeah, maybe we take our five minutes to inform the players about the protocol if there is a precedent [in Spain].’

Tuchel and the FA expect a hostile atmosphere in Madrid, especially as two Real players – Jude Bellingham and Trent Alexander-Arnold - will be in the England squad.

Last year’s Serbia game passed without incident in Belgrade and the FA are confident the same will apply this weekend.

Seven years ago in Sofia, though, captain Harry Kane almost led the England team off the field after following FIFA protocols by alerting the referee to abuse aimed at Tyrone Mings, Raheem Sterling and Marcus Rashford. The game was paused while the culprits were thrown out.

‘I still think the protocol is in place and it's important that the players know about is,’ added Tuchel.

‘And then yeah, we will give our answers if stuff like this happens. The best answers are on the pitch.’

The FIFA protocol – adopted by UEFA and the Premier League – is a four step-process that starts with a player alerting match officials and is followed by a warning to the crowd, a possible suspension of play and, if necessary, the abandonment of the game.

That Kane only touched it six times inside the 18-yard area and Bellingham on just four occasions points to a team still figuring themselves out.

That will be put to the severe test in Madrid on Sunday. England do not beat the truly elite nations; never have.

And it’ll be interesting to see whether Tuchel sticks to this sort of plan, one designed to dominate, or if he instead relies on the counter-attacking ability of a group he has viewed as a ‘Premier League team’ before now, leaning into their physicality.

If England are feeling confident enough, giving this tweaked style a go against the world champions might offer them some answers on what could be achieved when the proper silverware is on the line.

#2123. Lazio, nodo rinnovo per Cancellieri: la situazione sul futuro dell'esterno

Dopo aver respinto gli assalti estivi di Brentford, Parma e Fiorentina su richiesta di Gattuso, la dirigenza biancoceleste valuta il prolungamento oltre il 2027.

Il futuro di Matteo Cancellieri alla Lazio continua ad attirare l'attenzione in vista dei prossimi mesi. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, il club deve riflettere con attenzione sul contratto dell'esterno italiano, visto che l'accordo attuale scadrà il prossimo 30 giugno 2027.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Nel corso dell'ultima sessione di mercato estiva, diversi club avevano provato a sondare il terreno per assicurarsi il giocatore. Dall'estero si era fatto avanti il Brentford dalla Premier League, mentre in Serie A avevano preso informazioni prima il Parma e poi la Fiorentina, specialmente dopo l'approdo nella Capitale di Albert Gudmundsson. Tuttavia, a bloccare ogni possibile partenza è stato lo stesso allenatore Gennaro Gattuso, fermamente intenzionato a non ridurre ulteriormente le opzioni a sua disposizione nel reparto offensivo.

Il tecnico punta forte su di lui anche per gennaio. Nonostante qualche guaio fisico, Cancellieri ha trovato spazio ed è stabilmente nelle rotazioni, alternandosi con un Isaksen in grande forma. Ora tocca al presidente Claudio Lotito e alla dirigenza decidere se avviare le trattative per il rinnovo.
