#2122. Juventus, McKennie fa chiarezza sul Manchester United. E Chiellini: "Yildiz sta tornando"

In casa Juventus ci sono da registrare le parole di Weston McKennie, che è tornato a parlare delle voci di mercato sul Manchester United, facendo chiarezza su quanto circolato negli scorsi giorni. Intanto a Bruxelles, alla presenza di una delegazione bianconera composta dal Presidente Gianluca Ferrero e dal Chief Club Affairs Officer Giorgio Chiellini, si è tenuta l'inaugurazione Juventus Official Fan Club Unione Europea.

Queste sono state le parole di McKennie sul suo futuro, raccolte dall'emittente statunitense 90WCPO dopo la gara vinta dagli Stati Uniti nella notte italiana, contro Haiti (per 2-0): "Sono felice alla Juventus e sono felice di restare. È per questo che ho rinnovato il contratto e non vedo l'ora di trascorrere molti altri anni qui", ha detto.

Poi ancora, McKennie ha aggiunto: "Sono un giocatore della Juventus e c'è stato un fraintendimento su quello che ho detto, con le traduzioni e tutto il resto, che ovviamente mi si sono ritorte contro. Non so perché, immagino che qualcosa sia andato perso nella traduzione".

La Juventus al Parlamento Europeo, ecco le parole di Giorgio Chiellini al Parlamento Europeo: "Personalmente, è già estremamente emozionante essere al Parlamento Europeo. Non c’ero mai stato ed è sempre emozionante vedere cosa succede fuori dall’Italia e come si cerca di cambiare e sistemare il mondo". Chiellini ha parlato anche del ritorno di Yildiz: "Kenan può sempre aiutare, sta meglio. Kenan sta recuperando, però ci vuole un po’ di tempo. È un giocatore che può farci fare la differenza".

Lo stesso Chiellini ha rilasciato anche un'intervista al podcast Business of Sport, ed ha rivelato di non aver mai pensato di lasciare il club: "Sono stato molto fortunato a trovarmi nel club in quel periodo storico e a restarci con continuità, perché non è facile rimanere così a lungo in una grande squadra: ho giocato 17 anni di fila nella Juventus. Non è solo questione di fedeltà, ma di sentirsi parte di una famiglia. Per un italiano, essere nel miglior club d'Italia, iniziare a vincere e vedere la società crescere sotto ogni aspetto significa che non esiste un posto migliore dove stare. Non c'è mai stata davvero un'alternativa. È capitato un paio di volte, nei momenti difficili, di ricevere delle offerte, ma alla fine sei talmente fortunato che non riesci ad andartene".

Spazio anche a un retroscena sull'Arsenal: "Il mio primo contratto l'ho firmato a 16 anni. All'epoca avrei dovuto firmare per l'Arsenal: Liam Brady era il responsabile del settore giovanile e ricevetti un'offerta per andare lì. Era una cifra molto alta per l'epoca — oggi sarebbero circa 60.000 euro, allora non c'era ancora l'euro ed erano circa 90 milioni di lire — e dissi di no. Ancora oggi non so bene perché, visto che non aveva alcun senso rifiutare un'opportunità simile, ma non volevo lasciare il mio club gratis o quasi. Prima dei 16 anni, senza contratto, potevi andar via per un indennizzo minimo, così firmai per meno di mille euro l'anno. Ero cresciuto lì, era casa mia, e sentivo che non aveva senso partire in quel momento."

#2121. Barnsley vs Sheffield Wednesday predictions lodged as the Tykes ‘saddening’ decline continues

Barnsley come into Friday’s Yorkshire derby against Sheffield Wednesday in dire straits. The Tykes sit 23rd in the League One table with just nine points to their name after seven defeats in their last nine matches in the third tier.

Daniel Stendel‘s side had found some respite in domestic competition with wins over Aldershot Town and Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup and EFL Trophy respectively. However, their league woes continued with a 2-1 defeat away at fellow strugglers Wigan Athletic last Saturday.

As for Sheffield Wednesday, they claimed a valuable three points in Dave Challinor’s first League One game last weekend. Billy Mitchell, Davis Keillor-Dunn and Di’Shon Bernard scored on the way to an important 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle, marking the Owls’ first league victory in four matches.

SWFC have to get back on track on the road though, having lost back-to-back games against Notts County and Doncaster Rovers by 2-0 scorelines as Henrik Pedersen‘s tenure came to an end. Wednesday now sit 5th, six points away from the automatic promotion spots.

“Barnsley and Sheffield Wednesday are in drastically contrasting positions. The Tykes are lingering in the League One relegation zone with continued discontent towards the ownership, while SWFC are hoping to challenge for promotion as they rebuild under ambitious leadership.

“Daniel Stendel marked an exciting summer appointment at Oakwell but he has been completely hung out to dry. The decline in quality of that squad is just saddening, and with 24 goals conceded this season, I have little faith that their defence can curtail the visitors’ quality this Friday.

“SWFC are finding their feet under Dave Challinor, but the 3-0 win over Plymouth Argyle marked an exciting result in his first league game. Davis Keillor-Dunn is in fine form and Di’Shon Bernard is a true Championship quality centre-back, and a big asset at this level.

“Perhaps the occasion can motivate Stendel’s young side and drive them to a surprise result. However, you can’t help but feel the visitors win in pretty comfortable fashion at Oakwell.”

“Barnsley are in serious danger of being relegated this season. Their form has been abysmal at home and with no clean sheets at Oakwell this term, they will be up against it on Friday.

“It’s a great opportunity for the Tykes to incite revenge on the Owls who beat them in the play-off final three years ago. However, the difference in squad talent is a lot greater this time around.

“Sheffield Wednesday have been terrific on the road and with an average of 1.67 points a game, promotion is a real possibility. However, two losses away from home in a row should give a glimmer of hope to Stendel’s side.

“I can’t see the hosts winning this one, though, and Challinor will be eager to continue his winning start as boss. I think SWFC will be far too strong on the night.”

#2120. Napoli, Edo De Laurentiis: “Ho commesso degli errori, ma lavoro ogni giorno per essere all’altezza del mio ruolo”

E’ andato in scena la prima edizione del premio “Eccellenza Calcistica Italiana”, riconoscimento dedicato alle figure che hanno lasciato il segno nel panorama calcistico nazionale. Tra i vincitori figura anche il vicepresidente della SSC Napoli, Edoardo De Laurentiis, premiato per i meriti conseguiti nell’area della Governance: “E’ per me un grande onore ricevere questo riconoscimento, ma la gioia è doppia per averlo condiviso con mio padre, che è il mio mentore e la mia guida. Ho mosso i primi passi in questo mondo quando ero ancora molto giovane, ho commesso diversi sbagli, ma mi impegno quotidiano per crescere ed essere all’altezza del mio ruolo”.

Successivamente, a margine della cerimonia di premiazione, il vicepresidente ha aggiunto: “La chiave è proseguire giorno dopo giorno con costanza, facendo gruppo. La coesione è fondamentale per raggiungere gli obiettivi prefissati. Diventerà la futura casa del Napoli (riferendosi al nuovo centro sportivo), un luogo in cui far crescere i giovani, costruire il nostro vivaio e scoprire nuovi talenti. Abbiamo vissuto tantissime esperienze entusiasmanti, dall’era Spalletti fino a Conte. La più grande è stata osservare la squadra capace di conquistare costantemente traguardi prestigiosi e di mantenersi sempre ad altissimi livelli”.

#2119. Patrick Dorgu sent home from Denmark duty after failing fitness tests - just hours after joining up with team-mates - in early blow for new Man United boss Mauricio Pochettino

Published: 17:03 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:05 EST, 13 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino already has a fresh injury headache at Manchester United - and he's not even through the door yet.

Patrick Dorgu has been sent home from Denmark duty having failed a series of physical tests.

The full-back missed four Premier League matches in October due to a hamstring injury but came on to provide an assist and score the equaliser in the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa on Sunday.

Now he re-joins an injury list which includes Marcus Rashford, Luke Shaw, Carlos Baleba, and potentially Harry Maguire, with Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko ruled out longer-term.

Denmark's team doctor Martin Boesen said: 'We have carried out a series of tests on Dorgu, and he has not performed well enough to be ready to play in the two matches.

'He needs further recovery time after his injury, so we are sending him home for additional rehabilitation.'

Patrick Dorgu has been sent home fromDenmark duty after failing a series of fitness tests

It is potentially another thing for incoming manager Mauricio Pochettino to worry about

Dorgu, 22, will miss matches against Portugal and Norway in the Nations League.

Pochettino will hope Dorgu makes a quick recovery as he seeks to get Manchester United back on track.

United, who sit 18th in the Premier League, agreed to pay more than £10million to prise Pochettino and his staff away from the United States national team.

Pochettino has signed a two-and-a-half-year deal to replace Michael Carrick and will get down to business after his final matches in charge of the USA against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

The former Southampton, Tottenham, and Chelsea boss broke down in tears as he began his farewell and admitted that he couldn’t turn down the ‘dream’ job of Manchester United head coach.

Flanked by US Soccer Federation bosses at the team’s headquarters in Georgia, an emotional Pochettino wiped away tears as he thanked the players and staff.

‘I haven't prepared a speech, I'm talking with my heart,’ he said. ‘It's difficult for me to talk now because I am a very emotional guy.

‘When these moments arrive, it is always tough. Normally it is the opposite – you have been sacked! You cry because you want to stay.

‘I think it was one of the most difficult decisions of our career, but you have to move forward.

‘Not too many opportunities can happen in your career with a side like Manchester United. That is difficult to turn down when it arrives.

‘In some part it’s a very sad decision, in another part we are professional and we are trying to make our dreams come true. We are excited for that step forward.'

The 54-year-old Argentine revealed that United first made contact on Sunday after the 4-4 draw in Carrick’s final game left the club in the relegation zone.

‘I never said I wanted to go back in the Premier League,’ said Pochettino, whose first game in charge of United will be against Liverpool at Anfield a week on Sunday.

‘All of this was so quick because we arrived here last Friday and on Sunday afternoon this type of situation started to appear. There was some contact. When things became bigger, they are very sensitive.

‘We were very transparent with the federation and they understand the situation. The decision between US Soccer and Man United to agree to continue for these two games will not change our commitment.

‘That decision was between the three parties but, of course if Man United agreed different terms, I need to respect that.’

#2118. Kean è un caso per il Como, che non vuole l'operazione. La scelta per la lista Champions

Il Como attende novità sulle condizioni di Moise Kean, che è diventato un vero e proprio caso. 35 milioni di euro tra parte fissa e bonus la cifra spesa per acquistarlo, ma il giocatore fino ad ora è stato frenato da diversi fastidi fisici alla tibia. Le indiscrezioni hanno riportato un duro contrasto di gioco riportato in una seduta al centro sportivo di Mozzate. E il club ha scritto: "A seguito di un problema verificatosi durante l’allenamento, il calciatore Moise Kean si sta sottoponendo a ulteriori accertamenti medici. La priorità del Club è sostenere Moise nel suo percorso di recupero".

Rimasto ko per una botta ricevuta nel match contro il Belgio con la Nazionale italiana nella sosta di settembre-ottobre, è rimasto fuori inizialmente una settimana e mezza, per poi vedersi protrarre i tempi di recupero di oltre un mese. Una capatina a Barcellona, all’istituto Cugat, per svolgere un trattamento mirato alla risoluzione del fastidio alla tibia. Ora è comparso un ulteriore intoppo lungo il percorso di recupero, ma secondo le informazioni raccolte da TuttoMercatoWeb la volontà da parte del Como sarebbe di non arrivare a un intervento chirurgico. Preferita una via conservativa, in attesa degli esiti degli esami strumentali dopo quanto accaduto in allenamento.

Per il ko riportato da Jesus Rodriguez (di oltre 60 giorni), il Como ha effettuato un cambio liste Champions per permettere l'inserimento di Maxence Caqueret nell'elenco dei convocati di Fabregas per gli impegni europei - i prossimi contro Betis Siviglia, PSG e Barcellona. Tuttavia, stando a quanto appurato, questo non avverrà per Kean: l'attaccante italiano resta segnato e non verrà sostituito da un altro giocatore. Questo anche perché è possibile sostituirne uno solo di giocatore fino alla sesta giornata di League Phase compresa. Intanto, l'acquisto oneroso del Como non mette piede in campo con la maglia biancoblù dallo scorso 20 settembre.

#2117. Lazio, Mimmo Cugini: "Gattuso tira fuori il massimo, la squadra adesso viaggia forte"

Il giornalista della Gazzetta dello Sport analizza il momento d'oro dei biancocelesti: dai meriti di Gattuso al confronto con le big.

Il noto giornalista della Gazzetta dello Sport Mimmo Cugini, intervenuto ai microfoni di TuttoMercatoWeb durante la trasmissione Maracanà, ha analizzato il momento straordinario della Serie A, concentrandosi in particolare sulle ambizioni e sul percorso di crescita della Lazio guidata da Gattuso.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Se facciamo una valutazione tecnica, nelle seconde scelte c'è un gap con le squadre che lottano per il titolo. Bisogna però valutare come gioca questa Lazio, perché Gattuso è riuscito a far dare ai giocatori tutto ciò che hanno e anche qualcosa in più. L'esempio più lampante è quello di Frattesi, che all'Inter era in un sistema di gioco che non lo valorizzava nonostante i gol pesanti segnati in Champions League. I giocatori della Lazio fanno quello che sanno fare e quindi ad ora viaggiano.

Lo scudetto è una grande scommessa e illusione, ma capisco l'entusiasmo dei tifosi, perché una stagione in cui le altre non danno il cento per cento può capitare. Ad ora non vedo troppe squadre che possono metterla in difficoltà, ma sicuramente capiremo di più quando avrà affrontato Roma, Inter e Como. In questo momento sono sulle ali dell'entusiasmo.

#2116. Watford’s midfield struggles will continue until reinforcements are signed – here’s three January targets they must consider

A major issue for Watford this campaign has been the midfield. The summer departure of Imran Louza was a major blow, with his technical qualities, set-piece delivery and ability to dictate games sorely missed.

Similarly, Giorgi Chakvetadze’s transfer to Udinese has left the Hornets devoid of a creative spark, with his tendency to drift centrally and unlock defences. The club have simply not recruited adequate replacements, and they are suffering for it.

The current midfield options at Vicarage Road consist of Hector Kyprianou, Edo Kayembe, Edoardo Bove, and recent signing Martin Payero. None of these options are particularly offensive or astute going forward, lacking the ball-carrying and forward-thinking impetus of Chakvetadze, and the game-changing potential of Louza.

In order for Watford to change their fortunes this season, the club’s hierarchy need to address the glaring issues in midfield. Here, we put forward three targets the Hornets should consider moving for ahead of the January transfer window.

Patrick Kelly had a breakout season for Barnsley in 2025/26, establishing himself as one of League One’s most exciting prospects. He was touted for a potential step up, but stayed put and has remained a standout at Oakwell.

A busy and energetic player, Kelly could well offer Watford the spark they need in midfield. His excellent dribbling and physical attributes would complement a side in need of inspiration and dynamism. He ranks among the top 10% of players in his position League One for dribbles completed (FotMob).

Barnsley have had a disastrous start to the current campaign, and as such, a cut-price deal for the 22 year old could tempt clubs further up the footballing ladder.

Bate has been a critical part of highflying Stockport County’s success this season. The diminutive playmaker has contributed four assists and started all but one of the Hatters’ league games this campaign. He excels with his technical ability, ball retention and progression, and eye for a pass.

Having put behind him his injury issues from last season, he appears to have recaptured his impressive form from the 2024/45 campaign, cementing himself as a potential coup for alert Championship sides.

At 24 years of age, he represents a good blend of experience and potential to improve further. His position as club captain further demonstrates the leadership qualities necessary to navigate a Championship relegation scrap, which may await at Watford.

Hull City’s promotion and busy summer window has left players such as Kieran Dowell with limited minutes, opening the door to a potential exit. Dowell has been omitted from the Tigers’ Premier League squad, and the prospect of a January exit appears increasingly likely.

Dowell has donned the colours of six different Championship clubs, and has developed a reputation for scoring spectacular goals, as well as being an apt creator and technician. With three promotions at this level, he has the necessary pedigree and experience.

Out of contract in the summer, Dowell would represent a cost-effective option on either a short-term deal or a loan. His lack of playing time in recent seasons is a potential concern, but should he regain match fitness quickly he could be a useful addition to a threadbare Watford midfield.

As Watford seek to steer clear of the Championship’s lower reaches, bolstering the midfield is of high importance. The only summer addition in this area, Martin Payero, has been underwhelming when given the opportunity.

The fitness issues plaguing Edoardo Bove, the most talented player of the current options, has further stunted Watford’s progress.

The January window offers the club a chance to make amends for poor summer transfer business and to provide the squad with the best opportunity to consolidate their Championship status. The three midfield options put forward would help to push the club in the right direction.

#2115. De Laurentiis: “Film sul Centenario? De Giovanni ha scritto la sceneggiatura, Amazon lo porterà nel mondo

Aurelio De Laurentiis, presidente del Napoli, è stato tra i protagonisti della prima edizione del premio “Eccellenza Calcistica Italiana”, manifestazione dedicata alle personalità che si sono distinte nel panorama calcistico nazionale. L’evento si è svolto a Villa Campolieto, a Ercolano, dove il patron azzurro ha ricevuto un riconoscimento nella categoria Fair Play, che vedeva tra i finalisti anche Andrea Belotti e Luca Percassi. Nel corso del suo intervento, De Laurentiis ha parlato anche del film dedicato al Centenario del Napoli: “La sceneggiatura del film del centenario la scrive il qui presente Maurizio de Giovanni. Il calcio in Inghilterra è nato con gli aristocratici. Non era un fatto popolare. Arrivarono a Napoli e iniziarono a giocare con gli scugnizzi. Il 1922 sembrerebbe la vera data, poi procrastinata al 1926 per la nascita del Napoli. Che non si chiama Società Sportiva, ma Calcio Napoli. Questo film è venuto talmente bene che una piattaforma importante come Amazon lo porterà nel mondo. Quando abbiamo vinto lo Scudetto con Conte, ho fatto un film e sono andato a Los Angeles: presi questo cinema e ho fatto questa anteprima dove sono arrivati questi tifosi dall’America. C’erano donne emozionatissime che dicevano di non capire nulla di calcio ma che si sono comunque fatte trascinare. Ho scoperto che a San Pedro ci sono 30mila tifosi del Napoli legati all’isola d’Ischia”.

#2113. SCOTLAND 2 NORTH MACEDONIA 1: John McGinn at the double to spare the blushes of Pocognoli's shaky Scots on skipper Robertson's big night

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:01 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:11 EST, 13 November 2026

On the night when the nation looked over its shoulder and celebrated the past, the here and now for Scotland looked distinctly uncertain.

Coming 40 years and a day after his friend, Sir Kenny Dalglish, pulled on a Scotland jersey for the 102nd and final time, Andy Robertson joined the great man on a mark which, for years, seemed beyond the reach of mere mortals.

By time up, history had mercifully repeated itself at Hampden. Back in the day, Dalglish, by then player-manager at Anfield, sailed into the international sunset on the back of a routine 3-0 win over Luxembourg.

Facing another opponent of modest ability, Robertson’s red letter day didn’t go quite as smoothly. Yet, when the Tottenham man is old and grey and reflecting on the night he created history, the annals will at least record a slender victory.

While his side did just about enough to prevail here, this was another fraught night for Sebastien Pocognoli.

You can talk about the need for patience under the new manager until the cows come home. But North Macedonia are 71st in FIFA’s rankings. Scotland are 43rd.

John McGinn celebrates in trademark fashion after netting Scotland's winner from the spot

At Hampden Park, in front of an expectant crowd, Scotland should be putting them to the sword with something to spare.

Yet, for long enough, the Belgian’s new-look side were flat, predictable and plodding. It said much that it took the intervention of VAR for them to get the penalty which would ultimately lead to three points being accrued.

John McGinn’s second half double saved the Belgian’s blushes and truthfully there was little else to get the Tartan Army off their seats.

Too many big-name players scarcely merited pass marks. Too few looked comfortable in the system the manager is trying to bed in.

If it was a step in the right direction, it was a small one. After five games under the affable Belgian, the progress has certainly not been startling.

While it goes without saying that beating the Swiss in St Gallen on Monday will be an inordinately tougher task, having some hope of moving up a place and securing a kinder draw for the Euros is still better than no hope.

Regardless of what pot they end up in when the draw is made in Belfast next month, a radical improvement will certainly be required across the board if they are to stand any chance of qualifying for the tournament via the front door.

As well as the achievements of Robertson and Dalglish, the career of Craig Gordon was acknowledged before the match with the 84-times capped keeper deservedly afforded a warm hand by the Tartan Army.

McGinn slots home his penalty after Ben Gannon-Doak had been brought down in the area

There were absolutely no excuses for Scotland here. North Macedonia were a tier-two team in name only. They arrived at Hampden having not won in 12 matches and not scored in eight.

Another Pocognoli game saw another back-three combination. This time Luke Graham was selected in the centre of the unit with Ryan Porteous and Kieran Tierney deployed either side of the Stoke man in what were their first appearances under the Belgian.

Robertson and Ben Gannon-Doak were tasked with providing the width in the 3-4-3. Scott McTominay slotted back into an advanced role beside John McGinn with the returning Che Adams asked to lead the line.

For all there was a healthy crowd in excess of 40,000 inside Hampden, the atmosphere was subdued. In the four Nations League games to date under the new manager, the side hadn’t exactly given the supporters much to feed off.

Despite some big guns returning, Scotland made a laboured start to their night’s work. It said much that Graham, on his second appearance, was busier than any defender in white.

The first opening came as a result of an opposition error. John McGinn found Billy Gilmour who fed the overlapping Robertson. The skipper’s cross was well out of Adams’ reach.

Sebastien Pocognoli got his first Hampden win as Scotland boss but it was a nervy night

Although Ljupche Doriev worried Angus Gunn with a curling effort, Scotland began to exert some degree of control on the match. Lewis Ferguson kept the visitors hemmed in with a header into McTominay’s feet. The Napoli man saw his shot on the spin well saved by Stole Dimitrievski.

Pocognoli was frustrated when Slovakian referee Michal Ocenas blew for a foul on McTominay, his justifiable gripe being that playing an advantage would have seen Robertson have a crack at goal.

Dimitrievski had a decent night. Although Adams’ flashing header from McGinn’s corner took a deflection, a reflex save at his near post added to Scotland’s frustration.

North Macedonia were a spirited but ordinary side. They still had enough to thwart Pocognoli’s men.

Gannon-Doak saw plenty of the ball down the right, yet the end product often wasn’t there. The Bournemouth man did tee up Billy Gilmour for one dangerous cross. McTominay tried to replicate his iconic overhead kick against Denmark nearly a year ago. This time, his aim wasn’t true.

You could hardly blame Pocognoli’s star man for failing to find the net on that occasion.

Scott McTominay attempts an overhead spectacular a year on from THAT goal at Hampden

That wasn’t the case a few moments later when Adams slipped him in. Dimitrievski saved McTominay’s strike with an outstretched leg when he should have been picking the ball out of his net.

Adams had just blazed a strike high over the top while one-on-one with the keeper although the offside flag saved his blushes.

The visitors came closer to edging ahead when Eljif Elmas played the pass of the match over Scotland’s static defence to pick out Ezgjan Alioski. His lay-off was smashed into the side net by Enis Bardhi.

A wholly uninspiring first-half concluded with Robertson advancing up the left and launching a cross halfway to Castlemilk. This was not going well.

Pocognoli waited on the edge of the pitch for his players returning, his enthusiastic applause clearly audible in the arena.

His hopes of a breakthrough at the outset of the second period were dashed in extraordinary fashion. Adams did well to chase a ball down to the line and cut it back. McTominay and his marker couldn’t connect, leaving Gilmour the simple task of firing into an unguarded net. Leaning back, his failure to even hit the target was unfathomable.

Elmin Rastoder celebrates after silencing Hampden with a leveller for North Macedonia

Within seconds, the sense of relief inside Hampden was palpable as the opener finally arrived. Porteous got enough distance on his clearing header for Adams to barge Visar Musliu out of the way.

McGinn was alert to the situation and burst into the box. His crisp strike finally had the beating of the goalkeeper.

It should have settled Pocognoli’s side and paved the way for a comfortable result. Instead, they were pegged back within three minutes.

It was all just so simple and so avoidable. Bardhi’s whipped corner only needed McTominay to outjump his man. Flat-footed as the delivery arrived, he could only watch on in horror as Elmin Rastoder’s flashing header beat Angus Gunn.

Although Gilmour worked the keeper with a low strike, North Macedonia also sniffed a second goal. Time and time again, they got into pockets of space on the flanks and fired in dangerous crosses.

Adams made way for Oli McBurnie, the Hull man desperately hoping for his first goal on his 21st international appearance. He had a gilt-edged chance to get off the mark when rolled in by McGinn, but selflessly passed to the overlapping Gannon-Doak.

Scotland move on to their final game away in Switzerland as they look to finish on a high note

The winger appeared to take a heavy touch which allowed the keeper to rescue the situation. VAR, however, spotted that Dimitrievski had impeded him. Penalty.

McGinn did the necessary from 12 yards, smashing the ball straight down the middle for his 24th international goal.

North Macedonia, to their credit, kept going for it. They sensed a fragility in Scotland. Everyone in the ground could.

McGinn’s calm conversion proved to be just about enough to get the job done. But it’s fair to suggest that we are still awaiting lift-off in the new era.

SCOTLAND (3-4-1-2): Gunn; Porteous, Graham, Tierney; Gannon-Doak (Hickey 76), Ferguson, Gilmour (Christie 76), Robertson; McGinn; Adams (McBurnie 67), McTominay. Booked: Ferguson. Manager: Sebastien Pocognoli.

NORTH MACEDONIA (4-2-3-1): Dimitrievski; Ilievski, Mladenovski, Musliu, Alioski; Gashtarov (Elezi 64), Elmas (Stankovski 78); Churlinov (Maleski 64), Bardhi, Doriev (Nikolov 85); Rastoder. Booked: None. Manager: Goce Sedloski.

#2112. 3 strikers West Ham should target in January as Scott Parker sets new talisman demand

West Ham have the busy December schedule to see to before the January transfer window comes around, but Scott Parker is already intent on improving his squad. And, he’s identified two areas where he wants to sign new players: striker and left-back.

The centre-forward has been a position of contention for WHUFC, despite the fact they are the league’s top scorers with a remarkable 37 goals in 15 games. None of Joel Piroe, Pablo or Taty Castellanos have proven they can find consistent goalscoring form, and the latter reportedly wants to leave the London Stadium.

With that in mind, we put forward three strikers West Ham should target to resolve their dilemma.

Parker is said to prefer Championship experience, so who better than someone who is already starring in the division? Andri Gudjohnsen has been in scintillating form for Blackburn Rovers, with a goals per shot rate of 0.50 ranking him among the most clinical finishers in Europe this season.

The 24-year-old has scored 11 goals in 15 games this term and has starred on the international stage for Iceland too. Gudjohnsen scored four in two games over the November break.

A fast and powerful forward with smart movement and finishing, Gudjohnsen could come in and lead the line for West Ham and he’d be backed to make the step up to the Premier League too. It would not be a cheap signing, but he could be just what Parker needs.

Looking slightly further afield, one man who could catch the Hammers’ eye is Paulos Abraham. The 24-year-old was highly rated in his youth but a spell at Dutch side FC Groningen didn’t go as hoped, resulting in his return to Sweden with Hammarby IF.

Since returning to the Allsvenskan in January 2025, he has been one of the league’s most dangerous forwards. 17 goals in 28 league games this season take Abraham to 34 goals in 74 outings for the club, and he was rewarded with his first Sweden call-up earlier this season.

Abraham would bring electric pace to West Ham’s frontline, and his inventive finishing sees him score from unlikely situations. His ability to run beyond Championship defences could bring a new threat to a highly creative WHUFC outfit.

Last but not least is certainly the most ambitious of the lot. However, Aleksandar Mitrovic has had a slightly disrupted recent spell in the Middle East due to a ligament injury and may be tempted to return to London under the lead of Parker again.

The Serbian powerhouse played and starred under the new West Ham boss at Fulham, and he would be a dream addition for Parker. Mitrovic is one of the most prolific forwards in Championship history with 85 goals in 126 games at the level and he would certainly fit the bill as a prolific scorer who can lead the Hammers to promotion.

The main obstacle is obvious though. Mitrovic is on massive money in Qatar with Al-Rayyan, and even if WHUFC can persuade the club to let him leave, the 32-year-old might not be minded to leave his sizeable contract.

But, if the opportunity was there, Mitrovic would be a perfect solution for Parker’s striker issue.

#2111. Antonio Conte esce allo scoperto: "Tornare alla Juventus? Prima o poi il cerchio si chiuderà"

Antonio Conte esce allo scoperto e parla apertamente di un suo ritorno alla Juventus. In una anticipazione di un'intervista rilasciata a Cronache di Spogliatoio - la versione integrale uscirà venerdì - è tornato a parlare del suo rapporto con i bianconeri e della possibilità di tornare sia nel futuro: "Penso che prima o poi il cerchio si dovrà chiudere. Io so di aver lasciato qualcosa, so che quel cerchio prima o poi lo dovremo chiudere con la Juventus", la candida ammissione di Conte.

Conte ha raccontato in particolare che ancora oggi molti tifosi della Juventus lo fermano per strada chiedendogli di tornare alla guida della loro squadra del cuore: "Tante volte incontro dei tifosi juventini che mi chiedono: «Quando torni?»", ha raccontato l'ex ct della Nazionale. E poi, ha spiegato: "La risposta è che tornerò quando ci sarà l'opportunità, quando si incroceranno alcune situazioni...". Conte ha anche aggiunto: "So che non sarà semplice, anche se penso che prima o poi il cerchio si dovrà chiudere".

"Ci sono stati in passato dei momenti in cui ci saremmo potuti riunire, ed è molto difficile perché devono capitare due situazioni: io devo essere libero e la Juventus deve cercare un tecnico senza panchina. Ci sono state due situazioni: una quando la Juventus decide di prendere Maurizio Sarri, io ero libero e infatti poi vado all'Inter. La seconda volta che ero libero quando arriva Giuntoli, che poi prende Thiago Motta e io vado a Napoli".

"Dopo Bari potevo diventare l'allenatore della Juventus, avevo parlato con Castagnini e Secco più volte. C'era Ranieri sulla panchina della Juventus, poi lo mandano via e prendono Ferrara. Loro sapevano che avrei fatto il 4-2-4 , avendo Amauri, Iaquinta, Trezeguet e Del Piero dissi che eravamo a posto davanti, che dovevamo prendere esterni. Non so perché presero Diego dal Werder Brema. Mi chiamarono, quel giorno c'era la finale di Champions United-Barcellona a Roma, mi dissero che dovevo andare allo stadio per dare delle rassicurazioni. Io vado, entro da allenatore della Juventus, finisce la partita e mi dicono che dobbiamo discutere. Mi chiedono come volevo giocare e io ripeto 4-2-4. Al che mi chiedono dove avrei messo Diego. Dissi attaccante a fianco alla punta, ma loro dissero che lui voleva fare il 4-3-1-2. Pensa la mia follia, che fa capire come io sia una persona dritta che non scende a compromessi. Un altro avrebbe firmato, passando dal Bari alla Juventus... Lui mi dice questo, io forte delle mie convinzioni e idee, del fatto che non voglio promettere qualcosa che non darò mai dissi: "Se il giocatore deve decidere come dobbiamo giocare noi, non sono l'allenatore giusto per voi". Esco di lì e non ero più l'allenatore della Juventus…".

#2110. Inside Boreham Wood, the only unbeaten team in England's top seven tiers: Boss LUKE GARRARD explains how play-off heartache and a 'genius' owner are fuelling his swashbuckling side's promotion bid

Published: 21:00 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 17:17 EST, 13 November 2026

Of 252 clubs in the top seven tiers of English football, the last one unbeaten can be found in the pristine confines of Meadow Park, yards from the studios where the Indiana Jones adventures were filmed.

Boreham Wood are on their own crusade. And the Holy Grail is a long-coveted place in the EFL for the runaway leaders of the National League. They have 44 points from 16 games with 44 goals and are 12 points clear ahead of a trip to Yeovil on Saturday, finding success with a distinct passing style.

In a remarkable 4-3 comeback win against Hartlepool earlier this month, Luke Garrard's team made 1,022 passes, beating the Premier League record of 1,015 set by Manchester City against Swansea in April 2018.

The electric start to this campaign is, according to Garrard, fuelled by the lingering pain of defeat in the play-off final at Wembley in May when they conceded an equaliser in the seventh minute of stoppage time and lost on penalties.

'That broke me mentally for four or five days,' the Wood boss tells Daily Mail Sport. 'I reflected hugely and had a long, hard look at myself because I could not walk back for the first day of pre-season without the energy and heart for the job.

'I spoke to the owners and the staff about the first meeting I wanted to have when we came back in. It involved everyone at the club, and it was a message to tell all of them that as much as it hurt us, we needed to use the hurt as our fuel and our driving force.

'We got quite lucky getting some results on the board quite quickly and that builds momentum and belief.'

National League leaders Boreham Wood, under the guidance of their manager Luke Garrard, are the last unbeaten team in the top seven tiers of English football

Boreham Wood are a slick operation, skilfully run despite crowds seldom breaking 1,500. They share Meadow Park with Arsenal Women and Arsenal Under 21s and U18s

Boreham Wood are a slick operation, skilfully run despite crowds seldom breaking 1,500 by chairman Danny Hunter and his son Charlie, who is the sporting director and in charge of recruitment strategy. They are punching above their weight with a mid-table budget.

Hunter senior is a former props master in the film industry who worked at the nearby Elstree Studios among others, and whose father Mickey was a double promotion winning manager of club in the 1970s.

He took control in 1999 and has overseen investment of more than £14million and the rise from the depths of the Isthmian League to the brink of the EFL, forging effective relationships with local authorities and Premier League neighbours Arsenal, who use Meadow Park for their Women, Under-21 and Under-18 teams.

Arsenal ad boards surround an immaculate pitch, and work is underway at the venue on a £4m project to create a covered South Stand terrace incorporating improved 'Fan Zone' style facilities for away fans and a new first-team building.

Garrard's team train in the mornings on the artificial pitches beside the stadium, which are available for community use in the evenings and home in the afternoons to the club's academy.

Manchester City's Iliman Ndiaye and Hull's Sorba Thomas were both nurtured as young players at Boreham Wood. So was Abdul Abdulmalik, a 23-year-old winger signed after his release from Millwall in 2024 and sold to Djurgardens in Sweden for £1.5m in August.

The Abdulmalik fee shattered the non-League record set when Fleetwood Town sold Jamie Vardy to Leicester in 2012.

There was an untidy episode towards the end of the transfer window when Abdulmalik went public to plead for a move, but the Hunters secured an enviable price for their star and in another example of their good housekeeping have yet to reinvest the money in the team. It is still there as a war chest as they target promotion.

Abdul Abdulmalik, a 23-year-old winger signed after his release from Millwall in 2024, was sold to Djurgardens in Sweden for £1.5million in August

The club is run by chairman Danny Hunter (pictured) and his son Charlie, who is the sporting director. Hunter Snr is a former props master in the film industry

The windfall from Wood's famous FA Cup run in 2021-22 was invested into refitting the interiors of the ground including the dressing rooms and swish hospitality areas recognised among the best in non-League.

Meadow Park has a bar bedecked with souvenirs from those games and named in honour of Mark Ricketts, scorer of the goal to beat Bournemouth, who were at the time bound for promotion to the Premier League, and which clinched a date with Everton in the fifth round at Goodison Park.

'At no stage has the chairman given me any pressure to generate money from prize funds and cup runs,' says 41-year-old Garrard. 'It's only ever been about creating moments and memories.

'He (Hunter) is a Boreham Wood boy through and through and it's always been his greatest wish to lead the club into the EFL. He wants to leave a legacy. I adore him, I think he's a genius. We have a great working relationship. I do not take for granted the luxury of being manager here, especially after losing my job two years ago.'

Garrard has a long association with the club. First as a player, briefly in 2005 and then returning for a second spell four years later, a loan move from Wimbledon which became permanent and led him into his first coaching roles and the manager's job.

He took control in 2015 with the Wood in the top tier of non-League for the first time. Three times they reached the play-offs without promotion and there were many highs including Goodison before Garrard's tearful exit after relegation in April 2024, only for him to be back in charge by September.

During five months away, he turned down interest from one club because it took him too far from his family home and received two direct and sobering rejections from National League clubs. Feedback from the first told him he 'wasn't on the list and never would be' and from the second that he 'wasn't good enough' for them.

Perhaps it reveals some of football's inherent petty-mindedness towards Boreham Wood. They are perceived by some as upstarts with limited history and small crowds, putting the noses of 'bigger' clubs out of joint with the streamlined success of their business model and Hunter's backing, like a non-League version of Bournemouth or Brentford.

They have been known to charge scouts for tickets and charge away teams to broadcast, which some refuse to do on a point of principle. For a time, they had a reputation for pragmatic and unattractive football – but not any longer.

Garrard was rejected by two National League clubs and found himself back at Boreham Wood. 'The stars aligned,' as he puts it

Prolific striker Matt Rush has is fuelling Boreham Wood's promotion push

Garrard was probably entitled to have expected more interest in his services. Instead, he spent two days following the second of those rejections in earnest conversation with his wife about the need to look for work away from football when out of the blue came a call from Boreham Wood.

Within another two days he was back in his old job. 'The stars aligned,' as he puts it. 'It was the best team in the National South with the best players, the best facilities and the best budget. I had to go and deliver.'

And he did. With promotion back to the National League at the first attempt. Then the near-miss heartache of Wembley. Now this early-season surge thanks in part to the goals of striker Matt Rush. The Wood seem to have emerged stronger despite selling their most talented individual, which is a reassuring situation for any coach.

Unbeaten since April in the National League, they scored 25 goals in seven games in October and League One Wigan became the first team to beat them in any competition this season, in the FA Cup first round on Saturday.

It could yet prove a blessing in Boreham Wood's quest for the Holy Grail.

#2109. Leeds United, Nottingham Forest set sights on potential deal for Watford prospect

Watford are on the lookout for new players to bring into their ranks, but they’ve found some fresh prospects in the academy setup too. Amin Nabizada has made a name for himself this term, and among those hoping to follow in his footsteps is Jamael John-Baptiste.

However, the Hornets could have a fight on their hands in the bid to keep the teenage striker. Football Insider reports that Premier League pair Leeds United and Nottingham Forest are both interested in poaching John-Baptiste from the setup at Vicarage Road.

Watford hope to retain John-Baptiste, but his Premier League admirers are watching developments closely ahead of a potential swoop. He signed scholarship terms ahead of the 2025/26 season, and with no professional deal in place, it is believed that his contract runs out at the end of the current campaign.

Watford have given John-Baptiste a chance to prove himself after spells in the Spurs and Brentford academies, and he has delivered for the U18s. That could help them in their bid to keep him, as he may well be eager to pay back those who gave him a chance – much like Sydie Peck at Sheffield United.

However, Leeds United and Nottingham Forest are attractive clubs with strong academy setups. Not only have plenty of prospects come through their ranks and into the first-team squad over the years, but they will be able to offer elite facilities and a higher level of coaching than the Hornets.

If their interest does firm up, it could make keeping John-Baptiste a tough task. For now though, he will remain focused on delivering in the Watford academy to strengthen his reputation and continue his progression.

John-Baptiste is clearly a bright prospect, and one who Watford may well hope breaks into the first-team.

However, it has been a rarity for academy-reared prospects to break through at Vicarage Road. Nabizada has done so this term, but otherwise, the likes of Albert Eames and James Clarridge have only really been called upon to offer depth.

If the Hornets want to have a better chance of retaining talents like John-Baptiste for the foreseeable, they have to forge a clearer route to the first team. If not, then clubs of Leeds United and Nottingham Forest’s ilk will be able to poach youngsters.

Time will tell what comes of John-Baptiste’s situation though, as interest starts to emerge.

#2108. Andy's big night should have been so much more straightforward but that won't matter when he reflects on history made at Hampden

Published: 17:21 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:24 EST, 13 November 2026

If Andy Robertson’s first 101 caps for Scotland taught him anything, it’s that we have never been a nation to make life easy for ourselves. It’s in our DNA.

Why, therefore, would he have expected cap No102 to be any different?

This was meant to have been a celebration of one of Scotland’s greatest-ever servants on the night when he equalled Kenny Dalglish’s total appearances against the whipping boys of Nations League Group B1.

As the cliché goes, there’s no gimmes at this level. But that’s nonsense. This is exactly what Robertson’s special night should have been. A gimme.

The visitors hadn’t registered a goal yet in this campaign, never mind a point. They were rotten when Robertson and Co scored twice without reply in Skopje last month, and it was the same story for much of this forgettable contest.

The problem is that Scotland weren’t much better. In the first half, at least.

Andy Robertson joined Kenny Dalglish on 102 caps as Scotland saw off North Macedonia

Thankfully, they got the job done after the break to ensure it would be a night Robertson will look back on fondly after all.

He’s earned that too, even if his own individual performance didn’t quite hit the heights of so many that have gone before. But nobody will remember that.

Perhaps he knackered himself by covering what must have been around 10km in a small-sided kickabout during the warm-up. He may not have the same turn of pace in his arsenal that he possessed when he first broke into the international fold as a fresh-faced teenager. But, in terms of intensity, the 32-year-old is still an animal, in the nicest possible way. He hassled and harried every one of his team-mates throughout the pre-game stuff, barking out all sorts of instructions along the way.

In many ways, he is still the same standard-bearer he’s always been. It was a shame he couldn’t quite lead the way when the serious stuff got started.

Operating on the left-hand side of a four-man midfield, the Spurs man – as he does as frequently as ever – bombed up and down the flank all night. However, his deliveries from several promising positions weren’t quite up to scratch.

A couple hit the first man. A couple cleared the upright. None caused any sort of alarm in the North Macedonian backline.

Robertson was his customary energetic self as he bombed up and down the left-hand side

He was made to work harder than he would have liked at the other end too, as the visitors threatened to spoil the party on more than one occasion in the second 45.

In truth, he didn’t look particularly comfortable in Sebastien Pocognoli’s new-look side. Then again, few did.

The Belgian will be given plenty of time to really put his stamp on this Scotland team, but there’s no two ways about it. The early signs are uninspiring to say the least.

By the end, people got sick of the attritional style employed by Steve Clarke. Pocognoli arrived with a reputation for going with a more enterprising approach.

Really? It’s fair to say, the wait to see it in action goes on.

It’s not the time to panic. But you get the sense patience is already being stretched amongst some sections of the Tartan Army.

They weren’t shy in airing their frustrations last night. Scott McTominay was on the receiving end of a few grumbles, as was Ben Gannon-Doak who once again did so much right until the all-important crucial last bit.

Yes, he may have won the winning penalty which John McGinn duly slotted away, but let’s be honest, there was more than a touch of good fortune about it.

The Scotland skipper leads his side out at Hampden on a night he will remember forever

He should have burst the net when put clean through by Oli McBurnie long before keeper Stole Dimitrievski caught him near the byline.

The Bournemouth youngster is seen by many as undroppable, and there’s merit to that argument in that Pocognoli doesn’t have anyone else like him to call upon. However, he needs to improve the final part of his game to ensure he becomes Scotland’s main man in the years to come.

Had Scotland been a few goals to the good by the time McGinn stepped up to notch his second of the night, you suspect Robertson may have fancied taking the spot-kick himself.

Still, the record books will show that Robertson’s record-equalling night will ultimately go down as a triumph for all involved, even if it didn’t feel like it at the time.
